# Proofs: properties

| varint round trip: `decode(encode(v) ++ rest) = (v, len)` | `varint::encode`, `varint::decode` | `Varint.tla`: `EncodeOK` (`RoundTrip`), `u64` words: every value below 16500, `2^j - 1`, `2^j`, `2^j + 1` for `j` in 0..64, six rests; 110470 states | `Varint.decode_encode`; `Link.varint_roundtrip_boundary` (the same boundary values, kernel-evaluated) | `v < 2^63` | `TraceVarint.tla` (12391 calls); Lean replay of random values and encodings, both at the edges and uniform in bit width |
| one accepted encoding per value: an accepted prefix is exactly `encode(v)` | `varint::decode` | `Varint.tla`: `DecodeOK` (`Unique`, `Canonical`): an accepted prefix is `encode(v)`, and a string is accepted exactly when it has an encoding as a prefix; every string of 0 to 2 bytes, of 0 to 4 bytes over nine byte classes, and the runs of 5 to 10 continuation bytes with every terminator and trailing byte | `Varint.decode_canonical`, `Varint.unique_encoding`; `Link.varint_decode_boundary` | bytes are below 256 | `TraceVarint.tla`; Lean replay of random byte strings, including too long, truncated and non-minimal |
| encoded length is `encoded_len(v)` and at most 9 | `varint::encoded_len` | `Varint.tla`: `EncodeOK` (`EncodeLength`): the bytes written are `encoded_len(v)` and at most 9; same domain | `Varint.encode_length`; `Link.varint_roundtrip_boundary` | `v < 2^63` | `TraceVarint.tla` (bytes written); Lean replay (bytes written) |
| every decoded value is below `2^63` and at most 9 bytes were used | `varint::decode` | `Varint.tla`: `DecodeOK` (`DecodeBounds`), with the classification of every error (too long, truncated, non-minimal) apart from the code; same inputs | `Varint.decode_bounds`; `Link.varint_decode_boundary` | bytes are below 256 | `TraceVarint.tla`; Lean replay |

| insert fails iff the merged set needs more than `N` intervals, and then changes nothing | `VerifiedRanges::insert`, `CapacityError` | `VerifiedRanges.tla`: `FailIff` (counted independently as maximal runs of covered offsets), `FailureUnchanged`; same bounds | `Ranges.insertFixed_none_iff`, `Ranges.insertState_of_none` | none | the traces include capacity failures with `N` from 1 to 6 |
| `contains`, `contains_range`, `covered_len` agree with the coverage semantics | `VerifiedRanges::contains`, `contains_range`, `covered_len` (and the `Vec` set) | `VerifiedRanges.tla`: `Inv` (`Find` and `ContainsRange`, which run the `partition_point` loop, equal `Covers` and `CoversRange`, and `CoveredLen` equals the number of covered offsets); same bounds; `TraceVerifiedRanges.tla` compares the recorded answers | `Ranges.contains_iff`, `Ranges.containsRange_iff`, `Ranges.coveredLen_eq` | offsets are `u64`, so `covered_len` never saturates (proved) | TLC and Lean replay, with offsets at the top of the `u64` range in the Lean replay |
| `left_groups` is the largest power of two strictly below the node size; its `u64` code (checked subtraction, the shift by `ilog2`) never overflows and gives the model's value for every count up to `u64::MAX` | `left_groups` (crate private) | `Layout.tla`: `LeftOK` (`LeftGroups` is the code on `u64`: the checked subtraction, the shift by `ilog2`; every count below 300, `2^j - 1`, `2^j`, `2^j + 1`; `p < c <= 2p`); `RangeVerifier.tla`: `Left`, the specification | `Layout.leftGroups_pos`, `leftGroups_lt`, `le_two_leftGroups`, `leftGroups_max`; `Machine.leftGroupsM_eq`; `Link.left_groups_boundary` | `u64::ilog2` (see Assumptions) | `TraceEncoder.tla` runs `LeftGroups` of the code on the split of every node of 7397 step lists, 3511 `encode_range` results; the public `RangeEncoder` exposes the split of every node it visits: the complete step lists of 5863 layouts (from one group to `2^54` groups, around every power of two) equal `Encode.encRun`, which is built on `leftGroups` (Lean replay); the node walk of every verifier trace as before |
| the right child's pre-order index is `idx + left_groups`, the left child's `idx + 1`, a tree of `n` groups has `n - 1` parents, `preorder_index` returns a parent's position, and its `u64` code never overflows for any `lo`, `hi` and at most `2^54` groups | `preorder_index`, `RangeEncoder` (crate private) | `Layout.tla`: `PreorderNodeOK`, `PreorderOK` (the loop of `preorder_index` on `u64` against the position counted from the definition and against the pre-order list: every pair `(lo, hi)` of every tree of up to 24 groups, the paths of the trees of `2^j - 1`, `2^j`, `2^j + 1` groups for `j` up to 54); `Encoder.tla` `WalkOK`; `BuilderBytes.tla` `ArithOK`; `TraceRangeVerifier.tla`, `TraceBuilder.tla`: `Pos` | `Layout.right_child_index`, `Layout.left_child_index`, `Layout.pre_length`, `Layout.preorderIndex_correct`, `Builder.pre_nodup`; `Machine.preorderIndexM_eq`, `Machine.preorderOffset_lt`; `Link.preorder_boundary` | the node is a node of the tree (`Layout.Sub`) | the outboard offset of every recorded encoder step equals 64 times the node's position (TLC `Pos`, Lean replay of `preorderIndex`); `preorder_index` itself is checked through the slot the real builder writes each parent at, in 10484 builder events: TLC (`Pos`) and the Lean replay (`preorderIndex`) |
| `group_range` is the minimal covering group interval and stays within the groups | `Layout::group_range`, `Layout::groups` | `Layout.tla`: `GroupRangeOK` (exact arithmetic: `a * g <= s < (a + 1) * g`, `(b - 1) * g < e <= b * g`, `b <= groups`, the error exactly for a reversed range or one past the end); the group edges, the ends and the middle of the boundary lengths, every pair | `Layout.groupRange_spec`, `Layout.lt_groups_iff`; `Link.group_range_boundary` | none | `TraceLayout.tla` (7058 calls); Lean replay of random lengths, group sizes and byte ranges, and the runs of the verifier traces |
| `depth` is the height of the group tree, `outboard_size` is 64 bytes per parent, `group_size` is the bytes of the value in that group; none of them overflows `u64`, and a value of at most `2^64 - 1` bytes has at most `2^54` groups | `Layout::depth`, `Layout::outboard_size`, `Layout::group_size` | `Layout.tla`: `GroupsOK` (`(n - 1) * g < len <= n * g`, `n <= 2^54`), `DepthOK` (the least `d` with `n <= 2^d`), `OutboardOK` (`64 * (n - 1)`, a `u64`), `GroupSizeOK` (the remainder for the last group, 0 past the end, no overflow at index `u64::MAX`); every length 0..2100 for the logs 0 and 1, `k * g + d` for the logs 0..20, the boundary lengths for the logs 0..20; 74264 states | `Layout.depth_eq` (with `Layout.ht_eq`: the height of the recursive tree is `ceil(log2 groups)`), `Layout.outboardSize_eq`, `Layout.outboardSize_lt`, `Layout.groupSize_spec`, `Layout.groupSize_eq` (the saturating multiply is the plain one), `Layout.groups_le`; `Link.layout_boundary` | `u64::leading_zeros` (see Assumptions: `Layout.IsLz`) | `TraceLayout.tla`; Lean replay of 1500 random layouts, with lengths up to `u64::MAX` and every group log |
| every group the verifier releases is the genuine group at its offset, whatever the adversary feeds | `RangeVerifier::feed` | `RangeVerifier.tla`: `ReleasedGenuine`, `StackGenuine`; up to 7 groups, every range, a genuine or forged item at each step | `Tree.released_genuine` | collision resistance of the hash (`Tree.CollisionFree`: injective on parent pairs and on (offset, data)) and the genuine root | the decision of each recorded item equals the model's under the recorded hash values (`TraceRangeVerifier.tla`, Lean replay; `Tree.feed_congr`: the step depends on the hash only through the one call it makes); collision resistance itself is not testable |
| releases are strictly ascending, and at completion are exactly the groups that intersect the requested bytes | `RangeVerifier::feed`, `RangeVerifier::is_complete`, `Layout::group_range` | `RangeVerifier.tla`: `Ascending`, `InRange`, `Bookkeeping`, `CompleteReleasedAll`; same bounds | `Tree.released_ascending`, `Tree.complete_releases_range`, `Tree.verifier_correct` | none for the ordering; collision resistance and the genuine root for the genuine bytes | `Ascending`, `InRange` and `Bookkeeping` are invariants of the trace model, checked on every recorded state |
| a rejected item releases nothing, a failed verifier stays failed, a group is released only after its hash matched | `RangeVerifier::feed` | `RangeVerifier.tla`: `FailureSticky`, `RejectReleasesNothing`; same bounds | `Tree.feed_failed`, `Tree.feed_reject_releases_nothing`, `Tree.feed_release_verified` | none | the tampered runs: the rejected item, the repeated error of every later call, and `TooMuchData` after completion |
| completeness: the stream `encode_range` produces for a genuine value and outboard is accepted, with no false rejection; the verifier completes and releases exactly the groups intersecting the range, with their genuine bytes; the encoder reads inside the value and the outboard, and each step has the size the verifier expects | `RangeEncoder`, `encode_range`, `RangeVerifier::feed` | `VerifierBytes.tla` `Inv` (on the genuine stream every split completes with exactly the requested groups released, in order, and no item rejected); `Encoder.tla` `WalkOK` (the encoder's steps are the walk the verifier expects) | `Encode.complete_accepts` (with `Encode.lockstep`, `Encode.encStep_size`) | none: it holds for any hash | `TraceEncoder.tla` (the steps of every recorded range); the genuine runs: every step names the model's node, and the verifier accepts every item |
| the stack never overflows: the verifier stack and the encoder stack hold at most 55 nodes for any value of at most `2^64 - 1` bytes (any group log), so the 64-entry `STACK_CAP` is never reached and `TreeError::StackOverflow` is unreachable; the builder stack holds at most 54 entries after each group, plus one before a merge | `Stack<_, 64>` in `RangeVerifier`, `RangeEncoder`, `OutboardBuilder` | `Encoder.tla`: `WalkOK` (at most 55 nodes); `BuilderBytes.tla`: `Shape` (at most 55 entries) and no push onto a full stack; `VerifierBytes.tla`: `Inv` (at most 55 nodes, no overflow) | `Bounds.verifier_stack_le`, `Bounds.verifier_stack_le_len`, `Bounds.encoder_stack_le`, `Builder.mainPhase_stack_le` | none | the stack bound on every recorded verifier state |
| range verifier at the byte level: one `feed` call is the byte-by-byte reference over exactly the bytes it consumed, on the zero-copy fast path and on the buffered path alike, and never fails (no overflow, no failed narrowing, no `OutputTooSmall`, no wrong `TooMuchData`), on a 32-bit and on a 64-bit `usize` | `RangeVerifier::feed` (buffer, `filled`, `next_item`) | `VerifierBytes.tla`: `Plan`, `Feed`, `Complete` on `u64` words and a 32-bit or 64-bit `usize`; `Inv` (lockstep with the item-by-item reference, the item completed is the stream segment named, the next item starts at the bytes consumed minus those buffered, `TooMuchData` only on a complete stream, no failed narrowing, no buffer overflow); 14451 states | `VerifyBytes.feedCode_sim`, `VerifyBytes.needW_eq`, `VerifyBytes.complete_arith` | none | `TraceRangeVerifier.tla` takes `Plan` and `NextLen` from `VerifierBytes.tla` (4729 events); `TraceRangeVerifier.tla` (offsets advance by the bytes consumed, an item starts at `pos - filled`) and the Lean replay of `feedCode` on the real offsets, 4729 events |
| feeding the stream in any split into pieces gives the same decisions, released groups with their bytes and final state as the reference run over the whole stream, whatever the pieces (single bytes, one piece, any sizes, empty pieces), the caller feeding the unconsumed tail of each piece and stopping at the first error; a stream cut at item boundaries gives exactly the item-by-item run `Tree.run` | `RangeVerifier::feed` | `VerifierBytes.tla`: a state machine that feeds pieces (the caller feeding the unconsumed tail, stopping at the first error): every size of piece for groups of 2 bytes, the pieces that end around each item for groups of 4 bytes, 1 KiB, 2 KiB and 1 GiB; every requested range, the genuine stream and each item tampered with, extra bytes after the end; `Inv` holds in every state reached | `VerifyBytes.split_invariance`, `VerifyBytes.stream_equiv` (with `drivePiece_eq`, `driveAll_eq`, `bytes_items`, `itemwise_core`) | none; that a released group borrows the input on the fast path and the buffer otherwise is not a value property and is checked by the trace only (`ptr`) | the windows of every size of the verifier traces: whole stream, single bytes, item-sized, random, tiny |
| value verifier, equivalence: `GroupVerifier` (hasher fed as the pieces arrive, a 64-byte pair buffer, no group buffer) accepts and rejects exactly what `RangeVerifier` does, with the same bytes consumed on every call, the group reported at the call where `RangeVerifier` releases it (same offset, same length, and the bytes it hashed are the bytes `RangeVerifier` returns), the same error, `next_len`, `is_complete` and stack, for every split of one stream into pieces, valid and corrupted streams, on a 32-bit and a 64-bit `usize` | `GroupVerifier::feed`, `feed_pair`, `complete_pair`, `feed_group`, `next_item`, `next_len`, `at_boundary`, `is_complete`, `failure`, `new` | `ValuesVerify.tla` (with `ValuesVerifyOps.tla`): the two models fed the same pieces; `Inv` (`Agrees`: the same result kind, bytes consumed, group, stack, failure, bytes buffered `pair_n + group_n`, observables, and the bytes hashed are the item's) and `VerifierBytes.tla`'s own `Inv`; tiny (585 states), small (groups of 2 and 4 bytes, pairs of 2 and 3 bytes, every range and tampered item, every split: 7776 states), 1 KiB groups on a 64-bit (979) and a 32-bit (979) `usize` | `ValuesVerify.feedG_sim` (one call), `ValuesVerify.drivePieceG_eq`, `driveAllG_eq`, `group_equiv_range`, `group_split_invariance` (every split; with `VerifyBytes`), `ValuesVerify.Rel` | the BLAKE3 hasher is incremental (the state after some `update`s is the hash input of the concatenation: the symbolic `Hash.leaf` is applied to the bytes it took), collision resistance for soundness only | `TraceValuesVerify.tla` and the Lean replay of `feedG` on the real offsets next to `feedCode`, 4904 events (`tests/trace_values_verify.rs`: both real verifiers fed the same pieces, every observable compared) |
| value verifier, soundness: a group is reported only after its bytes hashed to the chaining value committed by already verified parents, so a reported group is a true part of the value, whatever the adversary sends and however the stream is split; a rejected item passes nothing, the failure is sticky (every later call returns the same error and changes nothing), an empty input is a no-op, a complete stream with more data is `TooMuchData`; the groups passed are ascending, inside the request, exactly the request when complete | `GroupVerifier::feed` | `ValuesVerify.tla`: `Inv` (every group passed is genuine, a failure only from a tampered item, `TooMuchData` only on a complete stream), `FailureSticky`, `RejectPassesNothing`; the genuine stream is never rejected and completes with exactly the requested groups in order | `ValuesVerify.released_genuine`, `pending_genuine`, `feedG_release_verified`, `feedG_reject`, `feedG_sticky`, `feedG_empty`, `feedG_toomuch`, `released_ascending`, `released_in_range`, `complete_releases_range` (over every reachable state, `Reach`) | collision resistance of the hash and the genuine root | the same traces: every error repeated, every rejected item, `TooMuchData` after completion |
| value verifier, `skip_group`: passes over the next group without hashing it, only at the start of a group item (refused with `TooMuchData`, the verifier unchanged, when failed, mid-item, at a pair or complete); for a group whose bytes the caller holds verified it leaves the verifier exactly as feeding the whole group would, with the same group reported | `GroupVerifier::skip_group` | `ValuesVerify.tla` with `SkipOn`: `skip_group` may be called anywhere between pieces; `RefusedSkip`, and the accepted skip equals `RangeVerifier` fed the genuine group (`Agrees`); tiny and groups of 2 bytes (1058 and 1209 states) | `ValuesVerify.skipG_ok`, `skipG_err`, `skipG_eq_feedG`, `reach_good` (skip in the reachable states) | the group the caller skips has the committed hash (the store holds only bytes verified against the same root) | `TraceValuesVerify.tla` and the replay: accepted and refused skips next to `RangeVerifier` fed the genuine group |
| value verifier, the 64-node stack never overflows (at most 55 pending nodes), the arithmetic of the code (`u64` and `usize`) never fails | `GroupVerifier::push`, `complete_pair`, `feed_group` | `ValuesVerify.tla`: `Inv` (at most 55 nodes, no failed narrowing, no overflow in the `U64` operators) | `ValuesVerify.reach_stack_le`, `hbnd_feed`, `completePairG_eq`, `finishGroupG_eq`, `feedGroupG_nf`, `feedPairG_nf` | none | the stack bound on every recorded state |
| the receive state keeps the verifier at an item boundary: a frame starts only with the verifier at the start of an item, the proof phase never leaves a half-fed group, `enter_data` accepts only a verifier waiting for exactly this frame's group at its start (a proof that does not end on a pair boundary is refused), `skip_group` never fails after those checks, the data phase finds the verifier taking all the bytes given, whatever the provider announces for the proof length; WHOLE_GROUP: a group that is hashed is fed only once all of its bytes are stored, so it takes at most two calls (one per receive chunk), never one per packet | `Rx::header`, `proof`, `enter_data`, `data`, `pump` (`Pump::Group`), `Fetch::drain` | `ValuesVerifyRx.tla`: the frames of the genuine stream, every proof length one byte too long or too short, every stored flag and piece size; `Inv`, `WholeGroup` (5159 states); witness `ValuesVerifyRx_nowhole` (the guard off, as before: a group fed per packet fails `WholeGroup`) | `ValuesVerifyRx.hdrDone_inv`, `proofStep_inv`, `dataStep_inv`, `enterData_inv`, `enterData_total`, `dataStep_whole` | the cut of a group into receive chunks is the session's, taken as given (at most one cut in a 16 KiB group) | none (the receive state is covered by the fetch scenario tests); `fetch::rx::tests` counts the hash calls of a fetch fed packet by packet |
| `OutboardBuilder` per piece: for any split of the value into pieces (and empty pieces) every `update` succeeds, the state after the last piece is the group-level state after the last group, and `finish` returns the root and has written the parents of `Builder.build` | `OutboardBuilder::update`, `finish` (`in_group`, `consumed`, the hasher offset, the split of the input at group boundaries) | `BuilderBytes.tla`: `StateDet` (the state after any pieces equals the group-level reference), `FinishOK`, `WritesGenuine`; every split for group sizes 1, 2 and 4 bytes, the pieces that end around each group edge for 1 KiB, 2 KiB and 1 GiB, on a 32-bit and a 64-bit `usize`; 1626 states. `TraceBuilder.tla` feeds the recorded calls to the same `UpdateCall` and `FinishCall` | `BuilderBytes.builder_split_invariance`, `BuilderBytes.builder_split_root` (the root is the genuine one, with `Builder.builder_correct`), `BuilderBytes.updLoop_inv`, `BuilderBytes.updStep_inv` | hashing a group in several `update` calls gives the hash of the concatenation (BLAKE3, see Assumptions) | `TraceBuilder.tla` and the Lean replay of `updateCode`, `finishCode`: 10484 events from 170 random values, group sizes and splits |
| `OutboardBuilder` errors: `OutboardSize` exactly when the buffer has the wrong size (checked first), `TooMuchData` exactly when the piece is longer than the bytes still due (and then nothing is consumed or written), `Incomplete` exactly when fewer bytes than declared were fed; a value of 0 bytes has 0 groups, an outboard of 0 bytes, writes nothing and returns the hash of the empty input | `OutboardBuilder::update`, `finish`, `new` | `BuilderBytes.tla`: `ErrorsOK` (`outsize`, `toomuch`, nothing consumed or written; an empty piece changes nothing), `FinishOK` (`incomplete` until the last byte; the empty value has no write and the empty hash as root); same domain | `BuilderBytes.update_errors`, `BuilderBytes.finish_errors`, `BuilderBytes.builder_empty` | none | the error probes between the real calls (2075 wrong-size buffers in `update` and `finish`, 1152 pieces too long, 717 early finishes) and the empty values; the empty root equals `blake3::hash(b"")` in the test, and the verifier's `RootMismatch` check uses the same constant |
| machine integers in the layout: `groups`, `depth`, `outboard_size`, `group_size`, `group_range`, `left_groups` and `preorder_index` as the code writes them on `u64` (checked, saturating and shifted operations) do not overflow, underflow or divide by zero for any length up to `u64::MAX` and any supported log, and return the model's values; `Layout::for_group_bytes` accepts exactly `1024 << log` for `log <= 20`, so the buffer holds a group | `Layout::groups`, `depth`, `outboard_size`, `group_size`, `group_range`, `for_group_bytes`, `left_groups`, `preorder_index` | `Layout.tla`: every operator of the layout on `U64.tla` words (a panic stops TLC), `NewOK`, `ForGroupBytesOK` (accepted exactly for `1024 << log`, `log <= 20`), and the properties of the rows above; same domain | `Machine.groupsM_eq`, `depthM_eq`, `outboardSizeM_eq`, `groupSizeM_eq`, `groupRangeM_eq`, `leftGroupsM_eq`, `preorderIndexM_eq`, `forGroupBytesM_iff`; `Link.for_group_bytes_boundary` | `u64::leading_zeros`, `u64::ilog2`, `usize::trailing_zeros` (see Assumptions) | `TraceLayout.tla`; `lake exe replay`; Lean replay of 1500 size cases and the layouts of the encoder traces |
| machine integers in the encoder: `RangeEncoder::step` never overflows or underflows on a node it visits and gives the model's step, for a value of up to `u64::MAX` bytes; `encode_range` returns `OutputTooSmall` exactly when the output buffer is smaller than the stream and the stream's length otherwise, with every `u64` to `usize` conversion succeeding (nothing truncates) on a 32-bit and on a 64-bit `usize`, and never `RangeOutOfBounds` | `RangeEncoder`, `encode_range`, `slice_at` | `Encoder.tla`: `Run` (the iterator on words with the stack of 64 slots), `EncodeRange` (`slice_at`, the conversions to `usize`, `at.checked_add`), `EncodeOK` (`OutputTooSmall` exactly when the buffer is smaller than the stream, never `RangeOutOfBounds`, on 32 and 64 bits); 21337 states | `MachineEnc.encStepM_eq`, `MachineEnc.encRunM_eq`, `MachineEnc.copyLoop_eq`, `MachineEnc.encodeRange_exact` | `u64::ilog2` | `TraceEncoder.tla` (7397 step lists, 3511 `encode_range` results); 800 `encode_range` results with buffers of the exact size, one byte short, empty, larger and random (Lean replay against `encodeRangeM`, on a 64-bit `usize`; the 32-bit case is proved, not run) |
| `encoded_len` adds the step sizes with checked `u64` addition: it returns `EncodedTooLong` exactly when the stream would be longer than `u64::MAX` bytes (for example `encoded_len(Layout::new(u64::MAX, 20)?, &(0..u64::MAX))`, whose stream is `u64::MAX + 64 * (2^34 - 1)` bytes), and the exact length otherwise | `encoded_len` | `Encoder.tla`: `FoldOK` (the checked fold fails exactly when the exact sum reaches `2^64`; every list of up to 3 addends from the boundary addends), `WalkOK` (the stream of the whole value is `len + 64 * (groups - 1)` for every layout the walk can run), `TooLongOK` (the verdict for every boundary length and log, and `EncodedTooLong` for `u64::MAX` bytes with 1 GiB groups); the verdict for the layouts whose stream has more than `2^35` steps is derived from the closed form and the fold lemma, not run | `MachineEnc.foldAdd_eq`, `MachineEnc.encodedLen_overflows_iff` (the checked fold is `none` iff the sum reaches `2^64`); `Link.fold_boundary` | none | `TraceEncoder.tla` (the `encoded_len` of every recorded range); the overflow input needs `2^35` steps, too slow for the gate; the proof covers it |
| machine integers in the builder: every iteration of the merge loop (`2 * rs`, `done - 2 * rs`) and of the finish loop (`ls + rs`, `n - size`) is free of overflow and underflow for a tree of up to `2^54` groups, and the slot of a parent (`preorder_index * 64`, narrowed to `usize`, the range checked against the buffer) is found for every parent, so `OutboardSize` is raised only for a buffer of the wrong size | `OutboardBuilder::complete_group`, `finish`, `parent` | `BuilderBytes.tla`: `ArithOK`, `ArithFinishOK` (the merge of the stack for the group that completes the `d`-th group of a tree of `n >= d` groups, for `d` and `n` up to `2^54` from the boundary set, and the right spine of `finish`: no overflow, the slot of each parent is the position from the definition), and every state of the split runs | `BuilderBytes.builder_arith` (with `mergeTopM_eq`, `finishTopM_eq`, `merge_safe`, `finish_safe`), `BuilderBytes.slotM_eq`; `Link.slot_boundary` | none | the slots of 10484 builder events |
| the outboard builder, group by group: it writes the parents in pre-order, every parent of the tree once (`n - 1` writes), each at the slot `preorder_index` gives, with the pair of the genuine chaining values of its children, and the root it returns is the genuine root; one group has no parent and the group hash is the root | `OutboardBuilder::update`, `finish` (and `build_outboard`) | `BuilderBytes.tla`: `FinishOK` (the root is the genuine one, the writes are exactly the genuine pairs at their slots, one per parent), `WritesGenuine`; the group-level reference of `StateDet` | `Builder.builder_correct`, `Builder.builder_outboard` (against any genuine outboard), `Builder.builder_correct_one` | the abstract hash is what the BLAKE3 calls compute (as in `Tree`) | `TraceBuilder.tla` (10484 events); as in the next row |
| transcript encoding: every field the handshake binds (version byte, ALPN, both connection ids, the 6-byte credit record, the AEAD offer byte, public values, prekey hash, identities with their credit record, signatures, tickets) is absorbed as its own length-prefixed field, so a list determines each argument separately; an Initial list is never a Hello list, a full-handshake list never a resumption list, and the hash after each stage determines everything absorbed up to it | `Transcript::absorb`, `initial_transcript`, `hello_transcript`, `absorb_reply`, `absorb_credit`, `absorb_identity`, `absorb_resume_reply`, `confirmation` | `Transcript.tla`: `InjectiveSmall`, `InjectiveImage`, `InitialRecovered`, `HelloRecovered`, `FullRecovered`, `ResumeRecovered`, `InitialNeHello`, `FullNeResume`, `LabelSeparates`, `IdCredInjective`, `TicketCtxInjective`; | anti-amplification: for every sequence of receives, validations and sends in which each send obeyed `can_send` (the definition of the reachable set), `tx <= 3 * rx` before validation and the margin `tx + 48 <= 3 * rx` holds after every send; `can_send` is the exact comparison (the 128-bit arithmetic cannot overflow); counters saturate, never decrease, validation is sticky | `AmpBudget` | `Amp.tla` (operators in `AmpOps.tla`): `AmplificationBound`, `Margin`, `CanSendExact`, `CanSendMeaning`, `SatAddExact`, `CountersMonotone`, `ValidSticky`; counters saturate at 100 (scaled), 17085 distinct states | `Amp.amplification_bound`, `reach_margin`, `canSend_iff`, `u128_no_overflow`, `onSend_margin`, `canSend_validated`, `validated_sticky` | the counters stop counting after `2^64` bytes | `TraceSecurity.tla` (real `u64` words) and the Lean replay: 4800 operations from 40 runs, including saturation near `u64::MAX`; the saturation runs send without asking `can_send`, so they check the operations, not the discipline |

| ticket stores: at most `N` tickets (`N = 0` stores nothing); server ticket ids are unique by the code whatever the generator returns (eight colliding draws fail with `TicketId` and store nothing); eviction takes an empty slot, then an expired one, then the oldest; | connectivity mailbox (`pinc-connect`): the bytes held for a member never exceed the quota, headers included, and the byte count is the sum of its messages; at most `N` members; every message is counted (stored = delivered + dropped oldest + expired + held); messages leave in arrival order and a sequence number is handed out once; overflow drops the oldest messages first; age expires from the front and a delivered message is younger than the maximum age; a packet too long for a datagram stays held; a refused hand-over removes no message (a look takes out only expired messages, the removal follows the hand-over, and a message whose hand-over was refused is still held in front; the relay's release pass is one ordered walk of the member's queue that offers each message that fits, takes it while the taker accepts, stops offering at the first refusal and steps over a message too long for the route, which `DrainFit` models as an action and `ConnectMailbox_drain` checks, with the relay configs checking the same rule through `StepsOver` and `MailboxOk`); the wake request fires on the first held message and again only after 30 s | `Mailbox::{push, look, remove, pop_fitting, look_fitting, take, sweep, clear}`, `Relay` hold, `release_held` and `deliver_held` | `ConnectMailbox.tla` over `ConnectMailboxOps.tla`: `Bounds`, `Conserved`, `OrderOnce`, `AgeBound`, `DropOldest`, `RefusedKept` (witness config `ConnectMailbox_wold`: the old rule, removed and then refused, violates it), `WakeFirst`, `WakeSpaced`; `ConnectMailbox_drain` adds the release pass (`DrainFit`) and checks `DrainOffered` (the fitting messages offered in arrival order up to the one refused, none after it), `DrainKept` (what stays is the rest in arrival order, none lost), `DrainBooks` (count and byte total are those of what stays), `DrainFresh` (nothing expired stays) and `DrainSteps` (the same mailbox as `LookFit` and `RemoveSeq` in steps); witnesses `ConnectMailbox_worder` (offers out of order) violates `DrainOffered` at the 4th state, `_wlose` (a kept message lost) `DrainKept` at the 3rd, `_wbytes` (the headers of what was taken left in the byte total) `DrainBooks` at the 3rd; for the release pass `drainWalk_kept`, `drain_kept` (what stays is a sublist of the held messages, in order), `drainWalk_offered`, `drain_offered` (what is offered is a sublist, in arrival order), `drainWalk_fits`, `drainWalk_stopped` (after a refusal nothing is offered), `drainWalk_perm`, `drain_partition` (what is taken and what stays are together the unexpired messages), `drain_books` (the byte total and the count are those of what stays) | 1 member slot and 2 nodes, 7 bytes of quota, 2-byte header, bodies of 0 to 2 bytes, 3 pushes, age 3 and re-arm 3; | connectivity batch builder (`pinc-connect`, `carrier/frame.rs`): a batch holds at most 64 frames and 16384 bytes of frames, in the order they were offered; a BATCH frame and a long-header packet are always refused (they neither join a batch nor start one that takes others); a frame is added exactly when it encodes, fits the buffer after the header room and stays within both limits; a refusal or an error changes nothing; a frame that does not fit the buffer is refused once the batch holds a frame, and is the wire error of the first frame otherwise; an empty batch finishes as nothing, one frame as the frame itself, more as a BATCH frame whose header fits the room kept for it and which `Frame::decode` reads back as exactly the members in order; resuming from `BatchState` continues the same batch | `BatchBuilder::{offer, finish, state, resume}`, `BatchState` | `ConnectPack.tla` over `ConnectPackOps.tla` (`Inv`, `OfferOk`, `FinishOk`, with the real decoder of `ConnectCodecOps.tla` reading the finished record); | connectivity bounded tables (`pinc-connect`): `Table` behaves as a bounded map for every hash (insert replaces or adds and answers full only for a new key at capacity, remove returns the value and keeps every other key reachable across the wrap-around backward shift, retain calls the predicate on every entry at least once, the length is the number of occupied slots, a probe visits at most `N` slots); `Cache` is direct mapped: a put replaces exactly the occupant of the key's bucket and no other slot, a get hits iff the bucket holds the key | `Table::{insert, remove, retain, find}`, `Cache::{put, get}`, `bucket_of` | `ConnectTable.tla` over `ConnectTableOps.tla`: `SizeOk`, `Unique`, `Reachable`, `Refines`, `CacheRule`, the retain visit check in `DoRetain` | `ConnectTable.cache_put_get`, `cache_put_evicts`, `cache_put_other`, `cache_get_iff`, `find_probes`, `insert_len`, `remove_len` (the probe invariant and the map refinement of insert, remove and retain are model checked only) | table capacities 3 and 4 with 4 keys and every bucket function, values 1 and 2; cache capacity 3 with 5 keys; the Lean theorems hold for every capacity and bucket function | `trace_connect_table` (`tins`, `trem`, `tret`, `tslots`, `kput`, `kget`: 22390 events on capacities 1 to 8 with the real hash buckets), `TraceConnectTable.tla`, same replay |
| connectivity relay (`pinc-connect`): delivery only between authenticated peer relays (a frame from any other node is refused and changes nothing); a packet that came from a peer relay is never forwarded again; the reply route holds for exactly 30 s, is learnt only for a node without a carrier here and never names this relay; the byte quota per window is never exceeded and a refused frame is not counted; sessions are closed by the sweep at the time limit, every client carrier is written a PING every 600 s, a newer carrier replaces the older and calls about the older are refused; a datagram is tunnelled only down a UDP session heard less than 20 s ago, for a member the host reports a validated direct route for, and only if `35 + len <= 1452 - 28`; a held message the tunnel refuses stays held, the pass stops there and is counted once, and the messages behind it are not tried; a message held for a member is released when a packet from the member is heard and its route is ready (nothing is emitted while the session is stale or the host reports the route not ready, and a member that holds nothing costs one probe), and the pass hands over every held message that fits the route, oldest first, and steps over a message too long for the tunnel (that is no refusal and is not counted); a packet for a member that is not asleep here, has no carrier and no ready route here and is homed at another relay is forwarded whatever this relay holds for it (routing starts no release); an uploaded packet needs a validated member; the second copy goes out only after 15 s of carrier silence; a sleep notice is accepted only within 10 s of the relay's clock, for a known node, from its own carrier, and a close within 10 s after it is a suspension | `Relay::{carrier_opened, carrier_closed, carrier_frame, peer_frame, datagram, member_returned, udp_heard, release_held, handle_timeout, add_peer}` and the routing of `route.rs` | `ConnectRelay.tla` over `ConnectRelayOps.tla`: `CallsOk` (no re-forward, relay frames only to peers, tunnel freshness and size, reply route), `QuotaOk`, `SessionLimit`, `SessionsBound` (and in `CallsOk`: a refused hand-over ends the pass and the member still holds messages), `Forwarded` (a packet for a member that is not asleep, has no carrier and no ready route here, homed at another relay, is forwarded to its home), `HeardDelivers` (an accepted heard packet leaves a ready member holding nothing that fits the route), `StepsOver` (after an accepted heard pass exactly the live messages too long for the route remain, in order) and `MailboxOk` (the books balance per member), `SpuSilent` (an uploaded packet from a member whose UDP stamp finds the table full is no refusal and traces nothing; `ConnectRelay_full` starts with the UDP table full of other nodes and checks all the invariants, witness `ConnectRelay_wfull` puts the traced refusal back and violates `SpuSilent` at the 2nd state); the five non-witness configs check them all; that the refused message is kept and delivered later is `RefusedKept` in the mailbox model, `trace_connect` and the hub test); `ConnectRelay_sleep` adds a sleep notice to the frames, so a packet for a sleeping member is held in the mailbox and the mailbox quota of `SessionsBound` is exercised (the base config never holds anything) | `ConnectRelay.peer_unauth`, `forward_peer`, `no_reforward`, `back_window`, `back_expired`, `fwdback_local`, `fwdback_learnt`, `charge_ok`, `charge_drop`, `charge_inv`, `charge_reset`, `tunnel_spec`, `tunnel_ready_spec`, `spu_member`, `heard_idle`, `heard_unready_quiet`, `forwarded`, `stale_copy`, `sleep_spec`, `close_asleep`, `close_asleep_of` | 2 session slots, 2 clients, 2 peers, calls up to depth 6 at the instants 0, 16 and 31 s (the rule constants are the real ones; the counts of the first five equal the first round's; the witnesses stop at the states generated and distinct of the run: `wfull` 43 / 5 (the 2nd state, one call), `wheard` 642,737 / 56,110 (the 6th state, one worker), `wfwd` 9,987,524 / 560,073 (the 7th), `wstop` 5,311,140 / 395,011 (the 8th, 8 workers), `refused` 27,840,775 / 557,956 (the 8th); | connectivity record store (`pinc-connect`, the relay's rendezvous): a record is stored only if its signature verifies and its key belongs to its node id (the check is an ideal primitive, a predicate of the bytes: a record that fails it is refused and changes nothing), and a node publishes only its own record; the store keeps at most `R` records and at most one per node id, the one with the highest sequence number accepted since the node had none stored; a lower number, or an equal one with different bytes, is refused and changes nothing (equal bytes only renew the receive time, a higher number replaces); a record not renewed within 5400 s (three republish periods of 1800 s) is dropped by the first `handle_timeout` at least 1 s after the previous sweep, and is never answered from the first instant it is expired, swept or not; a lookup answers only with a stored, unexpired, verified record (too large when over 1389 bytes) and changes only its counter; a published or own record goes once to every peer relay in slot order, a replica, a restored record and a refused one to none; `set_own_record` arms the republish 1800 s ahead and a republish renews the own record, sends it once to each peer and not before its time; `sync_record` sends only a record homed at this relay to an authenticated peer; the same-second subtleties of the code are in the model: an equal-bytes record renews the receive time (a restore may move it back), and an expired record not yet swept still refuses a lower number | `RecordStore::{accept, sweep}`, `Relay::{store, publish, replica, restore_record, set_own_record, republish_due, sync_record, lookup, answer, handle_timeout}` | `ConnectRecords.tla` over `ConnectRecordsOps.tla`: `Bounded`, `Keyed`, `OnlyGood`, `Highest`, `Lower`, `EqualDiff`, `EqualSame`, `Higher`, `BadRefused`, `WrongOwner`, `Replicated`, `LookupFresh`, `SweepRule`, `OwnArmed`; | connectivity carrier link (`pinc-connect`): the retry wait is 1 s doubling to 2 s; no deadline is in the past or overflows; a down link dials only when something is queued or it is the home carrier, and waits otherwise; | connectivity protected carrier (`pinc-connect`): the byte stream is read as units of a 2-byte big-endian length and that many bytes; after any sequence of reads and polls, in any split of the stream, the carrier is the unit-wise reader after some number of units with that reader's answers, and two hosts that took the same bytes and decided all they could agree on the state and on every answer; a poll decides the next unit exactly when the bytes it needs are in (the two length bytes for a length of 0 or over the stage's limit, which ends the carrier with `UnitSize` before the body arrives; the whole unit otherwise) and no byte past them changes the decision, so a unit cut by the end of the stream is never handed over; the input buffer never holds more than `IN_LEN` bytes and a live carrier whose next poll is idle has room, so the host is never stuck; each direction seals at packet numbers 0, 1, 2, ... (the k-th record carries k, a seal is never refused as not increasing) and no (key, nonce) pair is used twice; a sealed unit is the length `n + 16`, the ciphertext and the tag; a seal answers `Closed` on an ended carrier and `NotOpen` before the handshake completes, before anything else is looked at; bytes that are empty or longer than a frame are refused as a frame (`Frame`) and the carrier lives on; a seal refused for its stage, size, room or encoding changes nothing, one at the key's limit ends the carrier; a record is opened at `recv_pn` with its length bytes as associated data: an authentic one raises `recv_pn` and is delivered, or dropped with `Frame` (the carrier lives on) when its plaintext is not a frame, and a dropped, replayed, reordered, reflected or altered record ends the carrier with `Authentication`; no frame comes before the handshake opened the carrier; a call that ends the carrier delivers, opens and seals nothing and leaves both packet numbers; an ended carrier answers every poll, key answer and seal with `Closed` and delivers, opens and seals nothing; a key answer while no key request is outstanding answers `NoKeyRequest` and changes nothing (an open carrier stays open), while a wrong kind of answer to an outstanding request still fails the handshake and ends the carrier; an error is fatal (`CarrierError::is_fatal`) exactly when the call ended the carrier or found it ended; `free` is what `receive` would take, the unit the last poll took counted as room | `carrier/session.rs`: `ProtectedCarrier::{dial, accept, receive, poll, key_response, answer_soft, transmit, seal, seal_bytes, sealable, free, pending_transmit}`, `CarrierError::is_fatal` | `ConnectCarrier.tla` over `ConnectCarrierOps.tla` (which calls `SealOp` and `OpenOp` of `Record.tla`): `Bounds`, `Aligned`, `InOrder`, `NoFrameBeforeOpen`, `PnCount`, `PnUsed`, `NoNonceReuse`, `NoStall`, `EarlyRefusal`, `EndChangesNothing`, `EndedStays`, `InOrderAccepted`, `LimitEnds`, `EndedAnswersClosed`, `FatalIffEnded`, `NoRequestNoChange`, `SealStageFirst`, `BytesRefusedLive`, `FreeIsReceive` (the last six of the next call of every kind, seals of bytes of every length around the limits included), over every interleaving of reads (one byte, or all there is), polls, key answers, transmits and seals of both ends against a stream an adversary builds; 4 configs (`ConnectCarrier`: the handshake over the stream then a record, 753499 states; `_open`: two records each way, 1642411 states; `_limits`: a ChaCha20-Poly1305 key at its failed-open limit; `_pn`: the packet-number limit) and two witnesses TLC must violate (`_reach`: a record is delivered after a handshake over the stream; `_w_max`: a reader that kept the handshake limit for records refuses a record it must accept) | `ConnectCarrierThm`: `poll_eq`, `poll_idle_iff`, `poll_bad_len`, `poll_extend`, `poll_ended`, `receive_spec`, `receive_bound`, `idle_has_room`, `run_ref` (any interleaving of reads and polls is the unit-wise reader), `split_invariance`; `ConnectCarrierKeys`: `carrier_no_nonce_reuse` (`Record.no_nonce_reuse` applied to the send key of every reachable carrier), `carrier_pns`, `sealIn_never_not_increasing`, `sealIn_ok`, `sealIn_refused`, `sealIn_limit`, `sealFrame_refused`, `sealBytes_refused`, `procRec_ok`, `procRec_auth`, `poll_frame_open`, `ends_change_nothing`, `ended_stays`, `keyResponse_no_request`, `seal_not_open`, `sealBytes_size`, `receive_takes_free` | that an error is fatal exactly when the carrier ended (`FatalIffEnded`) is checked by TLC on the reachable states, not proved in Lean (a poll of an open carrier with no receive key would end it with `NotOpen`, which is not fatal; no reachable carrier has one); the handshake is a step that yields keys (its outputs are an input; it is proved in `Handshake*`); the AEAD is perfect (a record opens exactly under the key, packet number and length bytes it was sealed with) and the two directions have different keys (the key schedule); whether a plaintext is a frame is the frame codec's (proved in the codec row); `Frame::encode` writes at most `MAX_FRAME_LEN` bytes; the host offers the stream in order; the any-split theorem covers hosts that read and poll (key answers and seals are not stream events); | streams, early opens of an optimistic dial: a dial reported connected before its handshake lets the application open streams and queue data before the peer's limits are known (`Connection::allow_early_streams`, `Streams::set_early`); while early opens are on an open skips the limit on what we may open (the slot and pool tests stay), and only then: an open passes the limit only while they are on, so the opener never has more streams open than the limit or than it had opened while early; every stream we open keeps its ordinal, how many we had opened in all, itself included, and its data and its control frames (a reset, a stop request, a window update) wait until the limit covers that ordinal: no frame that names a stream we opened leaves while its ordinal is past the limit (a stream frame leaves only for a stream whose ordinal the limit covers, any other send changes nothing, and `pending_control` gives a stream's control frame only for such a stream), so the peer never receives a frame for a stream its limit does not cover and never closes the connection with a stream limit error for one (before #432 a reset or a stop request on such a stream left at once); once a stream may send it may for good (the limit only rises and an ordinal never changes), so the streams let go are the first ones opened, in order, and a control frame held for the limit leaves once the limit covers its stream (a stream reset or dropped before the limit ever covers it still sends its reset then: the peer opens every lower stream when a later one arrives, so a stream that never said it ended would hold a place at the peer for the connection's life); | pacing: the pacer holds one to ten tokens, lets exactly the tokens out before a pause, and after a burst waits ten gaps (`gap = mtu srtt 4 / (5 cwnd)`), or one tick when the window does not limit the sender and the ten packets already took ten gaps; the release never moves back; a sender the window limits at every packet never sends faster than one packet per gap past a burst of ten; for any sender the first packets of two bursts in a row are ten gaps apart, so the rate over bursts is the paced one; idle time adds no token | `recovery/pacer.rs` | `Cc.tla`: `PacerShape`, `PacerRate` (a limited sender: packets i and i + 10 at least ten gaps apart), `BurstStarts`, `NextMono`; `Cc_pacer.cfg` and `Cc_pacer_limited.cfg` run the pacer alone over three bursts with waits long enough for a burst to be slow; `Cc_pacer_strict.cfg` is a witness: an unlimited slow sender breaks the packet-by-packet rate | `TransportCcThm.lean`: `pacer_tokens`, `pacer_burst`, `pacer_pause`, `pacer_next_mono`, `pacer_pause_limited`, `pacer_span_from_start`, `pacer_start`, `pacer_release_ge`, `runSends_no_pause` | the gap is computed exactly in TLC by long multiplication (`MulDiv`) within a window of 2^27 bytes | `TraceCc`: the pacer events of the same runs |

| handshake fragments: the runs of a message are sorted, disjoint, non-adjacent and inside the message, at most 8, the message at most the largest allowed; a fragment that is kept adds exactly its bytes (cut at the end of the message) to what the runs cover, one that finds no free run changes no run, a fragment of a message of another length starts from nothing; a message is handed over only when every byte is covered, and then the reassembly is fresh | `conn/reasm.rs` (`Reassembly::add`, taken out of `Handshake::on_fragment`) | `Reasm.tla` with `ReasmOps.tla`: `Shape`, `Exact`, `NoFalseComplete`, `Total` | `TransportReasmThm.lean`: `mergeRuns_spec`, `insertRun_wf`, `insertRun_cov`, `add_inv`, `add_cover`, `add_complete`; `TransportReasmComplete.lean`: `add_hands_over` (the converse: when what is held and the arriving fragment cover every byte the message is handed over, for at least one run), `add_inc` (a reassembly under way always lacks a byte) | the bytes themselves are not in the model: the Rust test checks the buffer against the fragments it sent | `TraceReasm.tla` and the Lean replay: 80 seeded runs of a real reassembly, 24271 events with fragments in order, reversed, shuffled, repeated, overlapping, cut off, of another length in between, and sparse runs that fill the table; model-based tests (`MbtReasm.tla`, `tests/mbt_reasm.rs`): a real reassembly driven along every edge of TLC's state graph, 93636 edges in 267353 calls, each compared with the model's result and state and the buffer with the bytes sent |
| the buffer of packets that arrive before their keys: a connection holds at most 8 of them, oldest first, only while it is handshaking (nothing is held once it is up or closed), and a 0-RTT packet only on a server; a packet is held exactly when it is a 0-RTT packet on a server in its handshake that has no early keys yet or a 1-RTT packet in a handshake before the 1-RTT keys exist, and the buffer has a place and the pool has room, and then it goes last; a Handshake packet is never held; when the buffer is full (or the pool has no room) the packet that arrives is the one lost, counted as dropped, and the packets held stay: an attacker who addresses the connection by its ids and fills the buffer with junk first makes every early packet that follows, real ones too, be refused until the session is established (the handshake messages go on, and the sender sends the refused data again); a datagram counts toward the bytes received (the amplification budget) on arrival, whether it is held, refused or opened, and trying the held packets later counts nothing; establishing the session installs the 1-RTT keys and then tries the packets held once each, oldest first, leaving none (a close in the middle ends it, and what is left goes with the close); a packet that does not open is lost, counted as undecryptable if it is a 1-RTT one and not counted if it is a 0-RTT one (a 0-RTT packet held ahead of a Hello that brings no early keys is lost the same way); a 1-RTT packet that opens ends the use of the early keys, so a 0-RTT packet held behind it fails; a 0-RTT packet that opens and carries ACK, CLOSE, REKEY, NEW_TICKETS or PATH_RESPONSE (a frame only an established session sends) is dropped whole before any of its frames is applied, on arrival or in the drain, counted as refused (`early_refused`), and changes nothing else: the connection stays open, and the sender sends its data again; no other packet is refused so (the frames a client sends in 0-RTT, stream data, datagrams, credit, PING and PADDING, are applied as before, and a 1-RTT packet is applied whatever its frames); nothing held survives the close | `conn/recv.rs` (`on_packet`, `on_short`, `on_zero_rtt`, `hold_early`, `handle_inner`, `scan`, `process_payload`), `conn/drive.rs` (`on_hs_event`, `drain_early`), `conn/life.rs` (`begin_close`, `release_buffers`), `stream/datagram.rs` (`EarlyData`) | `Early.tla` with `EarlyOps.tla`: `Bound`, `Order`, `Phase`, `Kinds`, `HoldCount`, `HoldRight`, `FullRefuses`, `Refused`, `Counted`, `Amp`, `Drain`, `DrainOnce`, `Closed`, `EarlyRefused` (705590 states with a bound of 2 and five packets, 1672200 with a bound of 3); `eligible_iff`, `recv_held`, `recv_full`, `recv_held_eligible`, `recv_nu`, `recv_amp`, `recv_closed`; `drainQ_spec`, `established_ids`, `established_all`, `established_once`, `established_count`, `established_nu`; the refusal: `direct_refused`, `recv_refused`, `established_nr` | what the frames of a packet that opens do (it is a `fate`: opens, does not, closes the connection, or carries a frame only an established session sends; which frames those are is the frame scan's, tested one by one in `transport_conn`) and which keys open it; the room of the pool is an input (the real buffer is driven to refuse for want of room, the connections never are); the held packets of the connection runs are junk, so a held packet that opens is checked by TLC and the proofs only (the real 0-RTT packets of the resumed servers arrive after their Hello and are opened at once); a held packet whose connection id stops being routable before the drain is not modelled | `TraceEarly.tla` and the Lean replay: 30 seeded runs of the real buffer (2730 events: pushes of 16 bytes to 16385 into pools of 1 to 8 chunks, pops and clears, against a naive list; an empty buffer holds no chunk), 14 real connections, 7 servers and 7 clients (185 events: 0-RTT and 1-RTT junk before the session, more than the buffer holds, then the establishment, more junk, a close and more junk, each packet checked against the naive rule and the counters it moves), and 6 resumed servers (18 events: the Hello of a client with a ticket alone, which raises the early keys and establishes the session, then the client's first 0-RTT packet alone, sealed by the real client: 5 refused, one with each of ACK, CLOSE, REKEY, NEW_TICKETS and PATH_RESPONSE, and one with the frames a client sends there, applied) |
| packet headers: a long header written and parsed back gives the same type, ids and body length and the exact header length, whatever follows the body; the parser looks at the two top bits, the type bits and, on a handshake, the low four bits (on a 0-RTT packet they are under header protection and any value parses to the same header); an accepted header is the one encoding of the fields it yields (up to those four bits), its ids are at most 20 bytes and its body fits; a cut header is `Truncated` with the bytes missing, a body past the end of the input is `Length`, another version is `Version` with the byte, an id length above 20 is `ConnIdLength`, a first byte without both top bits is `Form`, type bits 2 and 3 are `LongType`, a low bit on a handshake is `Reserved`; a refused write, long or short, writes nothing, for every error: the packet-number length that does not fit the type (`PnLength`), a body length of `2^63` or more (`VarintOverflow`, checked before the room), the room (`BufferTooSmall` with the shortfall); | packets of a datagram: the iterator yields long packets, then at most one short packet or one error, and nothing after either; the byte ranges start at 0, each starts where the one before ends, none is empty, all lie inside the datagram and a short packet runs to its end; each packet is what the parser finds at its offset, with the length its header declares; after the last long packet only zero bytes remain (padding), and an error is the parser's refusal at the offset reached; the split of a long packet the parser accepted never fails. | frame codecs (every frame type: PADDING, PING, ACK, RESET_STREAM, STOP_SENDING, STREAM `0x08`-`0x0f`, MAX_STREAM_DATA, MAX_STREAMS, PATH_CHALLENGE and PATH_RESPONSE, CLOSE, DATAGRAM `0x30` and `0x31`, MTU_PROBE_ECHO, SLEEP, WAKE, REKEY, READY, NEW_TICKETS, extension frames from `0x40`): decoding the encoding of a frame yields the frame and takes exactly `encoded_len` bytes, whatever follows, with three frames that take what follows them: PADDING is the whole run of zero bytes, and a STREAM or DATAGRAM frame without a length runs to the end of the packet (a REKEY request comes back with a zero echo and switch number); an accepted input is the one encoding `write` gives for the frame it yields, with one leniency of the parser: a STREAM frame at offset 0 is also accepted with the OFF bit set and the offset written as 0, which `write` never produces; | held first-flight fragments (the endpoint's table of handshake fragments that arrive ahead of the datagram that starts their message): the table never grows past its 4 entries whatever arrives, and no datagram in it is longer than 1500 bytes (a longer one is refused and changes nothing); a datagram of at most 1500 bytes is always held, in a free entry or one whose datagram is older than 1 s if there is one, and else in place of the oldest arrival, which is counted as evicted; a datagram is given out only for the message it was held for (the same destination id, message length and source address), only while it is at most 1 s old (the bound included), the oldest arrival of that message first, and at most once over any run of calls; a datagram older than 1 s is gone after the first `hold` that is not refused, `take` or timer call (`expire`), and never given out; what the table holds is what `hold` put there, unchanged; `held = replayed + expired + evicted + waiting` after every call | `crates/pinc-core/src/orphan.rs` (`Orphans::hold`, `take`, `expire`) | `Orphan.tla` with `OrphanOps.tla`: `Bounded`, `Balance`, `Orders`, `Fresh`, `GivenRight`, `GivenOnce`, `HoldRight` (59835 states, a clock that steps back included); | packet builder (what a packet carries): a handshake packet carries one fragment of a handshake message (its length and offset, then the bytes) and no frames, and a 0-RTT and a 1-RTT packet carry the same frames: the builder fills both with `fill_frames`, and the receiver hands the payload of both to `process_payload`, which takes every frame the decoder accepts, except that it drops a whole 0-RTT packet that carries ACK, CLOSE, REKEY, NEW_TICKETS or PATH_RESPONSE (see the row of the buffer of early packets); only a client seals a 0-RTT packet; the frames of a fill come in the order of `fill_frames`: ACK, SLEEP, WAKE, the MTU echo, READY, tickets, REKEY, extension frames, the stream layer's credit and resets, PATH_RESPONSE, PING, PATH_CHALLENGE, stream data (in the order the stream layer gives it), datagrams, then the credit a stream gives back when its answer has just left and the PING of a forced packet; the probe timeout's packet is ACK, WAKE and PING, a path MTU probe ACK and PING, a tail repair ACK and stream frames, and the CLOSE packet CLOSE alone; `build_app` reads the pending tail repair only for a packet it builds as a repair (on the active path, with no path MTU probe due and no probe timeout's packet owed), so a repair due together with a path MTU probe or the probe timeout's PING stays pending and goes with the next packet (before #293 the probe packet took it and carried none of it, so it was lost); the repair leaves the pending state exactly when the packet built for it is sealed, or when `fill_repair` drops it because nothing a packet can carry is left of it (its packet is no longer in the sent ring, each of its frames is acknowledged, the first does not fit the repair's room of `min(datagram size - 23, 600)` bytes, or its bytes cannot be read): a build that ends without a packet (a buffer with no room for a packet or for the repair's first frame, no destination id, a sent ring with no slot, a seal that fails) leaves it pending, and it leaves with the next packet that is sealed (before #433 `build_app` took it as the build started, so such a build lost it and the tail waited for the probe timeout); a fill writes every frame with its length and never PADDING or CLOSE (`put_stream_header` writes the header `write` gives); the place it keeps for an implicit length is its last frame, a STREAM frame whose data runs to the end of the fill (a datagram or a credit frame written after it forgets it, and the forced PING never follows it), so `implicit_stream_len` never refuses it and the `?` after it in `build_packet` never gives up a packet whose frames were already taken from their owners; `implicit_stream_len` drops the length only of a STREAM frame that ends the payload, and refuses, changing nothing, when anything follows it; the payload sealed is the fill padded to the least payload, or the fill with that length dropped, never both, and `FrameIter` reads it, with no error, as the frames written in order, the last without its length when it was dropped, then one PADDING frame for the padding | `conn/send.rs`: `build_app`, `build_packet` (the seal takes the repair), `fill_frames` and its slots (`fill_control`, `fill_extensions`, `fill_stream_control`, `fill_path`, `fill_streams`, `fill_datagrams`), `put_stream_header`, `implicit_stream_len`, the tail of `build_packet`; `conn/send/repair.rs`: `fill_repair`; `conn/send/close.rs`: `build_close`; for the kinds, `conn/recv.rs` (`on_packet`, `on_handshake`, `on_zero_rtt`, `on_short`, `process_payload`) | `FramePack.tla` over `FramePackOps.tla` (the codecs of `FrameOps.tla`): `HintRight`, `RoundTrip`, `LastOnly`, `Order`, `Sizes`, `RepairKept`, and for the end of the build `RepairLeaves`, `RepairPending`, `RepairCarriedWithRoom` (`FramePack.cfg`: every ordinary fill of 3 ACKs, 3 sets of control frames, 2 of path frames, 4 of stream chunks, 3 of datagrams, credit given back or not, the ring, window, force and ACK switches and 2 rooms with a least payload of 2 bytes or the room, 41472 cases; `FramePack_modes.cfg`: the probe timeout, MTU probes and tail repairs, each as `build_app` picks it from whether the probe timeout owes packets, a path MTU probe is due, a repair is pending and the packet goes on the active path, a tail repair also with each way its build can end besides the fill (none, a buffer with no room for a packet, no destination id, a sent ring with no slot, a seal that fails) and its packet in the sent ring or not, 11520 cases); | the body of a Handshake packet, `pad_to` and the header protection sample: a Handshake packet is the long header whose body length counts the three parts, then `varint(message length) varint(offset) fragment`, written on one writer over the window; it is written exactly when both numbers are below `2^63` and the window holds the header, both varints and the fragment, and what it wrote is that encoding; with the room the code computes (the window less the fixed part, two bytes for the body length and both varints) a fragment always fits as long as the body length takes two varint bytes (below `2^14`; the largest message is 6529 bytes), so `write_handshake` returns 0 only for a window under the fixed part; the datagram parses back to the same header and the body reads back as the message length, the offset and the fragment, whatever follows, and that fragment is what `Reassembly::add` is given; a body that parses is the one encoding of its parts, a body that does not (first or second varint cut, too long, not minimal) leaves the reassembly alone; a message length of 0 or above the largest message is refused and changes nothing, an offset at or past the length is refused (a message of another length has by then started the count over), a fragment that runs past the length is cut at it and kept, not refused (there is no length field in the body, so a fragment cannot run past the body); `pad_to` appends exactly the zero bytes that bring the writer to `min_len` (in chunks of 64), leaves what was written, leaves a writer that already holds `min_len` alone, and when `min_len` does not fit is refused with the shortfall (`BufferTooSmall`) before anything is written; `sample_at` is the 16 bytes at `pn_off + 4` and is `TooShort` when fewer than 16 follow (also for an offset whose sum overflows); header protection changes only the first byte and a packet number of 1 to 4 bytes, so the sample of a protected packet is the sample of the unmasked one (4 is the longest packet number), and `seal` refuses a packet for being too short exactly when no sample can be taken from the packet it would make | `conn/send.rs`: `write_handshake`; `conn/handshake.rs`: `on_fragment`; `conn/reasm.rs`: `Reassembly::add`; `packet/datagram.rs`: `pad_to`; `packet/protect.rs`: `sample_at`, `unprotect_header`, `seal` | `HdrBody.tla` with `HdrOps.tla` (`ReasmOps.tla` for the reassembly, `HeaderProt.tla` for the mask): `WriteOK`, `WriteRead`, `RoomOK`, `Canon`, `RecvOK`, `PadOK`, `SampleExact`, `SampleStable`, `SealSample`; ids of 0, 1 and 20 bytes, numbers at 0, 1, 5, 63, 64, 127, 128 and the varint boundaries up to `2^63 + 1`, fragments of 0, 1, 3 and 130 bytes, windows around the header and the datagram, every string of 0 to 6 bytes as a body, four reassembly states, every window of 0 to 140 bytes and every minimum length for `pad_to`, every packet-number length and offset for the sample; 33795 states. | the endpoint's connection-id table (`Table`, a hash table of `CAP` entries with a chain per bucket and a list in insertion order; the endpoint's demux table, which takes duplicates through `insert_dup`, and its other keyed tables): it never holds more than `CAP` entries, whatever the bucket function (collisions included: it is a parameter, and the proofs hold for all of them); every key held is found by `get`, `contains`, `index_of` and `matches` whatever other keys share its chain and whatever was removed or evicted before; `get`, `touch`, a replacing `insert` and `remove` act on the holder of the key that was pushed last (the head of its chain), not on one a `touch` moved to the end of the order list; `matches::<M>` gives the first `M` holders of the key in the order of their pushes, newest first, and how many (at most `M`: with more than `M` holders it returns `M` and cannot say how many more), so the holders pushed before those are not returned, and a `touch` does not change that; a removal (`remove`, `remove_pair`: the newest holding that value) takes exactly one entry, the order list loses that entry and no other and every other entry is still found; a key has more than one entry only through `insert_dup` (`insert`, `insert_room`, `remove`, `remove_pair` and `touch` keep every key at one entry or none when it starts so, and `insert_dup` puts the new holder in front of the old ones); `insert` of a held key sets the value of the newest push and moves nothing, of a new key appends it as the newest and, only when the table holds `CAP` entries, evicts exactly the oldest entry (the head of the order list, which a `touch` moves) and returns it; `insert_dup`, and `insert_room` of a new key, on a table of `CAP` entries answer false and change nothing, the links included; an entry keeps its array position (`at_index`, `index_of`) until it is removed; | deadline heap (the endpoint's indexed binary min-heap of `(deadline, id)` pairs for the ids below `N`, which `poll_timeout` and the timer loop read): the position index and the heap are inverse on the ids present (`heap[pos[id]] == id`, `pos[heap[i]] == i`), so an id is in the heap at most once; a call changes exactly the entry it names (every other id keeps its presence, its deadline and the deadline stored for it; the id named has the deadline given or none; the size changes by one only when an id comes or goes) and an id outside `0..N` changes nothing; the heap never holds more than `N` items, and at `N` every id is held, so a deadline given then moves its id in place; removing the root over and over (a peek and a `set` with none, a pop) gives the pairs held in strictly increasing order, each once, and no other, after any run of calls | `crates/pinc-core/src/heap.rs` (`DeadlineHeap::set`, `peek`, `deadline`, `up`, `down`) | `Heap.tla` with `HeapOps.tla`: `Typed`, `Bounded`, `Inverse`, `Once`, `Order`, `MinRoot`, `DrainOK`, `SetExact` (129034 states: 5 ids, deadlines 0 to 2, every sequence of calls including an id outside the heap, the heap full and drained); | event queue (the endpoint's bounded ring of events waiting for the host, `4 * K + 1024` places for a table of `K` sessions): the ring never holds more events than its places, whatever is pushed; events leave in the order they entered (what was popped, then what is queued, is a subsequence of what was pushed, over any run of pushes, pops and purges); when the full ring holds none it gives up its oldest event, counted as lost, so an event the application cannot recover is evicted only if the queue holds nothing recoverable; a pop gives the oldest; a purge of a session keeps the other events in their order and drops the events that name the session but its `Closed`; every drop is counted: pushes = pops + events a purge took + `dropped` + queued after every call, and unrecoverable pushes = unrecoverable pops + unrecoverable events a purge took + unrecoverable events queued + `lost`; the recoverable count the stall test reads is the number of recoverable events queued; the back-pressure rule: while the queue is not stalled (the unrecoverable events queued leave at least `2 * K + 64` places, `event_headroom`), a step that pushes at most that many unrecoverable events, and any number of recoverable ones, does not change `lost`, so the endpoint, which starts nothing and refuses the packets that would raise events while the queue is stalled, never evicts an event the application cannot recover; | keepalive schedule (`pinc-core` `keepalive.rs`: when a session's active path and its standby paths are pinged, and when the plan is computed again): a ping is never due before `now` nor before 5 s after the last send, and a review moment is after `now`; without a standby window and a watched direct path the active path is not pinged before its idle interval; after a resume nothing is due before the quiet period ends unless packets are in flight, and then it is no part of the plan; computing the plan again at a later `now` below the review moment gives the same review moment and the same ping (so `review_at` is the first moment the answer can change); the idle intervals leave 5 s below the 75 s idle timeout, dialer before acceptor, and a quiet session planned in time has its ping due before the peer's idle timeout can fire; | half-open cap (the endpoint's admission of a first flight for an id no session holds, or a different one for an id that fewer than four sessions hold, and the life of a session whose peer is not authenticated yet): a datagram makes a session only when the event queue is not stalled, its first packet is a long Handshake packet that starts a message (offset 0) of type 1 (a full Initial), 2 (a short Initial) or 6 (a resumption Hello), it is at least 1200 bytes for a full Initial, 464 for a short one and 93 for a Hello (the length of the shortest one there is; a first packet under its floor is dropped before the admission and counted in `first_too_short`), the responder does not only reject it, and fewer than 256 sessions are counted, unless it resumes; it resumes exactly when it is a Hello whole in its first packet that names a registered protocol and a ticket this node's store still holds (so unused), unexpired, for that protocol and AEAD, whose binder verifies; a protocol must be registered and a slot free. A short Initial and a Hello are screened before the admission (`screen_first`, the checks the responder makes on its first message, without taking a ticket): one that is not whole in its first packet, does not parse, names a version or protocol this node does not serve or an AEAD it does not share, or is a Hello whose ticket opens but whose binder does not verify, is dropped and counted in `dropped_in`; a short Initial of a prekey this node does not hold, and a Hello whose ticket this node does not hold, holds past its expiry or holds for another protocol or AEAD, is answered with the reject the responder would have sent, built by the endpoint without a session and charged to the ledger for its address like any answer to an address not yet heard from (sent only when the ledger allows it: `stateless_rejects`, `stateless_refused`), and takes no half-open slot, so a trickle of such packets cannot fill the cap and a genuine first flight is never refused for the sessions they made. A first flight for an id that sessions hold goes to that admission only when each of them refused it as a first flight other than its own and the id has fewer than four holders (else it is dropped and counted in `holders_refused`), and gets a session of its own beside them. No arrival frees a session: the first holder of an id stays until its life, its own handshake limit or a close ends it, and no first flight makes a fifth holder of an id (a session that lists an equal id for itself is not counted as a holder in these models). A session made by a resumption is not counted, any other is; both are unauthenticated. The count never passes 256 and is the number of counted sessions after every call, whatever arrives; a first flight that makes no session changes no slot, and counts one dropped datagram (one stalled datagram while the queue is stalled); what it leaves behind is the endpoint's counters (`datagrams_in`, `dropped_in` or `stalled_in`), the ledger entry of its source (every datagram is counted there before it is routed) and, when the connection's construction fails after the admission (it does not read the datagram), a slot retired for good. A session leaves the count exactly when it is established or its slot is freed (its connection ended, a close finished, which for a session still in its handshake is at once, or its life ended); a closing session counts until it is freed. An unauthenticated session lives the node's connect limit, 30 s (60 s on a thin node, as long as its handshake may last), and at least 60 s once it answered as a responder (its flight went out, a reject is not one: the responder hold, pinc#387), whatever the node is, so never above 60 s; it is there at `created + life` and gone one tick past it (`handshake_end`, the one function the transport's own handshake deadline and the endpoint's sweep both ask); it holds a deadline at most its own life after its creation (the models and their traces cover a node that is not thin), so `poll_timeout` is never later than the end of any of them (and is none while the event queue is stalled); a timer call at or after that end, `created + life + 1`, frees it, unless the event queue stalls during the call | `crates/pinc-core/src/rx.rs` (`unrouted`, `offer`, `screen`, `accept`, `MAX_HALF_OPEN`, `MAX_CANDIDATES`, `HALF_OPEN_LIFE`, `HALF_OPEN_HOLD`), `slot.rs` (`half_open_life`, `half_open_end`), `stateless.rs` (`reject_stateless`, `send_reject`), `pinc-security` `responder.rs` (`screen_first`), `pinc-transport` `conn/handshake.rs` (`handshake_end`), `events.rs` (`on_established`), `endpoint.rs` (`free_slot`), `timers.rs` (`settle_core`, `poll_timeout`, `handle_timeout`, `run_timer`) | `HalfOpen.tla` with `HalfOpenOps.tla`: `Capped`, `Exact`, `Shape`, `DeadlineBound` (per slot: its own life, `LIFE` or `HOLD`, never above `HOLD`), `PollBound`, `RefusedRight`, `LeftRight`, `TimerRight`, `ResumeRight`, `HolderKept`, `HeldBound` (`HalfOpen_held`: 148498803 states generated, 141986 distinct, with three slots, a cap of 2, one id and every length; | accept room (pinc#436: the endpoint starts no session the host's accept queue has no place for, instead of the facade closing finished sessions as busy): every server session a first flight makes is owed to the host from its creation until the host reads its `Incoming` (`session_taken`) or the session ends, established or not; a session that ended before its `Incoming` was read gets no place (`Taken::Gone`); a sub-session (no first flight, `Taken::Unreserved`) waits only in a place no owed session holds, and is closed as busy otherwise; | small round trips of the integration layer. Relay path addresses: `relay_parts (relay_addr (v6, r, p)) = (r, p)` in the same family for every relay slot `r < 256` and peer `p < 65536`; an address `relay_parts` accepts is rebuilt by `relay_addr` as the same IP (and `is_relay_addr` is true of exactly those), one it refuses is none `relay_addr` builds; the port is not preserved: `relay_addr` gives port 0 and `relay_parts` ignores the port it is given, so a relay path address at another port is still a relay path and round trips only up to the port. The stream tag (one length byte 1 to 64, then the bytes): `read_tag (write_tag t ++ rest) = t` with `rest` left unread (trailing bytes are not refused, they are the next thing on the stream); `write_tag` refuses exactly the tags of 0 and over 64 bytes (`Config`, nothing written); an accepted tag is exactly the length byte and that many bytes, written back identically (one encoding); a length byte outside 1 to 64 is `Config` with only that byte read, the rest of the stream untouched; a stream that ends inside the tag is the stream's own error. The stream id of a range-proof body: `head_sid` reads the one canonical varint at the head; `with_sid` writes the varint of the new id and copies `body[used..]` unchanged, refuses a head that is no varint, an id of `2^63` or more and a result over `EXT_MAX = 1216`, and replacing an id by itself changes nothing | `relay_net.rs`: `relay_addr`, `relay_parts`, `is_relay_addr`; `_tag64`: the real limit of 64 on boundary strings), `SidRT`, `SidOK` (`_sid`: ids at the edge of every varint width, 2^63 and `u64::MAX`, bodies with rests that fill a 12-byte buffer exactly and over, bodies that are no varint); witnesses `_w_nobound` (a segment over the byte range taken as a relay slot: `RelayInv` fails), `_w_tagzero` (length byte 0 accepted), `_w_readpast` (one byte too many read), `_w_wlong` (a tag of 65 written), `_w_sidskip` (the rest copied one byte late), `_w_nocap` (no check against the buffer): each fails its property; IntegrationCodecs_tag64 checks the tag boundary only; EXTMAX is read by the session-id invariants of IntegrationCodecs_sid | `RelayNet.lean`: `relayParts_relayAddr`, `relayAddr_relayParts`, `relayParts_none`, `relayAddr_family`, `relayAddrSock_port`, `relayPartsSock_port`, `relayAddrSock_relayPartsSock`, `isRelayAddr_iff`; `StreamTag.lean`: `writeTag_isSome`, `readTag_writeTag`, `readTag_ok`, `readTag_consumed`, `readTag_unique`, `writeTag_injective`, `readTag_config`, `readTag_eof`, `readTag_bound`; `ValuesFrames.lean` (on `Varint`): `headSid_enc`, `headSid_canonical`, `withSid_enc`, `withSid_ok`, `withSid_head`, `withSid_self`, `withSid_none_iff`; the TLC model takes a chunk of at least the minimum target (`MinChunk`: `MinT` = 4 groups as in the code, 1 in the small configurations so that a value of a few groups is striped, which the real minimum would give whole to the first provider) and a rate victim keeps at least one group; the queue arithmetic is `ValuesSched`, the timing `ValuesTime`; in Lean the in-order frame is `acceptHdr`, then `acceptEnd` (the group is still not stored: it is written) or `acceptDup` (another stream stored it: only accounted for, no write, no event), `midrace_enabled`, `acceptEnd_writes`, `topUp_enabled`; the 64-span capacity widening is not modelled; a wrong range proof, out of band or against a held group, evicts its provider (`BadProofEvicts` in `_loss`; reverting it violates the invariant); a frame for a group the store already holds is skipped without hashing;
