| charged store: one byte budget for bundles and mailbox items; bytes charged never exceed the budget; live custody is never evicted; an admission sweeps the expired items first, so an expired but unswept key is new, refuses an item already expired with `Expired`, and succeeds exactly when the key is new and custody leaves room; a refusal changes nothing but the expired items; evictions are expired first, then transit oldest admission first, only as many as needed; a reclass needs no room | `Ledger::{admit, expire, release, reclass}` | `DtnStore.tla` (`Inv`, per-step asserts, `FitsSpec`); `DtnStore_cross.cfg` finds a mailbox admission evicting a bundle item (the space is shared) | `DtnStore*.lean`: `admit_inv`, `admit_budget`, `admit_keeps_live_custody`, `admit_ok_iff`, `admit_refused_state`, `evictLoop_spec`, `admit_eviction_order`, `admit_eviction_minimal`, `reclass_ok_iff`, `used_by_space`, `mailbox_evicts_bundle_transit` | `next_seq` needs 2^64 admissions; the Lean model counts in `Nat` | `trace_dtn_store`: 9760 events in TLA+ and Lean, 7380 more near `u64::MAX` in Lean |
| custody: never dropped before expiry (a live custody item leaves only by delivery, a release of its key, or an ack of its bundle); relief only by a valid ack from exactly the node custody was offered to and for the offer outstanding (each offer gets a fresh id from the book's counter, the signed ack names it, a stale ack or one for an offer a hand-back cancelled is `NotOffered`); a refused accept changes only the expired items; capacity exactly when no record is free; a held transit item is promoted without new room | `CustodyBook::{accept, offer, on_ack, delivered, expire}` | `DtnCustody.tla` (`Linked`, `Distinct`, per-step asserts); `DtnCustody_deep.cfg` | `DtnCustody*.lean`: `reach_inv`, `custody_exit`, `custody_to_transit_only_by_relief`, `relief_iff`, `onAck_ok_iff`, `accept_err_state`, `accept_capacity_iff`, `delivered_exits`, `bexpire_exits`, `onAck_ok_iff` (node and offer id) | the ack signature check is the boolean `valid` (signature scheme assumed); the host never admits or releases a key the book holds; `accept` returns the incoming previous node and offer id, and `on_ack` of an item the ledger no longer holds is `Store(Unknown)`: both pinned and stated in the docs | `trace_dtn_custody`: 14033 events, real Ed25519 acks including forged and stale ones, with their offer ids |
| forwarder copies: held + handed = received per bundle, a repeated hold adds nothing, handed copies are never taken back, binary spray hands `copies/2` and keeps at least 1, delivery sends carry 0 copies and the destination is served unless the transfer table is full, one transfer per (bundle, peer) | `Forwarder::{step, poll}` | `DtnForward.tla` (`Balance`, `A_Kept`, `A_PollActs`, `Served`), `DtnForwardNet.tla` (`Account`, `HeldLeBudget`) | `DtnForward*.lean`: `balance`, `spray_total`, `hold_total`, `hold_refused_total`, `spray_send_spec`, `poll_serves_ready`, `dest_served`, `reach_one_transfer` | none beyond the model | `trace_dtn_forward`: 5342 events in TLA+ and Lean, 12260 more near `u64::MAX` in Lean |
| forwarder timers: a timer is charged elapsed time only while its link is up, expiry is wall-clock and releases first, a transfer gives up only with the timer out, the link up and the sends used (at most `max(1, max_sends)` sends), poll order is release, retransmit, spray (a retransmission or a handover means no bundle was done, and a handover means the retransmission scan of that poll found nothing to send) | `Forwarder::{advance, poll}` | `DtnForward.tla`: `A_TimersExact`, `A_TimersFrozenDown`, `A_LeavesOnlyDone`, `A_GiveUpOnly`, `SendsExact`; DtnForward.cfg takes T 1, so the transfer table fills (QuietSound's full case) | `advance_timer_up`, `advance_timer_down`, `poll_release_done`, `poll_handover_scan_empty`, `poll_gives_up_only`, `reach_sends`, `gives_up_exact` | none | as above |
| a finished bundle stays finished: every accepted bundle is remembered in a bounded table (`F` slots) until its own expiry, held or released (delivered, expired, forgotten); a hold of a remembered, unexpired bundle that is no longer held is ignored and counted, never given fresh copies; a full table refuses new holds rather than forget a finished bundle; so the copies in existence never exceed the budget, also with four nodes and a replayed hold | `Forwarder::{step, poll, ignored_holds}` | `DtnForward_rehold.cfg` (`A_NoReHold`, `A_MemoryKeeps`, `A_FinishedSound`), `DtnForwardNet_replay.cfg` (`NoReplay`), `DtnForwardNet_over.cfg` (`HeldAlways`) | `DtnForwardWitness`: `hold_finished`, `step_keeps_entry`, `poll_keeps_entry`, `finished_after_release`, `finished_after_forget`, `finished_run`, `full_run` | none | `trace_dtn_forward`: 5342 events; `trace_dtn_scenario`: 5781 events |
| GF(2^8) with 0x11d is a field and the table `mul` and `inv` equal the polynomial product for all 65536 pairs; the slice kernels are pointwise (the 8-byte word path too) | `gf256::{mul, inv, mul_add, scale, xor_into}` | `DtnGf.tla`: `ExpLog`, `MulIsPoly`, `Field`, `Kernels` | `DtnGf.mulT_eq_pmul` (kernel-checked), `DtnGfField.mulT_assoc`, `mulT_xor`, `mulT_invT`, `DtnGfSlice.mulAdd_eq`, `scale_eq`, `wordXor_eq` | none | `trace_dtn_rs`: 6026 events, every `inv` and a spread of `mul` |
| Reed-Solomon: the Cauchy code is MDS; `reconstruct` returns the whole code word from any k symbols with the closed-form Cauchy inverse correct; `Rs::new` accepts exactly 1 <= k and k+m <= 255; at most 127 erasures; `Buffer` and `Incomplete` (block untouched) errors | `Rs::{new, encode, reconstruct}` | `DtnRs.tla` (`Recover`, `Mds`, `Bounds`; 5 configs) | `DtnCauchy.pf`, `cauchy_BA`, `cauchy_AB`, `DtnRs.mds`, `reconCol_ok`, `reconCol_buffer`, `reconCol_incomplete`, `validParams_iff`, `erasures_le`; `DtnRsSym` (whole symbols from the slice kernels): `mulAdd_spec`, `scale_spec`, `encodeSym_spec`, `reconSym_col`, `reconSym_recovers` | the in-place updates of `solve` are modelled as their dataflow (a written slot is read only when final) | as above, with 14 shapes and the 127-erasure solve |
| slice layout is exact: counts, `locate`, `byte_range`, padding below one slice, no overflow at any bound once `new` succeeded; the clock and lifetime saturate and expire at `expires <= now`; the dtn labels are distinct | `SliceLayout`, `Hlc`, `Head::{expires_at, is_expired}`, `labels` | `DtnLayout.tla`, `DtnClock.tla` (u64 limb words: any overflow stops TLC) | `DtnLayout.new_iff`, `all_fit`, `blocks_cover`, `locate_iff`, `locate_inj`, `byteRange_spec`, `DtnLayoutOutboard.outboard_len_eq` (the outboard is 64 bytes per parent of the tree over the symbols and fits `u64`), `DtnLayoutHlc.tick_lt`, `observe_gt_remote`, `expired_iff` | `locate` assumes a 64-bit `usize` | `trace_dtn_layout`: 2143 events including the exact overflow boundary |
| head and ack codecs are canonical: a round trip, injective, every proper prefix, trailing bytes, an unknown tag and wrong endorsement lengths are rejected | `SignedHead`, `CustodyAck` encode and decode | `DtnCodec.tla` (all four identity algorithms, every mutation) | `DtnCodec`, `DtnCodecWire`: `signed_roundtrip`, `signed_canonical`, `signed_injective`, `signed_trailing`, `signed_truncated`, `ack_*` | signature validity is the crypto assumption | `trace_dtn_codec`: 11236 events |
| reception: only verified bytes enter the buffer, the held set only grows, a full set keeps earlier symbols; `finish` reconstructs each block in caller-provided scratch and copies back only the symbols that are not held, so it never writes a held symbol (also when it fails), reports `Incomplete` exactly when a block holds fewer than k symbols (earlier blocks done, later ones untouched), `Buffer` for a wrong-sized outboard or scratch, and `Ok` exactly when every block that is not fully held is rebuilt to the signed root; `next_missing` returns the first run of unheld symbols; resume keeps the verified ranges | `Receiving::{feed, finish, scratch_len, resume}` (over any held set), `Reception::{new, next_missing}` (the range-set `Receiving<'a, VerifiedRanges<W>>`) | `DtnReception.tla` (a small real code over GF(5); `HeldAlways`, `FinishCorrect`, `FinishBufferCorrect`; `DtnReception_w3.cfg` for a held set of 3) | `DtnReception`: `feedGo_verified`, `feedGo_mono`, `feed_ok_holds`, `feed_empty`, `finish_held_untouched`, `block_ok_iff`, `honest_block_ok`, `finish_complete_iff`, `finish_stops`, `finish_exact`, `blocksOf_ordered`, `resume_spec`; `DtnReceptionNext`: `next_missing_some`, `next_missing_none`, `feedGo_closed`, `feedGo_within` | tree verifier soundness, signature validity and `Rs::reconstruct` (proved in `DtnRs`, `DtnRsSym`) are hypotheses | `trace_dtn_reception`: 3936 events |
| per-block held set: a reception into a `BlockHeld` never refuses a symbol of its bundle (no interval cap, whatever its size); each block's count is the number of its held symbols, at most `k + m`, so a byte holds it and a new symbol adds exactly one; a held symbol stays held; only whole symbols are recorded; the running total of held symbols is their number, and summing the counts of a kept buffer gives it back | `held::{BlockHeld, HeldSet}`, `Receiving` | `DtnReception_blocks.cfg` (a held set of 4 intervals, more than 7 symbols can split into: every insert fits, the same rules hold) | `DtnHeld`: `inv_empty`, `inv_insert`, `inv_insertAll`, `count_le`, `insert_new_count`, `insert_mono`, `insert_holds`, `decodable_iff`, `cnt_blocks`, `held_of_counts` | the bits and counts are modelled as functions on `Nat`; `per = k + m <= 255` is the layout's bound | `dtn_held`: the counts and the total against the bits under proptest, also for a set continued from a buffer; `TraceDtnReception.tla` and the Lean replay: real receptions into a `BlockHeld` lent as `&mut` (the cap of 4: it never refuses), feeds, finish and resume |
| what a receiver asks for: data first, each block at most what it is short of k, parity only in place of a symbol the holder could not prove (so with no loss only data is fetched); a held symbol, or one this transfer lost, is never asked; a transfer whose holder can no longer complete a block is refused, not drained, after at most m + 1 lost symbols of the block still missing; what a holder lost belongs to its transfer, so holders feeding one partial at once never refuse an honest one; the bundle finishes when some holder can prove k symbols of every block and keeps offering | `Dtn::want`, `dtn_offered`, `dtn_advance`, `dtn_data` | `DtnRequest.tla` (`NoParityWithoutLoss`, `AskWithinNeed`, `AskFresh`, `ActiveReachable`, `LostBounded`, `HonestNotRefused`, `FinishDecodable`, `Progress` under fairness, with concurrent transfers; 4 GREEN configs; RED `_nomark` (liveness), `_parityfirst`, `_noneed`, `_shared`, `_nostuck`) | the per-block count it decides with: `DtnHeld` | a corrupt symbol of a holder's copy is its only loss; the transport delivers a frame whole or ends the stream | `TraceDtnWant.tla`: the real `Dtn::want` (through `dtn_want_probe`) for every held and lost set of a bundle of two blocks of 2 + 1 symbols at every valid cursor (4^6 pairs) is the model's `Want`, with `NoParityWithoutLoss`, `AskWithinNeed` and `AskFresh` on each ask and `Decodable` on each finish; `TraceDtnRequest.tla`: real transfers from one holder whose copy is whole, corrupt in one data symbol of each block, in two symbols of block 1, or everywhere, follow the model's one behaviour for that case event for event (kept runs, lost symbols, parity asks, refusals, the finish) with every invariant of `DtnRequest.cfg` on every state; several holders and drops are not driven |
| one-shot encryption: the payload is secret to anyone without both recipient static key halves (neither primitive broken alone is enough); the key is bound to recipient, epoch, bundle id and every public value; a wrong recipient or epoch fails; `decapsulate` recomputes the bundle id from the head's source, creation time and ephemeral key and refuses a head whose id differs; a forged KEM ciphertext gives another key; a nonce is never reused under a key; the signed head is authentic. There is no forward secrecy beyond static-key rotation: compromise of the recipient static secrets later reveals every bundle sealed to those keys | `seal::{encapsulate, decapsulate}`, `Cipher`, `SignedHead` | `DtnSeal.tla` (`SecrecyIff`, `PayloadSecret`, `DecapSound`, `SliceBinding`, `NonceUnique`, `AuthSound`); witnesses `_nofs`, `_bothbroken`, `_reuse` | `DtnSeal*.lean`: `known_key_iff`, `payload_secret`, `only_x_broken`, `only_k_broken`, `no_forward_secrecy`, `key_binding`, `decap_wrong_iff`, `forged_ct_other_key`, `nonce_inj`, `slice_binding`, `seal_nonces_distinct`, `head_authentic` | perfect primitives; hash collision resistance and signature unforgeability as hypotheses; seeds never reused | `trace_dtn_seal`: 637 events |
| scenarios: copies conserved across the network under any contact schedule and loss (held + in flight + dropped + released = budget, held <= budget); custody handoff chains keep a custodian; expiry during an outage releases while timers stay frozen; a bundle reaches its destination if a contact recurs before expiry | `Forwarder`, `CustodyBook`, `Ledger` with host glue | `DtnScenario.tla` (12 configs): `Account`, `HeldLeBudget`, `CustodyAlive`, `A_CustodyExit`, `A_TickExpires`, `Served` under fairness; witness `nolive`; `_cycle` and `_replay` hold; DtnScenario_spray and _replay end at the bundle's expiry (Exp = MaxTime), so `A_TickExpires` is met | `DtnScenario*.lean`: `copies_conserved`, `held_le_budget`, `custody_chain`, `relief_only_by_offered_ack`, `tick_expires`, `expiry_release`, `delivery_schedule` | the host may replay a hold of a finished bundle: it is ignored; liveness side conditions: room in the transfer table and the store, lasting contacts; the Lean liveness is a schedule form, not fair traces | `trace_dtn_scenario`: 5781 events from 4 real nodes |

| membership, incarnations and epochs are monotone across restart and clock jumps: the persisted counter is `max(remembered, floor) + 1` sealed before use; the clock fallback is never at or below the floor; the word only goes up through records and observed words; exhaustion is reported, never wrapped; the view epoch only goes up | `Incarnation::{start, next_record, observe}`, `Membership::view_epoch` | `Incarnation.tla`: `EpochBound`, `SealedBeforeUse`, `Mono`, `FloorOK`, `ObserveOK`, `HlcMono` (`Incarnation.cfg`, `_pers`, `_hlc`), witness `Incarnation_clock.cfg` (a clock that went back reissues a lower word without the counter or the floor); `MembershipSys_restart.cfg` | `MemInc.restart_word_gt`, `start_epoch_gt`, `start_hlc_gt_floor`, `nextRecord_word_lt`, `observe_word_lt`, `start_inv`, `nextRecord_inv`, `observe_inv`, `*_exhausted`; `lineage_word_mono`; `ViewEpoch` (`applySnap_epoch`, `sweepAbsent_epoch`, `recheckDue_epoch`, `renew_epoch`) | the wall clock is arbitrary; without a store the host passes the highest epoch it heard as the floor | `TraceIncarnation.tla` and the Lean replay: 6546 events (restarts, clock jumps, rollover, the 48-bit edge) |
| membership, accuracy: a live member is never confirmed down by a single lost probe, a slow member, a snapshot after a heal, or another hub's DOWN while this hub's lease is fresh; a confirmation needs a probe on an independent path: the second probe never takes the path of the session (it goes through another hub's relay, the first one the host takes it for), and a probe the host refused is not a sent probe, so with none sent the hub waits for the lease; a suspicion is not an event: the hub and the view report only the confirmation (no `MemberSuspected`), and the internal suspicion record that drives the probes ends when the member answers | `Membership::{carrier_watch, on_down, apply_snap}` | `MembershipSys_{lostprobe,slow,partition,steady,jump}.cfg`: `NeverConfirmed`-style invariants, `DownOK`; witnesses `_w_confirm`, `_w_down`, `_w_susp` and `_w_lostprobe_susp` (`NeverSusp`: a suspicion record is kept after a crash, and after a lost probe, until the member answers), `_w_direct` and `_w_refused` (`SecondProbe <- OldSecondProbe`, the rule before: the second probe on the direct path of the same session, and a refused probe counted as sent, confirm a live member: `NoConf14` fails) | `cw_cleared`, `independentRoute_path` (the second probe is never on the session's path), `cw_lone_old` (the first probe's wait is over), `cw_lone_waiting` (inside it the check is scheduled for its end), `cw_lone_new`, `carrierWatch_keeps_unless_reach`, `onDown_fresh_keeps`, `onDown_refuses`, `onDown_accepts`, `applySnap_copies_mono`, `recomputeOne_listed_stays`, `recomputeOne_removal_requires`, `markAbsent_marks_only_unlisted` | the schedule hypotheses of `Silent` (no evidence after the drop); the code can send a probe on no named path of the session, so there is no probe on the direct path | `TraceMembership.tla` and the Lean replay: 24626 calls over the real hub and member machines (loss, partitions and heals, slow members, probes on every path, every DOWN refusal, the home switch of #343, a heal and a late timer while a removal waits, #284) |

witness `MembershipSys_live_w_stale.cfg` (the rule before the fix, `ViewAbsent <- OldViewAbsent`: `LiveCompleteAll` fails); `WaitKept` in the 16 safety configs that check `Bounds` (a call that is not a late timer moves a waiting removal only on its own schedule); witnesses `MembershipSys_w_healwait.cfg` (the rule before #284, `WaitOnQuiet <- OldWaitOnQuiet`, under which a heal defers every waiting removal again: `WaitKept` fails) and `MembershipSys_w_healseen.cfg` (`NeverHealWait` fails: the run reaches a heal while a removal waits) | `hubTick_fresh`, `hubTick_expires`, `hubTick_expires_c`, `onTimeout_expires`, `applyDeltaCore_down_defers` (a deferred removal waits at the window's end with the window's grace state, or under the entry it already had), `beginQuiet_window`, `beginQuiet_heal` (a heal leaves every waiting removal as it is), `beginQuiet_late` (a late timer defers every waiting removal to the new window's end), `recomputeOne_defers`, `recheckDue_waits`, `recheckDue_arms` (the first tick at or after a removal's instant gives it the extra look), `recheckDue_removes` (the tick after the look removes it with no window open, whatever window is open), `removeFromView_holds` (a removal held for want of a confirmed home view is queued again), `recheckDue_clears` (source not held), `deferred_removal`, `down_then_gone`, `down_then_gone_ticks`, `awareness_budget`; over any sequence of the host's calls, in `MembershipTimerRun.lean`: `step_tm` (no call but `on_timeout` moves the tick deadline), `pollTimeout_le`, `onTimeout_ticks`, `tick_window` (a tick in every window of one tick period plus the host's slack); `MembershipTimerLease.lean`: `runCalls_held` (no call renews a crashed member's lease), `hub_lease_gone` (the lease is gone at a hub tick in `(t0 + 80 s, t0 + 81 s + σ]`); `MembershipTimerDown.lean`: `hub_hands_down`, `hubTick_hands_down` (that tick hands the DOWN delta to every attached hub); `MembershipTimerQuiet.lean`: `heal_keeps_waiting` (a heal opens the window and leaves the recheck table), `heal_then_removed`, `heal_then_gone` (a removal a window deferred is out of the view at its own instant plus the extra look after a heal), `late_tick_reopens` (a late tick opens the window and defers every waiting removal to its end) | the chain is proved link by link (lease, delta, view, quiet window, second tick) and `awareness_budget` adds up the times (80 s lease, 1 s hub tick, 10 s window, two 0.5 s node ticks, 5 s grace, so 145 s holds when the DOWN reaches the node within 48 s); the hub side is a theorem over runs whose one timing hypothesis is the host's (`Timely σ`: each call comes within `σ` of the deadline `poll_timeout` returned; the 1 s of the budget is `σ = 0`); that the DOWN reaches the member within 48 s is a hypothesis; the quiet-window link needs that the member's host is not later than `WAKE_GAP` at a tick between the DOWN and the removal: a late tick defers every waiting removal to the new window's end (`late_tick_reopens`, the reference model's rule), while a heal leaves it at its own instant (#284: `heal_keeps_waiting`, `recheckDue_removes`); that the removal's own instant is within `QUIET` of the DOWN is the window's length (`beginQuiet_window`) and stays a hypothesis of `awareness_budget`, not proved over runs; the rule of #343 (`mark_view_absent`) is in the Lean model of `apply_snap`, so the theorems over `apply_snap` (the invariants, the epoch, the copies only growing, the timers) cover it; it is model checked and traced, but no Lean theorem states its effect yet | as above, with a heal and a late timer while a removal waits (#284) |

| hub push under backpressure: a push a subscriber's full stream refuses (`Host::backlogged`) keeps the subscriber's place in the log; it is pushed nothing new directly until `peer_writable` caught it up, in order; a store that would evict a message still owed to it is refused, so it is not acknowledged and the publisher retries (a hub's own publish is refused with `Refused::Backlog`); a subscriber whose stream took nothing for 2 s (`PUSH_STALL_MS`) or that is owed a message older than 30 s (`PUSH_OWED_MAX`) is let go: it stops holding back the log, keeps being served from it, and is told with RESTORED (`Event::Restored`) once caught up if the log evicted anything it was owed. A subscriber kept behind whose session is replaced (its new stream is never reported writable) is served again when it subscribes on the new session (`on_sub` runs `peer_writable`, pinc#428). A subscriber whose session ended at the hub is released, comes back, and asks for what it lacks (GET, or the replay of a SUB): what its stream has no room for puts it behind at the earliest such message, let go from the start, so the repair is finished from the log and never paces the topic (pinc#504). A hub that cannot give a subscriber everything it asks for tells it (RESTORED): a subscription made live again that asks from a time at or before a message the log dropped since, or from after the first message the hub owed a subscriber whose session ended while it was behind and no later than the hub forgot that place; and a GET that names an id the log dropped since it was offered (pinc#505). Every acknowledged message reaches every subscriber (every subscriber in the origin's component when the subscriber is scoped, pinc#86), or the subscriber is told it missed some | `behind::{fall_behind, room_to_store, peer_writable}`, `gap::{on_sub, on_get, owe, tell_short}`, `msg::hub_store`, `log::HubLog::{evicts, seq_of, dropped_since}`, `node::Topic::{forget_place, forgot_since}` | `BroadcastPush.tla`: `AckedReachesLive`, `MissedOnlyHeld`, `OwedInLog`, `Bounds`, `AllAcked` (under fairness; `_n6` with six messages, the smallest at which a let-go subscriber misses an evicted position, so `MissedOnlyHeld` and the missed-or-told part of `AckedReachesLive` are exercised); `_nocursor` finds the acknowledged push dropped (before 05bf029), `_noguard` finds the owed message evicted after it was acknowledged (the place in the log without the store refusal), `_notell` finds a let-go subscriber past a gap it is never told about; `WakeOrPending` and `LiveAgain` with one session swap per subscriber, `_noresume` finds a subscriber behind with nothing to wake it and `_noresume_live` finds it never caught up (before pinc#428); `_repair` checks all of it with one session end per subscriber (the place forgotten, the SUB, the GET answered into a stream that fills), and `_getdrop` finds the rest of the GET dropped: `AckedReachesLive` fails (before pinc#504); `_away` checks all of it with a session that ends at any time and a log that evicts freely while the subscriber is away or its GET is on its way, and `_nosubtell`, `_noforgot` and `_nogettell` each leave one notice rule out and find an acknowledged message the subscriber neither gets nor is told about (before pinc#505) | `BroadcastLogModel.pushOwed_inv`, `pushOwed_keeps_owed`, `pushOwed_refused`; `BroadcastWake.wake_inv`, `stuck_closed`, `wake_stuck_without`, `sub_wakes`; `evicts_predicts_push` (Rust) checks that `evicts` counts what `push` evicts | the publisher is paced by the slowest subscriber that is not let go, only once the log is full of what that subscriber is owed; a host whose `backlogged` answers false (the default) keeps the old behaviour; the model takes a SUB to ask from at or before every position the subscriber lacks, except those the hub owed it at a place it forgot (the rule "forgot" is for those); the code keeps one span for the topic where the model keeps the forgotten place of each subscriber, so the code may tell a subscriber the model does not, never the reverse; a notice of a GET to a subscriber that is behind is sent once it has caught up and counts as told at once in the model; a session the hub moves the subscriber off (`Swap`) is taken to lose nothing the log no longer holds, and such a subscriber is not told (pinc#508); the Lean files model the log's eviction and the wake states, which the repair and notice steps do not change | `TraceBroadcastPush.tla`: about 600 events of a hub with a log of three messages and two subscribers with streams of two frames (the code's `hub_store`, `behind.rs` and `peer_writable` on real frames: stores, refusals of a store that would evict a message still owed, duplicates, drains and the pushes a writable stream resumes, scripted and 250 seeded actions): after each event the log bounds, each subscriber's place and each stream equal the model's, and `Inv` holds (`AckedReachesLive`, `MissedOnlyHeld`, `OwedInLog`, `Bounds`, `WakeOrPending`); letting go, swaps, the end of a stream, SUB and GET are not driven; `tests/broadcast_behind.rs`, `tests/broadcast_repair.rs`, `tests/broadcast_told.rs` and `tests/node_burst.rs` run them |

| facade access decisions per protocol and direction (#440), against the endpoint's one 30 s answer per peer, with revoke, unrevoke and hook changes: no session is taken up on an allow the hook gave for another protocol or direction, as a session is taken up only if the gate allows its own protocol and direction at that moment or the remembered allow for exactly them covers it, whatever the host does; the endpoint holds a deny only for a peer the gate allows no listed protocol in either direction and no protocol and direction of a session or an unfinished dial this node holds with it, whatever its protocol (a node may dial a protocol it does not list), and no refusal closes a session the gate allows at that moment, so a refusal of one protocol never refuses or closes a session or a dial of another the hook allows; a dial the gate allows drops a refusal the endpoint holds for its peer, so that dial's session asks with its own question; every session is decided with the answers the gate gives at that moment (the facade's: are the session's own protocol and direction allowed; the endpoint's: an allow only for a peer the gate allows something, a refusal only when the gate allows none of the questions a refusal must not stand against, the session's own among them), so a deny cached while a peer was revoked or refused never refuses it after `unrevoke` or `admission_changed`, and an allow cached before `revoke` never lets it through after; every cached allow is for a peer the gate allows something now and every remembered allow is the gate's current one, so none outlives a revoke, an unrevoke or a hook change, and clearing the table of remembered allows at any time breaks none of this; a dial to a revoked peer is refused before anything is sent, so the peer never holds a session of ours its own dial could ride (the second part of #440: before it the answer was worked out over the listed protocols only, and a later check of a session this node dialed asks the inbound question since no dial waits any more, so an outbound session on a protocol the node does not list was closed about 25 s in, and a refusal held for a peer failed a dial the hook allows for up to 30 s) | `crates/pinc/src/engine.rs` `Core::{session_up, session_admitted, admits_other}`, the `AcpCheck` arm of `on_event`; `crates/pinc/src/engine/gate.rs` `Known::{covers, remember, forget}`, `direction`; `crates/pinc/src/engine/ops.rs` `Core::{connect_start, revoke, unrevoke, forget_access, listen}`; `crates/pinc-core/src/commands.rs` `Command::AcpForget`, `Query::PeerSession` (the sessions and dials of a peer, from the endpoint's per-peer list), `Query::Access` (the answer held for a peer); `crates/pinc-core/src/acp.rs` `start_acp`, `ask_acp`, `acp_answer`; `crates/pinc/src/endpoint.rs` `Endpoint::{revoke, unrevoke, connect, admission_changed}` | `FacadeAcpForget.tla`, with the questions of the sessions and dials held with each peer and a mark set when a refusal closes a session the gate allows: `NoCrossAllow`, `NoCrossDeny`, `NoCut` (the mark is never set), `DecideFresh`, `CacheSound`, `KnownSound`, `NoDialRevoked`, `TypeOK` (`FacadeAcpForget`: one peer, two listed protocols taken either way, 445360 states, 23376 distinct; in each any hook at the start and the host editing it one answer at a time, sessions and dials ending at any step, remembered allows lapsing or the whole table cleared at any step); witnesses `FacadeAcpForget_perpeer` (the rule before #440: the endpoint gets the hook's answer for the session that raised the check and no session is asked on its own, so a protocol and direction the hook refuses comes up on an allow given for another, `NoCrossAllow` fails), `FacadeAcpForget_noany` (the endpoint told the asking session's own refusal: a peer the hook allows another protocol is denied, `NoCrossDeny` fails), `FacadeAcpForget_nolift` (the code before the unrevoke fix: the cached deny outlives `unrevoke`, `DecideFresh` fails), `FacadeAcpForget_norevoke` (the cached allow outlives `revoke`, `DecideFresh` fails), `FacadeAcpForget_nodial` (a dial to a revoked peer goes out, `NoDialRevoked` fails) and `FacadeAcpForget_nohook` (the host allows a peer without `admission_changed`, `DecideFresh` fails), `FacadeAcpForget_unlisted` (the answer worked out over the listed protocols only: a refusal closes the session or the dial of a protocol the node does not list, which the hook allows, `NoCut` fails) and `FacadeAcpForget_nodialask` (a refusal kept across a dial the hook allows, `NoCrossDeny` fails) | `FacadeAcpForgetThm.lean`: `inv_reach` (any number of peers and steps, any protocols listed and dialed), `noCrossAllow_reach` (any host), `noCrossDeny_reach`, `noCut_reach`; | facade standby slab and the drive's turn: every entry of the queue of candidates for the driving role (a queue of tickets) names a slot of the slab, and a queued slot is live with the entry of its ticket in the queue; no stale entry ever names a live ticket again, whatever the calls (a slot released unarmed, or the bare slab driven past 2^32 releases of a slot): the release that brings a slot's generation round (from `u32::MAX` to 0) drops every stale entry, so an entry of an earlier round never matches a new ticket and `alloc` never hands out a ticket an entry of the queue names (since the fix of [#419](https://github.com/sourcenetwork/pinc/issues/419): before it a stale entry named a live slot again once its slot was released a multiple of 2^32 times while the entry waited, which the bare slab could reach); so the slot of every live entry is queued, no ticket has two live entries, and the queue holds at most twice as many entries as the slab has slots (arm drops the stale entries when it holds that many); a slot keeps a waker only while it is live and queued; the free stack holds exactly the slots not live, each once; the slab is as long as the most tickets held at once; an `arm`, `notify` or `release` with a ticket that names no live slot of its generation changes nothing and wakes nothing, and a released ticket stays dead until its slot was released a multiple of 2^32 times; the baton wakes the waker of the first entry of the queue whose ticket is live and armed (every entry up to it leaves the queue, the slot of each live one is unqueued and a stale one's slot is left as it is), exactly one whenever a slot is armed; the live entries of the queue are the tickets waiting for the role in the order they were queued, so a ticket that reuses a released slot queues behind those already waiting and the baton wakes the oldest armed ticket, as `standby.rs` documents (since the fix of [#311](https://github.com/sourcenetwork/pinc/issues/311): before it a ticket that reused a slot still queued kept that place). In the turn: at most one future drives, exactly while the flag is set, and the core's driver ticket is its ticket (with the relay role, only the owner); no future holds a stale ticket, each live slot is held by one future and released once, by it; a slot's waker is its holder's and a waiting future's waker is kept; every datagram the host delivered is still with the host or was handed to the core exactly once, in order, at most `DRAIN` in a poll; while the flag is free and a future that may drive waits, one that may drive and holds a ticket is running or flushing, so the role is taken under fairness | `crates/pinc/src/standby.rs` `Standby::{alloc, arm, drop_stale, notify, baton, notify_all, release}`; `crates/pinc/src/drive.rs` `Shared::run_as` (the poll function: the registration, the role, the burst), `Turn::drop` | `FacadeStandby.tla` over `StandbyOps.tla` (`DROP` the release of the code, `FALSE` the release before #419), with the documented order and a mark set once a stale entry names a live slot again beside the slab: `NoRevive` (the mark is never set), `QueueOnce`, `QueueOrder`, `WrapCost`, `WakerQueued`, `FreeExact`, `SlabBound`, `GenRange`, `DeadStay`, `StaleRight`, `AllocRight`, `WakeRight`, `BatonRight`, `BatonOldest` (3 tickets held at once, 7 handed out, 2 generations, so slots' generations go round, every stale ticket and a forged one: 2014883 states, 76806 distinct); `FacadeStandby_wrap` (2 tickets held at once, 5 handed out, 2 generations, the run of the witness below among them: `QueueStrict` holds too, 18770 states, 969 distinct); witnesses `FacadeStandby_oldest` (the code before the fix of #311: `BatonOldest` fails), `FacadeStandby_wrapkeep` (the release before the fix of #419: a ticket armed and released, its slot taken and released again and taken a third time: its stale entry names a live slot, `QueueStrict` fails) and `FacadeStandby_nogen` (one generation: a stale ticket names a later future's slot, `NoStaleHit` fails); `FacadeTurn.tla` over the same operators: `OneDriver`, `TicketsLive`, `WakerOwner`, `WaitArmed`, `QueueOnce` (`QueueLive` and `QueueExact` of `StandbyOps.tla`), `WakerQueued`, `FreeExact`, `SlabBound`, `FedExact`, `DrainBound`, `RoleOffered` (`FacadeTurn`: three futures, three datagrams, a burst of two, 943105 states, 110148 distinct; `FacadeTurn_two`: two futures started three times each, 1878159 states, 241176 distinct; `FacadeTurn_role`: the relay role, 448377 states, 56760 distinct; `FacadeTurn_wrap`: one future drives while the other is started six times on one slot, 5 generations: the slot's generation goes round and the stale entry is gone first, 2549609 states, 357522 distinct; `FacadeTurn_wrapfew`: the same with 3 generations, not more than twice the slab: the release that brings the generation round drops the stale entry, 353879 states, 50444 distinct), `RoleTaken` under fairness (`FacadeTurn_live`, 248245 states, 31116 distinct); witnesses `FacadeTurn_nopass` (a future that ends while the flag is free does not pass the baton on: a waiting future is stranded, `RoleOffered` fails), `FacadeTurn_drain` (a poll stops at the bound with datagrams still at the host) and `FacadeTurn_wrapkeep` (the release before the fix of #419 with 3 generations: a stale entry names a live slot again, `QueueOnce` fails) | `FacadeStandbyThm.lean`: `Inv` with `init_inv`, `alloc_inv`, `arm_inv`, `notify_inv`, `baton_inv`, `notifyAll_inv`, `release_inv`, `exec_inv`; `alloc_unnamed`, `exec_norevive` (no run revives a stale entry, so `NoRevive` holds of every run), and from the empty slab over any run `exec_exact` (`QInv`), `exec_inorder` (the documented order) and `exec_oldest`; `FacadeTurn.lean`: `inv_reach` (any number of futures, datagrams and steps), `pollWait_takes`, `noPass_bad` | the async parts are assumed of the host ([#246](https://github.com/sourcenetwork/pinc/issues/246) (c)): its executor polls every woken future, a dropped receive future loses no datagram, `sleep(at)` does not finish before `at`, every future runs on the thread that built the endpoint (a poll is one step, and no borrow of the core is held across an await, so the borrow a poll takes succeeds); the environment may notify any held ticket at any time (a superset of the core's wait lists); a transient receive error is no datagram; the liveness is TLC's only (Lean proves `RoleOffered` and that the polled future takes the role); the turn's decisions are not traced (its I/O is the host's); `FacadeTurn.lean` carries the slab's `Inv` and not `QInv` (the exact queue inside the turn is TLC's, `FacadeTurn_wrap` and `FacadeTurn_wrapfew`; Lean's `exec_exact` is of runs of the slab's calls); the drop at the wrap is not traced: the probe offers no way to set a generation and releasing a slot 2^32 times is out of a trace test's reach (TLC with 2 and 3 generations and Lean with 2^32 check it, and the unit test `a_stale_entry_does_not_name_the_ticket_a_wrapped_generation_hands_out` of `standby.rs` sets one) | `trace_standby` (60 seeded runs of a real slab, `StandbyProbe` behind the facade's `trace` feature, against a naive slab and the documented invariants, with stale, forged and reused tickets, slots reused while the entry of their released ticket is queued, and one slot reused 200 times, which compacts the queue: 8060 events), `TraceFacadeStandby.tla`, same replay, both also keeping the documented order: every alloc hands out a ticket no entry of the queue names, the live entries are that order after every call, and every baton wakes its oldest armed ticket |
| dial report: an optimistic dial (a cached allow) is reported at once, before its handshake, and ends as `Unanswered` when its resend timer fires after 4 first flights went out with nothing heard from the peer (15 first delays for a full handshake, 7.5 s at the 500 ms floor; 6 for a resume, 3 s); a held flight is not sent and does not count, a message from the peer (a rejected ticket too) starts the count again, a thin node keeps its 60 s, and only the handshake's own limit raises the node's floor; a dial to a running peer ends early only if all 4 counted flights were lost; a dial over a held session is reported at once; a dial reported before its peer answered (the optimistic one, a sub-session) is reported unanswered and gets `Event::Answered` once the peer is heard on it (its handshake ends, or its first packet arrives), so `Connection::answered` returns only for a peer that was heard; a sub-session opened over a session whose handshake still runs waits for its keys, and getting them is not an answer | `crates/pinc-core/src/commands.rs` `connect` (`end_unanswered` on the optimistic branch); one that never answered keeps its connect limit; the hold is fixed (nothing the peer sends moves it); the end of the handshake is the first instant at which it has run out, and an input handed over at that instant is taken before the timers due at it, so a Finish that arrives at the end instant still completes the handshake, and a half-open session is gone once the timers of the instant 60 s after its creation have run: the half-open cap bounds the table at 256 sessions of that life; ## Properties of the models added with the trace tests

The rows below came with the trace tests and model-based tests of the PR line, one table per crate group. A trace
check named in a row runs in the proofs step unless the row says it waits for its test or a code fix.

### broadcast

| Property | Rust item | TLA+ | Lean theorem | Assumptions | Trace validation |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| the adjacency record: `parse` takes a record only in the one layout its writer makes (origin, tag, the key unless Ed25519, incarnation, sequence number, a count of at most `ADJ_MAX` peers, the peers strictly ascending and without the origin, the signature of the tag's length, nothing after), so a parsed record writes back to the same bytes and no proper prefix or longer string parses; the signed bytes alone decide every field but the signature, so a signature over their digest binds origin, algorithm, key, incarnation, sequence number and peers; a node's own record names its peers sorted, without itself, the least `ADJ_MAX` of more, and verifies under its key; a record verifies only under the key its origin id names | `reach/record.rs` `{parse, verify, digest}`, `reach.rs` `Topics::{adj_frame, set_adjacent}` | `BroadcastAdjRecord.tla`: `WellFormed`, `Canonical`, `Strict`, `SignedBinds` over every byte string of up to 7 symbols over {0, 1, 2}, origin and peers of 1 symbol, the integers of 0 symbols, a 2-symbol count, at most 2 peers, tags 1 and 2 with keys and signatures of 1 symbol (3,280 states), and `RoundTrip` as an ASSUME over every well-formed record; witness `_peers` (`NoTwoPeers`: a record with two peers parses) | none | the signature schemes are unforgeable and the digest is collision free (ideal crypto); TLC shrinks the widths, the trace runs the code's | `TraceBroadcastAdjRecord.tla`: about 3,000 events: hand-made records of 8 keys (every algorithm) signed by the test, each cut, extended, re-tagged, recounted, with peers out of order, repeated or naming the origin, with a bit flipped in the signature, a field or a peer, under another origin or key; records four nodes wrote through `set_adjacent` (none, one, all, their own id and repeats given, 1,100 ids); 400 seeded random mutations. Each parse is the model's `Parse`, each verify verdict is the oracle of the signatures made (crypto ideal), and each written record writes back to its bytes, names its writer and holds the given peers |
| version 2 origin authentication: a message verifies only if its origin's key signed exactly its fields (algorithm, origin, incarnation, sequence number, topic, wall time, counter, flags, parents, body), so a statement made for one topic or origin is never accepted for another and no node, hub or node that carries the relay role can forge one; the key in the head must be the origin's (Ed25519: the origin is the key); `sign2` refuses, writing nothing, for a key of another algorithm, fields that make no statement or a buffer too short | `auth2::{sign2, verify2}`, `auth::origin_signed`, `pinc_crypto::identity::verify_node` | `BroadcastAuth2.tla`: `DeliverExact` (every delivered message from an honest origin equals in every field one its origin signed): honest nodes 1 (Ed25519) and 2, a Byzantine node 3, algorithms 1 and 2, every field of 2 values, at most 2 signatures, any one field changed, any key in the head, any held signature (about 1,000 states); witnesses `_delivers` (`NoDelivery`: a delivery is reached), `_notopic` (without the topic in the statement a message is accepted for another topic: `DeliverExact` fails), `_nokey` (without the key check a Byzantine signature is accepted for an honest origin: `DeliverExact` fails) | none | the signature schemes are unforgeable and BLAKE3 is collision free (ideal crypto: a signature is the term of its signer and statement); which fields make a statement is `BroadcastStatement.tla` | `TraceBroadcastAuth2.tla`: about 1,000 calls of `sign2` and `verify2` with 8 keys (two of each algorithm): every signature verified unchanged, with each field changed alone, with another node's key or junk in the head, with another signature or a flipped bit, signs refused for another algorithm, a non-statement or a short buffer, and 300 seeded random rounds; the checker keeps the signatures made and decides with the model's `Verify` whether each verify returns an id, and that it is the signer's content id |
| a hub's record goes back to an older one only over a tombstone and only from the member's own frame | pinc-broadcast reach/table.rs `Reach::take`, reach.rs `Topics::on_adj` | `BroadcastReach.tla` `NoRollback` (BroadcastReach_expire.cfg: N 3, MaxChanges 1, MaxExpire 2; BroadcastReach_table.cfg: TableBytes 7, MaxLeave 1) | none | ideal signatures; the pair (inc, seq) abstracted to one sequence number | not driven by TraceBroadcastReach (the test sends only a member's newest record) |
| the version 2 statement: the fields a signature covers are written as one DAG-CBOR map in the canonical key order, every integer under its shortest head, so one set of fields has one statement and no two sets share one (its content id names the fields); fields that make no statement are refused in the code's order (a topic over 255 bytes, more than 8 parents, parents not strictly ascending, a reserved flag) and nothing is written; a statement is at most `STATEMENT_MAX` bytes and the widest is exactly that long; the signed message is the statement label followed by the content id | `statement::{encode, cid, body_cid, content_id, signed_message}`, `STATEMENT_MAX` | `BroadcastStatement.tla`: `RoundTrip` (a strict reader that takes only shortest heads and the canonical key order gives every field back: the encoding is canonical and injective), `Bound`; `.cfg`: node ids and digests of 1 byte, at most 2 parents, topics of at most 2 bytes, every CBOR head boundary of the 64-bit and 32-bit fields, every algorithm, flags 0, 1, 2, 255 (about 300 field sets, one state each); `_wide`: the code's widths (32-byte ids and digests, 8 parents, 255-byte topics), the widest statement, a base one and each refusal (7 states), `RoundTrip` and `Bound`; witness `_refused` (`NoRefusal`: a refusal is reached) | none | BLAKE3 is collision free (the content id stands for the statement); the key order is the one the code's `MapKeys` enforces (an ASSUME); TLC shrinks the widths in `.cfg`, the trace runs the code's | `TraceBroadcastStatement.tla`: about 2,100 calls of `encode` (each integer field at every CBOR head boundary up to `u64::MAX`, topics of 0 to 256 bytes, 0 to 9 parents in and out of order, the flag classes, every algorithm, the widest statement, the refusal order, 2,000 seeded random field sets): the bytes or the refusal are the model's `Encode`, at most `STATEMENT_MAX`, and the signed message is the label and the content id |
| the version 2 head: one accepted encoding (a parsed head written back gives the bytes it was read from, and `write` accepts every head `parse` returns); `parse` takes exactly the body length the head states, so no proper prefix and no longer message parses; `parse_prefix` reads nothing past the head; `write` refuses what `parse` refuses and writes nothing then; the refusals and their order are the code's (`version`, `algorithm`, more than 8 `parents` before reading on, `message cut short`, `flags`, `parents` not strictly ascending, `key or signature` of the wrong length, `body length`); no head is longer than `MAX_HEAD2` | `wire2::Head2::{parse, parse_prefix, write, check}`, `wire::{read_key_sig, key_sig_fit}`, `MAX_HEAD2` | `BroadcastWire2.tla`: `Canonical`, `Strict`, `PrefixOnly` over every byte string of up to 2 symbols and every one of up to 9 that starts with the version, alphabet {0, 1, 2}, origin and digests of 1 symbol, the integers of 0 symbols, body length of 1, keys and signatures of 1, at most 1 parent, tags 1 and 2 (about 9,850 states); `RoundTrip` and `Bound` as ASSUMEs over every head of the alphabet, wrong key and signature lengths, reserved flags and bad parent lists included; witness `_keyed` (`NoKeyedParentHead`: a head with a parent and a key parses) | none | TLC shrinks the widths; the trace checks the code's widths (`FIXED_LEN` 68, `MAX_HEAD2` asserted as an ASSUME of the trace spec) | `TraceBroadcastWire2.tla`: about 9,000 calls of `parse`, `parse_prefix` and `write` (every algorithm, 0, 1 and 8 parents, the integers at 0 and their maxima, every refusal, every cut of the shorter heads, a body one byte long or short, single bytes changed, the widest head, 1,500 seeded random strings and 500 random heads): each result is the model's `Parse`, `ParsePrefix` or `Write`, and every written head is at most `MAX_HEAD2` |

### core

| Property | Rust item | TLA+ | Lean theorem | Assumptions | Trace validation |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| datagram queues: entries stored back to back in a ring of pool chunks, never straddling a chunk; a chunk returned to the pool as soon as its last entry is removed, an empty queue holding no chunk; at most `Q` entries in at most `C` chunks, each charged to the pool once; a full queue drops its oldest entries, only those, the rest in order; the drop counters count every datagram dropped to make room and every one refused by the pool (`send_dropped`) or not stored (`recv_dropped`), a datagram too large for `send_datagram` being an error, not a drop | `stream/datagram.rs` (`PackedQueue::push`, `push_drop_oldest`, `pop`, `clear`, `front`; `Datagrams::send_datagram`, `on_datagram_frame`, `pop_send`, `release`, `send_dropped`, `recv_dropped`, `clear`), over `stream/pool.rs` (`alloc_chunk`, `free_chunk`, `set_budget_bytes`) | `DgramQueue.tla` with `DgramQueueOps.tla`: `TypeOK`, `Bounded`, `Contiguous`, `PoolKept`, `NoStraddle`, `InHeldChunk`, `BackToBack`, `EveryChunkUsed`, `EmptyHoldsNoChunk`, `Counted`, `DropsOldest`, configs `DgramQueue_send` and `DgramQueue_recv` (3 entries, 2 chunks of 2 bytes, lengths 0 to 3, a pool of 2 shared with other users, its budget 0 to 2, counted as documented; states not measured yet, estimated under 20000 each); witnesses `DgramQueue_drop` (a push drops entries: `NoDrop` fails), `DgramQueue_sendcount` and `DgramQueue_recvcount` (findings, the code's counting: `Counted` fails) | none | the ring of entries is kept as its live window (places outside it are never read); the u8 and u16 narrowings of `push` cannot fail and are not modelled; the early-data buffer of the same file is not this unit | `TraceDgramQueue.tla`: about 12000 calls on real `Datagrams<4, 2>` and `Datagrams<64, 16>` over pools of 2 to 16 chunks: boundary runs (an empty datagram, a full chunk, too large for the chunk and for the max, drops, an empty pool, a budget lowered below what is charged) and 60 seeded runs of 200 calls; the counters, the chunks charged and the oldest datagram of each queue after every call |
| connection event queue: a ring of `QUEUE` places (640 with `alloc`) read from its head is a FIFO of events; a repeat of a queued event the application learns nothing more from (same kind and stream or path, or the same kind for the kinds that name none; never for datagrams, extensions and undeliverable extensions) is not queued; when full, a recoverable notification offered is dropped, an unrecoverable event gives way the oldest recoverable one queued, and is counted in `lost` only when there is none; every accepted unrecoverable event is queued, given to the application or counted lost; every queued place was written, so `pop` never comes back empty while events are queued; the written prefix never goes past `QUEUE`; with `queue_fits` holding for the callers, nothing is lost | `conn/event.rs` (`EvQueue::push`, `pop`, `find`, `remove`, `Ev::lossy`, `Ev::same_as`, `queue_fits`, `FIXED_EVENTS`), over `stream/lazy.rs` | `EvQueue.tla` with `EvQueueOps.tla`: `TypeOK`, `RingIsQueue`, `QueuedWritten`, `NoRepeat`, `Accounted`, `LostOnlyFull` (QUEUE = 3, events Readable 1, Datagram, Opened 1, Closed, an undeliverable extension; up to 2 lost; states not measured yet, estimated under 10000); `EvQueue_fits`: `NoLost` with 3 places for the 3 distinct unrecoverable events the callers can raise; the sizes 640 and 256 against `queue_fits` as ASSUMEs; `EvQueue_over` (QUEUE 4, two undeliverable extension events unread: `NoLost`, `RingIsQueue`, `Accounted` hold with FIXED_EVENTS 32) | none | `push_tickets` (it sets the batch and pushes `Tickets`) is not driven; that the callers raise at most `queue_fits`' count of distinct unrecoverable events is the premise, not checked here, and the extension send queue breaks it (finding) | `TraceEvQueue.tla`: about 10000 calls on real queues of 640 places: repeats, a queue full of unrecoverable events pushed on (drops, losses), full of recoverable ones then unrecoverable ones (each gives way the oldest, deep in the queue too), the head round the ring, pops of the last event, seeded mixes; against a naive queue; head, length, lost count and written prefix after every call, the queued events when at most 24 are queued, every 101st call and at the end of a run |
| interfaces, link changes and suspension: an event on an interface id that names no address changes nothing; a link going down or coming back reaches exactly the sessions, established or in their handshake, whose active path leaves from the interface; an address change moves every path that left from the old address to the new one and wakes the established sessions whose active path left from it; a suspension puts every established session to sleep; on resume a session is closed for a redial when the node moved (said by the host or seen while suspended), when it was timing out at its SLEEP, or when the node lost its link and was suspended before it came back, and is woken otherwise; the flags clear on resume; keepalives stay quiet for 10 s after a link return, an address change or a resume | `Endpoint::iface_event`, `link`, `addr_change`, `suspend`, `resume` (`iface.rs`); `Command::Suspend`, `Command::Resume` | `Iface.tla` over `IfaceOps.tla`, each event checked in every reached state for every event that could come next: `TypeOK`, `Ignored`, `Scope`, `AddrMoves`, `SuspendAll`, `ResumeRule`, `LostThenSuspended`, `SleepDoubtKept`, `MovedWhileSuspended`, `Quiet`, `Flags`; `Iface.cfg` 2 interfaces, 2 sessions, 3 addresses, a standby path, probe timeouts on or off (at most 1440 states); witnesses `Iface_w_doubt` (`NeverDoubt`: a session sleeps in doubt), `Iface_w_moved` (`NeverMoved`: the node moves while suspended) | none | the transport's link down and return, sleep and wake are the connection's (pinc-transport `life.rs`), seen here as calls; time is not in the model state (the trace checks `quiet_until`); a connection wakes only through a resume (the trace replay keeps the asleep flag from the model) | `TraceIface.tla`: about 200 calls of a real endpoint with 4 peers and 2 interfaces (links down and back, dead ids, address changes with active and standby paths, suspensions with a timeout, dark, moved, twice, and 150 seeded events), each replayed from the recorded view: the calls on every connection, the flags, the interfaces, the doubts, `quiet_until` in `u64` words and the paths moved |
| the endpoint's use of its tables: no table grows (at most `K` sessions; a first flight that finds the table full is dropped and counted, nothing kept); every session in the table holds one deadline in the heap, no later than the end of its life, and routing entries for its id, and a free slot holds neither; a session at or past its end (30 s and one tick; saturating at `u64::MAX`) is freed by the next timer call unless the event queue is stalled, and after a timer call below `u64::MAX` no deadline is at or before its time; a later part of a first message that names no session is held (one over 1500 bytes is refused) and handed, oldest first, to the session the start of its message makes from the same source, and to no other: no part is left held for a live session's message, and every held part is replayed, expired, evicted or waiting; while the event queue is stalled (2K + 961 unrecoverable events) a first flight, a part and a stray are counted in `stalled_in` and nothing else, a timer frees nothing and `poll_timeout` is none, and a dial is refused with `EventsPending`; a dial of this node's own id is refused and leaves one `ConnectFailed`; an empty, oversized or unroutable datagram is dropped and counted; every session command, `read_datagram`, `session_taken` and the `Stats` query on an id that names no live session (freed, never made, outside the table) is refused and changes nothing | `crates/pinc-core/src/endpoint.rs` (`alloc_slot`, `free_slot`, `slot_of`), `rx.rs` (`handle_datagram`, `unrouted`, `accept`, `replay_held`, `starts_held_message`), `timers.rs` (`settle_core`, `poll_timeout`, `handle_timeout`, `run_timer`), `commands.rs` (`command`, `live`, `connect`, `read_datagram`), `events.rs`, `orphan.rs`, `queue.rs` | `MbtEndpoint.tla` with `OrphanOps.tla`: `TableBound`, `Conserved`, `Handed`, `QueueBound`, `Made` (`Complete`), on three case configs: `MbtEndpoint.cfg` (2 slots, ids 1 and 2 from sources 1 and 2, parts at 0 of lengths 1 and 2, at most 5 held, flights at 0 and 1001 ms, a timer at 1001 ms), `MbtEndpoint_life.cfg` (2 slots, 3 ids, flights at 0, 30001 ms and `u64::MAX`, timers at 30000 ms, 30001 ms and `u64::MAX`, stale-id calls and strays), `MbtEndpoint_stall.cfg` (1 slot, the queue at 0, 1, 2K + 960 and 2K + 961 events, timers held by the stall); witnesses `MbtEndpoint_w` (two holders of one id, the second made by a held part, after an eviction), `MbtEndpoint_life_w` (a session made at `u64::MAX`), `MbtEndpoint_stall_w` (a session past its end kept by a stalled queue) | none | forged first flights only (a session that answers or completes a handshake is the transport's; its timers and its routing ids are not modelled, so the case of the finding at `u64::MAX` is not reached); the demux table never full (`D = 10K`, documented as needed, not checked); the generation of a slot does not wrap (2^31 reuses); the held-event ring (`HELD_EVENTS`), the access gate and the keepalive of an established session are not driven here | model-based test (`tests/mbt_endpoint.rs`): a real endpoint of 2 slots (1 for the stall file) driven along every edge of the three graphs, comparing after every call the sessions (time, id, source), their heap deadlines and routing entries, the free slots, the counters, the held parts entry by entry, the event queue and `poll_timeout` with the model, and every refusal of the stale-id calls with an unchanged snapshot |
| the keepalive plan applied: a connected session that is not closing gets, at every settle, the plan of `Inputs::plan` on what it read; when the plan changed the connection gets the interval ping minus last send (none in a pulse or without a ping), and outside a pulse the interval it holds, counted from its last send, ends at the plan's ping, while in a pulse it holds none (the endpoint sends no PING itself); the session's deadline is the earliest of its connection's timer, the review moment, a later ping in a pulse, the earliest standby ping, the access recheck and, unauthenticated, the end of its life; a ping at or before now is reported due in a pulse | `crates/pinc-core/src/timers.rs` (`settle_core`), `commands.rs` (`SetPulse`, `Pulse`), `pinc-transport` `conn/life.rs` (`set_keepalive`) | `Settle.tla` with `KeepaliveOps.tla`: `Applied`, `PulseOff` (`Inv`); `Settle.cfg` every role, side and path kind but a quiet dialing session on a direct path, calls at 0, 10, 25 and 100 s, no quiet period (under 20000 states); `Settle_watch.cfg` (a hub session this node dialed on a direct path: a send at 30 s, a datagram heard at 40 s, an acknowledgement at 40 s; `Applied` holds with the interval handed at every settle (fix C4; before it the PING fell 10 s late)) and `Settle_held.cfg` (a plain session in a 50 s quiet period: an acknowledgement at 10 s; `Applied` holds with the interval handed at every settle (fix C4; before it the PING fell 10 s late)) | none | every input that moves last send, last receive, the flight or the application data is followed by a settle at the same time (as the code does); the connection's PING timer is last send plus the interval (`conn/timer.rs`); the standby pings and the access recheck are inputs (`standby_due` is the keepalive row's); times in whole seconds | `TraceSettle.tla` (`PlanOK`, `AppliedOK` per session, `DeadlineOK`, `DueOK`) over every settle of a connected session recorded by `tests/trace_settle.rs`: pairs of real endpoints in every role, with silence, writes, pulses, a resume's quiet period, a close and 16 seeded random runs |
| the datagrams out: sessions wait on two queues, ordinary first, broadcast after (a broadcast session with a sub-session open still to leave counts as ordinary), each at most once; a session joins the queue its class names, and the session served moves to the back of the queue of its class; a session waiting in the broadcast queue that becomes ordinary (a sub-session open queued, the gossip flag cleared) moves to the back of the ordinary queue at once, and one waiting in the ordinary queue that becomes broadcast moves at its next turn; a call returns one destination and a run of equal-size datagrams, the last possibly shorter, at most `max_gso` of them, fitting the caller's buffer; no datagram leaves without passing the ledger, which is charged its bytes plus 28 per datagram; a datagram for another destination or size is kept and is the first thing of the next call, which drops it when its path has no address or the buffer is too short | `Endpoint::poll_transmit`, `gate`, `send_stash`, `batch` (`tx.rs`); each call is replayed step by step: the session asked, the window, the size sent to the ledger, the join, the kept datagram, the queue moves and the result |

### dag

| Property | Rust item | TLA+ | Lean theorem | Assumptions | Trace validation |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| block refusals and tolerances: trailing bytes, no delta, unordered or duplicate heads and links, an invalid CID, links not an array, a link name not text, a link without link or name, a non-canonical map, nesting deeper than 64 each get their own error; an unknown key, a null or empty heads or links and a null signature are accepted | `BlockRef::parse`, `BlockError` | `DagBlock.tla`: `StrictOK` (32 hand-built blocks); witness `DagBlock_loose.cfg` (`LooseRoundTrip` violated: a tolerated block does not re-encode byte for byte, as `block/mod.rs` documents) | none | as above | `TraceDagBlock.tla` |

| the topic event queue holds at most `EVENTS` (1024); events come out in the order pushed, each once; every event pushed is delivered, queued or dropped; a push to a full queue drops the newest and counts it; the node's own losses join the count; the count comes out as `Event::Lost` before any further event, exactly while fewer than 2^32 - 1 drops are owed (it saturates there: witness); a poll ends only when the endpoint stopped with nothing queued or owed, and waits only when it did not | `crates/pinc/src/engine/topics.rs` `push_event`, `poll_event`, `on_node_event` (`NodeEvent::Message`, `NodeEvent::Lost`), `EVENTS` | `FacadeEvents.tla` over `FacadeEventsOps.tla`: `Bounded`, `InOrder`, `Conserved`, `CountExact`, `EndOnlyStopped`, `NewestDropped`, `GapFirst` (2 places for 1024, count saturating at 1000, node losses of 1 or 2, 6 calls; estimated under 2,000 states, not measured); witnesses `FacadeEvents_full` (`NeverDropped` fails), `FacadeEvents_sat` (count saturating at 1: `CountExact` fails) | none | one thread drives the core; the Lost event comes before every queued event, also those queued before the drop, so a reader cannot place the gap (the doc's "before the next event" is met) | `TraceFacadeEvents.tla`: unit test `engine::event_tests::trace_events`: fill past 1024 and drain, a node loss while events are queued, the count saturated at `u32::MAX`, a stop with events left and a push after it, 4 seeds of 3000 random calls; every answer and the queue (length, ends, count, the whole queue every 50th event) checked at EVENTS 1024; the test is not on this branch yet: the trace check waits for it |
| with `std` every endpoint of a thread takes the thread's one pool of handshake machine boxes, so a handshake reuses a box another endpoint returned and the idle boxes of the thread stay at most `MACHINE_BOXES` however many endpoints it runs; without `std` each endpoint has its own pool and a box never crosses endpoints; in both a box is in one place and a handshake allocates only when its pool is empty | `crates/pinc/src/node.rs` `machines()`, `AnyEp::new`; `pinc-core` `Endpoint::share_machines` | `FacadeMachines.tla` (pool steps from `Machines.tla` by instance): `TypeOK`, `OneHolder`, `OwnPool`, `IdleBound`, `AllocOnlyEmpty` (two endpoints, 1 cell per pool for the 64, 2 handshakes each at once, up to 4 boxes; `FacadeMachines` with std, `FacadeMachines_own` without; estimated under 6,000 states each, not measured); witness `FacadeMachines_cross` (`NoCross` fails: with std a handshake takes a box the other endpoint allocated) | none | one thread per endpoint (the pool is `Rc`); the std thread-local is a distinct pool per thread (std semantics, not traced across threads) | `TraceFacadeMachines.tla`: unit test `node::tests::trace_facade_machines` in the builds with and without `std`, two threads of four endpoints each over rungs 1, 4, 64 built by `AnyEp::new`, each endpoint's pool compared with the thread's handle and the earlier endpoints' through the probes `Endpoint::machines` and `Machines::shares`; the pool's steps are `TraceMachines`; the test is not on this branch yet: the trace check waits for it |

### node

| the backoff of the node's delay-tolerant dials: the table keeps at most 4096 entries (`TABLE_MAX`), one per peer, and a full table refuses an entry for a peer it holds none for and changes nothing; a failed dial waits 2 s the first time and then twice the wait of its entry up to 60 s; the entry ends the wait at the time of the failure plus the wait, saturating at `u64::MAX`; the layer's redial time becomes the earlier of itself and the end of the wait, also when the entry was refused; a dial waits while `now < until`; a membership event about the peer and a session to it that lived 5 s forget the entry | `crates/pinc-node/src/io.rs` (`Io::dtn_failed`, `dtn_waits`, `dtn_forget`), `layer.rs` (`dial_layer`, `layer_failed`, `layer_closed`), `node.rs` (`service`), `dtn.rs` (`sooner`, `redial_due`, `dtn_timeout`) | `NodeDtnBackoff.tla` with `NodeDtnBackoffOps.tla` (the wait and table step of `NodeBackoffOps.tla` without the hub cap): `Bounded`, `WaitBounds`, `FailRight` (every failure checked against the rule on exact words), `OwedHasEntry`; 2 peers, a table of 1 entry, times 0, 1 s, 2 s, `u64::MAX` that never go back; under 10,000 states; witnesses `NodeDtnBackoff_w_full` (the full table refuses), `NodeDtnBackoff_w_cap` (a wait reaches 60 s), `NodeDtnBackoff_w_sat` (an end saturates) | none | which peers the contacts want dialed is not modelled: every peer is taken to be one the layer wants dialed; the 5 s lifetime test of a session is the rule of `NodeBackoff.tla` and is abstracted to a step | `TraceNodeDtnBackoff.tla`: 56 runs and 20,762 events of a real table (`Io` over an endpoint that does nothing, the probe `DtnBackoffProbe`, cargo feature `trace`) and the layer's redial time: doubling to the cap, forgetting, the top of the clock, a full table of 4096 and seeded random runs; every failure, window check, forget and timer equal to the model's, and dumps of the whole table |
| every delay-tolerant peer that failed and still waits has the layer's redial time at or before the end of its wait, so it is redialed when its backoff ends (`layer.rs:154`, `dtn.rs:220`, `dtn_deadline`) | `crates/pinc-node/src/dtn.rs` (`redial_at`, `dtn_timeout`), `layer.rs` (`layer_failed`, `dial_layer`) | `NodeDtnBackoff.tla`, `NodeDtnBackoff_redial.cfg`: `NoLostRedial`, same bounds. **Finding, open:** the code keeps one redial time and clears it when it fires; a peer whose wait ends later is skipped by the look and no redial time is set for it; TLC reports the violation with a failure of a peer at 0 and again at 1 s, then the timer at 2 s | none | as above | the trace runs the case (run 2 of the test) and checks each call; the cross-call property is the model's |
| the resume records of the delay-tolerant layer: each record written reads back as written; a received record is 10 bytes and a held one 48 bytes plus its head; a record is read only whole: another version or kind, a proper prefix, a byte too many, a custody byte other than 0 or 1, an unknown algorithm or a head that does not decode and verify is refused; a partial record's runs are refused when one starts at or after its end, ends past the bundle, or is not on slices, and otherwise taken up to the first run cut short, whose bytes are cut off, so a partial record torn by a crash is resumed from its whole runs and its tail rewritten | `crates/pinc-node/src/dtn_save.rs` (`read_record`, `received_record`, `held_record`, `partial_head`, `put_run`, `check_runs`, `take_runs`, `restore_one`) | `NodeDtnCodec.tla` with `NodeDtnCodecOps.tla`, `NodeDtnCodec_record.cfg`: `RoundTrip`, `Sizes`, `Unique`, `Strict`, `Whole`, `PartialSafe` over every record of two heads of 1 and 3 bytes, the edge values of each field, every proper prefix and every changed field (about 800 states); `NodeDtnCodec_runs.cfg`: `RunsSpec`, `CheckSpec`, `CheckThenTake`, `CutWhole` over a bundle of 4 bytes in slices of 2, runs of every header in 0..5 and `u64::MAX`, alone, after a good run and cut at every byte (about 1,100 states); witness `NodeDtnCodec_agree` (`Agree`: the finding) | none | signatures are ideal and the head codec is canonical: the heads that decode and verify are a given set (in the trace, the heads the test made); bundle sizes are parameters | `TraceNodeDtnCodec.tla`: about 12,000 events of the real writers and readers (the probe `dtn_save_probe`, cargo feature `trace`) over heads of the four signature algorithms: round trips of every field's edge values, every proper prefix, every byte changed, run headers at every slice edge alone, after a good run and torn, and seeded random bytes; every result equal to the model's |
| the frames of the delay-tolerant layer: a frame is its type, its body length as 4 big-endian bytes (saturating at `u32::MAX`) and the body; the reader takes a frame only when it is whole and returns its type and whole length, never more than the input; it waits on a cut frame and refuses an announced body over `BODY_MAX` (1 MiB); the integer readers return the big-endian value and the rest, or nothing when a byte is short | `crates/pinc-node/src/dtn_wire.rs` (`start`, `finish`, `next`, `take_u64`, `take_u16`) | `NodeDtnCodec.tla`, `NodeDtnCodec_frame.cfg`: `FrameOK`, `FrameWait`, `FrameBreak`, `NeverPast`, `TakeRight` over the types 0, 1, 255, bodies of 0, 1, `BODY_MAX` and one more, `u32::MAX` and one more, cut at every header byte and followed by more bytes (about 350 states) | none | the bodies over `u32::MAX` exist in the model only | `TraceNodeDtnCodec.tla`, same run: frames of every type and boundary length, whole, cut and followed by more bytes, hand-made headers of 2^24, 2^31 and 2^32 - 1, and random inputs to the integer readers |
| the node's queue of application events: it never holds more than 8192 events (`EVENT_CAP`) and keeps them in arrival order; below the cap nothing is dropped; at the cap exactly one event of the queued ones and the new one is dropped, the others keep their order, and the dropped one is one the application can recover by reading state unless none is; every drop is counted (saturating at `u32::MAX`) and the count is delivered as `Lost` before the next event, then reset; a read otherwise delivers and removes the oldest event; `is_empty` exactly when a read delivers nothing; the node takes no endpoint event from 7168 queued (`EVENT_HIGH`) until the queue is back to 4096 (`EVENT_LOW`), and endpoint events alone never fill the queue | `crates/pinc-node/src/io.rs` (`EventQueue::push`, `pop`, `can_take`, `resumable`, `is_empty`, `Extend`), `event.rs` | `NodeEventQueue.tla` with `NodeEventQueueOps.tla`: `Bounded`, `Ordered`, `DropRule`, `LostCount`, `PopRight`, `EmptyRight`, `TakeRight`, `ResumeRight`, `RoomForEndpoint`, each step checked against the rule restated on a history; a queue of 3 that stops at 2 and resumes at 1, a lost count saturating at 1, 5 arrivals from layers and from the endpoint in any order with reads; a few thousand states; witnesses `NodeEventQueue_w_kept` (an unrecoverable event is dropped when nothing recoverable is queued), `NodeEventQueue_w_sat` (more drops than the count holds), `NodeEventQueue_w_stop` (the node stops taking endpoint events) | none | the events are their recoverability and an id; which layer emits what is not modelled; the sizes are parameters in TLC and the real numbers in the trace | `TraceNodeEventQueue.tla`: about 54 runs and 40,000 events of a real `EventQueue` (the probe `EventQueueProbe`, cargo feature `trace`): small random runs, fills to the cap and past it, full queues with and without recoverable events, the stop and resume edges, and seeded random runs across 4096 to 8192; every call, length and lost count equal to the model's (the `u32::MAX` saturation is reached in the model only) |
| the node's port-mapping client: the connectivity client holds exactly the address the port-mapping client holds after every call; a node whose host never turned port mapping on sends nothing for it and arms no timer; a stopped client with a release unsent or unanswered is the client or in the queue of `RETIRING` (4), whose releases go out before the new client asks; a full queue replaces one release, counted once; a stop before any nonce starts nothing later; a failed draw is retried `NONCE_RETRY` later, saturating at the clock's maximum. **Known limitation (finding):** a full queue replaces slot 0, not the oldest release as documented (`NodePortMap_woldest`) | `crates/pinc-node/src/portmap.rs` (`start_port_mapping`, `pm_seed`, `stop_port_mapping`, `pm_restart`, `pm_datagram`, `pm_timeout`, `pm_poll_udp`, `handle_http`, `pm_apply`, `Pm::sending`, `Pm::reap`) | `NodePortMap.tla` `Inv` (`ConnHolds`, `Kept`, `Slots`) and `StepProps` (`NoPmNothing`, `ReleaseFirst`, `HaltedStays`, `Counted`, `RetrySat`); `NodePortMap`: 2 retiring slots, 4 clients, at most 2 failed draws per start, times 0 and the maximum (not run yet, state counts not measured); witnesses `NodePortMap_overflow`, `NodePortMap_sat`; finding `NodePortMap_woldest` (5 clients) | none | the client's phases (asking, holding, release unsent, sent, done) are pinc-portmap's and free here; the protocol bytes and the HTTP carrier are abstract; a client's address is its own | `TraceNodePortMap.tla`: every call of a boundary script that overflows the queue and of 40 seeds of 80 calls at times up to `u64::MAX`, checked state by state and step by step; a failed draw of the nonce (and so a halted client) is not reached, the node's random source does not fail |
| the layer tables of the reconcile layer and its rounds over real sessions: the layer session table holds at most 4096 (`TABLE_MAX`) sessions and the dials in flight at most 4096, a session is one layer entry, the session we dialed per peer is one outgoing entry of the table; every layer session is a session the endpoint reported, and every answered stream and every round's stream is on one, so a closed session leaves nothing of the layers behind; no layer session comes up for a revoked peer or on a leaving node, and the layer sessions of either are being closed; at most `ROUNDS` (8) rounds and `RESPONDERS` (16) answered streams, a full table refuses (`Busy`, a reset with the BUSY code); a round spends at most `ATTEMPTS` (4) attempts (a stream opened, a failed dial) and fails `Exhausted` only after them, a stream the endpoint cannot give yet is retried after `OPEN_RETRY` (saturating at the clock's maximum) and not counted; a round that left the table ended with one event, or was cancelled and has none. **Known limitations (findings):** a round whose dial's session comes up into a full layer table (or for a revoked peer, or on a leaving node, when the endpoint still reports it up) is never told and holds its round slot with no event (`NodeSets_way`); a round of a revoked peer or of a leaving node ends `Exhausted`, never `Cancelled` as `ReconcileFailure::Cancelled` documents (`NodeSets_cancel`), by two paths: a dial of ours that fails after the revoke (`ConnectFailed`, `layer_failed`) and a round on its last attempt whose session closes after it (`sets_down`, `round_retry`) | `crates/pinc-node/src/reconcile.rs` (`sets_start`, `sets_cancel`, `sets_timeout`, `sets_up`, `sets_failed`, `sets_down`, `sets_stream_opened`, `sets_stream_gone`, `round_begin`, `round_retry`, `round_readable`), `layer.rs` (`dial_layer`, `layer_up`, `layer_failed`, `layer_closed`, `layer_session`), `node.rs` (`revoke`, `leave`, `handle_timeout`, the service loop of `settle_with`) | `NodeSets.tla`: `Inv` (`Bounds`, `OneLayer`, `LoutSound`, `LiveRefs`, `GoneClosing`, `Accounted`, `WaitingOK`) and `StepProps` (`NoNewForGone`, `OpenAtSat`); a fetch the full provider table refuses takes nothing and is counted; a stream refused by either full table gives its use back and changes nothing; a fetch done as it starts leaves the tables as they were; `next_fetch_id` is never 0 or a running id and takes at most the running ids plus two steps | `crates/pinc-node/src/values_net.rs` (`Net::acquire`, `hold`, `release`, `acquire_all`, `begin_fetch`, `end_fetch`, `add`, `add_for`, `drop_stream`, `next_fetch_id`), `values.rs` (`start_fetch`, `values_stream_opened`, `open_stream` as the callers) | `NodeValuesNet.tla` with `NodeValuesNetOps.tla`: `Inv` (`Bounded`, `Trimmed`, `OnePeer`, `Exact`, `ByPeak`) and `StepProps` (`KeepsPeer`, `FirstFree`, `GrowsOnlyFull`, `RefusedWhole`, `GivesBack`), `ASSUME NextIdOK`; tables of 2, 3 peers, fetch ids 1 and 2 with lists of 1 or 2 peers, at most 3 streams added, ids over two limbs of 3 values (not run yet, estimated under 20,000 states); witnesses `NodeValuesNet_refused` (`NeverRefused`), `NodeValuesNet_sfull` (`NeverStreamFull`), `NodeValuesNet_hole` (`NoHole`: the table is longer than the providers in use) | none | ideal crypto not involved; a use count never nears `u32::MAX` (its holders are bounded by the tables), so the saturating add is a plain add; `TABLE_MAX` is 2 in TLC and in the trace a window of 2 past 4094 fillers that are never given back | `TraceNodeValuesNet.tla` over `tests/trace_node_values_net.rs`: a real `Net` through `values_net::probe` (feature `trace`), a boundary script and 40 seeded runs of 60 calls, every call and the window's slots, streams, lists, refusals and stream number checked against the operators; `next_fetch_id` on u32 limbs around 0 and the wrap and 200 seeded cases |
| the roots a node serves: at most `PROVIDED_MAX` (4096); a provide answers true exactly when the root is registered afterwards (already there, or room left) and false, changing nothing, when 4096 others are; an unprovide answers whether it was registered | `crates/pinc-node/src/values_provide.rs` (`Node::values_provide`, `values_unprovide`) | `NodeProvided.tla` (operators in `NodeValuesNetOps.tla`): `Bounded`, `StepProps` (`ProvideAnswer`, `UnprovideAnswer`); a cap of 2 and 3 roots (7 sets); witness `NodeProvided_full` (`NeverRefused`) | none | the values layer on (off answers false, not modelled) | `TraceNodeValuesNet.tla`: a real node over the scripted endpoint with 4094 filler roots, a boundary script and 100 seeded calls, every answer checked |
