| Property | Rust item | TLA+ | Lean theorem | Assumptions | Trace validation |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| port mapping (`pinc-portmap`): the shared range is exactly 100.64.0.0/10, and the private ranges are exactly those of RFC 1918 and the shared one | `addr::is_shared`, `is_private` | `PortMapAddr.tla` SharedRule, PrivateRule (`.cfg`: 2430 IPv4 addresses on both sides of every boundary, each also IPv4-mapped, 70 IPv6; about 4930 states) | - | the core library's `Ipv4Addr` predicates as core defines them | `TracePortMapAddr.tla` over `trace_portmap_addr` (the boundary set and 8192 seeded addresses) |

| an external address is a usable, non-private IPv4 address, or an IPv6 address outside ::, ::1, ff00::/8, fe80::/10, fc00::/7, and an IPv4-mapped one is judged as the IPv4 it maps | `addr::external_ok` | `PortMapAddr.tla` ExternalRule, MappedSame (`.cfg`); `_mapped.cfg` witness NoMappedRefused: a mapped address the IPv6 rules alone accept is refused by its IPv4 class | - | `IpAddr::to_canonical` as core defines it | as above |
| port mapping (`pinc-portmap`): with a gateway known the client asks first with PCP, but from an unspecified socket with NAT-PMP; with none known it searches for a UPnP gateway, and only from a private IPv4 socket, else asks nothing; PCP is used only with a gateway and a specified socket, NAT-PMP only with a gateway, UPnP only from a private IPv4 socket | `PortMap::new`, `first`, `searchable` | `PortMapFallback.tla` FirstStep, Allowed (`.cfg`: every gateway and socket class, every answer kind of each protocol from every state, up to two failed renewals per lease; about 3300 states) | - | time abstracted to the next deadline, a lease expiry as its own call (header of `PortMapFallbackOps.tla`); the gateway's epoch consistent (restarts are `PortMapLease.tla`'s) | `MbtPortMapFallback.tla` cases run by `mbt_portmap_fallback` (every edge of its graph, about 1300 states and 35,000 edges) |
| no call but stop, restart and a lease's expiry takes an asking client back to an earlier protocol or request (PCP, NAT-PMP address, NAT-PMP map, search, description, external address, AddPortMapping); a protocol that gives no mapping hands on to the next documented one (PCP to NAT-PMP, NAT-PMP to the search when it may search, else to the retry; UPnP to the retry), and the retry starts again from the first | `fail`, `pcp_answer`, `natpmp_answer`, `handle_datagram`, `handle_http`, `handle_timeout` | `PortMapFallback.tla` Order, FallbackNext, IdleRetry (`.cfg`); `_full.cfg` witness NoUpnpWithGateway: a UPnP mapping is held with a gateway known, so the whole chain PCP, NAT-PMP, UPnP is reachable | - | as above | as above |
| a renewal asks with the protocol that granted the mapping, and one that fails goes back to holding it | `renew`, `fail`, `grant` | `PortMapFallback.tla` Renewal (`.cfg`) | - | as above | as above |
| an external address no internet peer can reach is not held (PCP, NAT-PMP, UPnP's external address) | `acceptable`, `addr::external_ok` | `PortMapFallback.tla` PrivateRefused (`.cfg`) | - | as above | as above |
| after stop no mapping is held and nothing is asked again; stop releases the mapping held or the request in flight that may have made one (UPnP by DeletePortMapping), and restart releases a PCP or NAT-PMP mapping held | `stop`, `restart`, `in_flight`, `queue_release`, `poll_datagram` | `PortMapFallback.tla` StopFinal, StopReleases (`.cfg`, both release slots free to fill) | - | as above | as above |
| an answer changes the client only when it answers the request in flight (PCP by nonce, protocol and port; NAT-PMP by opcode and port; a search answer only while searching; HTTP only when sent), and a PCP or NAT-PMP answer that does not is counted | `owns`, `handle_datagram`, `pcp_answer`, `natpmp_answer`, `handle_http` | `PortMapFallback.tla` AnswerScope (`.cfg`) | - | as above | as above |
| port mapping (`pinc-portmap`): the bounded writer never writes past its buffer; on an overflow it reports `None` and writes nothing more, a later put that would fit included; without one it reports exactly the bytes put, in order | `text::Out::put`, `done` | `PortMapText.tla` WriterOK (`.cfg`: every capacity 0 to 6 with every run of up to 4 puts of 0 to 4 bytes, 5467 runs; about 9800 states in all); `_full.cfg` witness NoLaterFit: a run where a put after the overflow would have fitted on its own | - | slice copy and `get_mut` as core defines them | `TracePortMapText.tla` over `trace_portmap_text` (the boundary runs and 64 seeded runs up to 128 bytes) |
| a number is written in decimal, without leading zeros, and parses back to itself; a dotted IPv4 address the same | `text::Out::dec`, `ip4`; `parse_u32`, `parse_ip4` | `PortMapText.tla` DecOK, RoundTrip, Ip4OK, Ip4Trip (`.cfg`: 0 to 999, every power of two and ten and its neighbours, `u32::MAX`, into capacities 0 to 11) | - | as above | as above |

### security

| Property | Rust item | TLA+ | Lean theorem | Assumptions | Trace validation |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| a store from entropy draws every byte (the sealing key first) from a generator seeded with it, so it is a function of its entropy and its calls; `reseed` mixes entropy into such a store and a store on the operating system ignores it; `refresh` calls `fresh` once on a generator store and mixes the bytes in exactly when it returned true, and never calls it on an operating-system store; the sealing key is fixed at creation (a reseed does not change it) and never repeats as an output; no output repeats | `crates/pinc-keystore/src/software.rs` `Source`, `SoftwareStore::{new, from_entropy, reseed}`, `KeyStore::{random, seal, unseal, refresh}` for `SoftwareStore`; the trait default `refresh` (lib.rs) | `KeystoreRefresh.tla` (generator from `CryptoDrbg.tla`): `OsNeverAsked`, `SealNotOutput`, `NoRepeat`, `SameSeed`, `RefreshRule`, `ReseedRule`, `SealFixed` (stores from 2 seeds and one on the operating system, every sequence of 3 calls among random, 3 reseeds and 4 refresh answers; estimated about 1,800 states, not measured); witness `KeystoreRefresh_mix` (`NeverReseeded` fails) | none | ChaCha20 ideal (CryptoDrbg's abstraction: a key's stream is a fresh term); the operating system's randomness is fresh; the refused fill over `MAX_FILL` is CryptoDrbg's | `TraceKeystoreRefresh.tla`: `tests/trace_keystore_refresh.rs`: 1024 runs from entropy and 512 on the operating system over every sequence of 3 calls, reference fills of `Drbg::new`, seal and unseal across stores; the bytes are checked to be a function of the model's terms and injective (by cardinality), and each run's `fresh` count; plus a unit test in `software.rs` in both builds of the crate; the test is not in the tree yet: the trace check is not run |
| the TPM store's object templates: the storage primary is a restricted decryption ECC P-256 key with AES-128-CFB and no scheme, the signing key an unrestricted ECDSA-SHA256 P-256 key, the sealed object a keyed hash with no sign, decrypt or restricted bit and no sensitive data origin; every object is fixedTPM and fixedParent, none has a policy (empty authPolicy, userWithAuth, no adminWithPolicy): the password is the only gate; each template is one well formed TPM2B_PUBLIC (its size counts the rest, a parse of the TPM layout consumes every byte), SHA-256 names, fixed sizes 28, 26 and 16 bytes, and no two kinds share one | `pinc_keystore::tpm::template::{storage_primary, p256_signing_key, sealed_data}` (`tpm/template.rs`), sent by `TpmClient::create_primary` (`client.rs`), `TpmStore::generate` and `seal` (`store.rs`) | `KeystoreTemplate.tla`: `WellFormed`, `TypeOK`, `Fixed`, `NoPolicy`, `Role`, `Sizes`, `Distinct` (`KeystoreTemplate.cfg`, the three kinds, 3 states); witness `KeystoreTemplate_nofixed` (the sealed object without fixedParent: `Fixed`) | none | the TPM device and its transport; the bytes of a template are written by the protocol library from the store's structures, the model writes them from the field layout of TPM 2.0 Part 2 | `TraceKeystoreTemplate.tla`: the inPublic of every TPM2_CreatePrimary and TPM2_Create the real `TpmStore` sent to a software TPM (three auth pairs, opens, P-256 keys, seals; at least 18 commands) is exactly the model's template of its kind, the library unmarshals it whole, and every property holds of the bytes as sent |
| the output queue of the handshake machines: it holds at most one message (the last flight, kept to send again), one outstanding key request and one of each event; `poll` hands out what is queued in the fixed order key request, message, early keys, resume rejected, established, failure, each once; a request set again is announced again and a taken one is gone; `resend` queues the kept message again with its padding floor; a failure discards everything else queued (`pending` is then none); a refused `build` keeps the length and send flag of the message before it while the bytes its closure wrote stay in the buffer, so the kept message is torn (witness; no caller can make `build` fail, and the machines fail fatally on it, which clears the send flag, and never resend once failed) | `outbox.rs`: `Outbox` (`build`, `clear_message`, `resend`, `set_request`, `pending`, `take_request`, `set_resume_rejected`, `fail`, `poll`, the fields `early` and `established`) | `Outbox.tla` over `OutboxOps.tla`: `TypeOK`, `Agrees` (the code's queue, request and buffer are the contract's), `PollAgrees` (`Outbox.cfg`: messages of 1 and 2 bytes, two padding floors, two requests, two early keys, one established, two failures, every sequence of calls, under 16000 states); witnesses `Outbox_torn.cfg` (a refused build that wrote 1 byte: `Agrees` fails), `Outbox_full.cfg` (every kind queued at once: `NotAllQueued` fails) | none | messages and events are marks; the bytes of a message are its mark (the codec is `MessageCodec`); the module doc does not place the resume-rejected event in its order, the code's place is the one checked | `mbt_outbox`: the real queue through the `trace` probe `OutboxProbe` along every edge of `MbtOutbox.cfg` (at most 9216 states, refused builds of 0 and 1 byte included), compared after every call; and 16 seeded runs of 2000 calls against the documented order |
| identifiers and parameters of the handshake: a connection id is accepted at 0 to 20 bytes and an ALPN at 1 to 32, nothing else, and gives its bytes back; two of them are equal exactly when their bytes are; an ALPN list holds 1 to 9 valid ALPNs and `find` answers the one equal to the ALPN wanted, nothing when none is; the AEAD tags are 1 and 2 and nothing else; an AEAD set is accepted only at bits 1 to 3 (never empty, no unknown bit), `contains` is the bit of the tag, the offer is ChaCha20-Poly1305 alone or with AES-128-GCM exactly on hardware AES; `choose` answers an AEAD of both sets, AES-128-GCM when both hold it, nothing exactly when they share none, either way round; a local identity is made exactly from a key of its algorithm's length and gives back its algorithm, key and key handle, and an Ed25519 node id is the key | `ids.rs`: `ConnId`, `Alpn`, `AlpnList`, `aead_tag`, `aead_from_tag`, `AeadSet`, `LocalIdentity` | `SecIds.tla`: `CidRange`, `AlpnRange`, `ListRule`, `TagRoundTrip`, `SetRule`, `ChooseRule`, `IdentRule` (`SecIds.cfg`: one state; lengths 0 to 40, 255 and 256, every byte value, every pair of sets, about 170 ALPN lists of 0 to 11 items, key lengths around each algorithm's) | none | the node id of a key other than Ed25519 is a BLAKE3 derivation (an ideal primitive), compared in the trace with `NodeId::from_public` | `trace_sec_ids` (about 2600 calls on the boundaries and seeded random inputs), `TraceSecIds.tla` on all of them |
| software prekey rotation: the store holds one prekey; `acquire` rotates it first when it has served `max_uses` handshakes or its age (saturating, so a clock that steps back gives 0) has reached `lifetime_ns`, the new handle being the old one plus one, and then counts the use, so the prekey handed out is younger than the lifetime or new and has served at most `max_uses` handshakes (one when `max_uses` is 0); the handle never goes down; a rotated-out handle is refused by `decapsulate` (`InvalidSecretKey`), never answered with the current key; after 2^64 rotations the wrapping handle would name the current key again (witness at 2 handles; a store starts at 0 and rotates at most once per handshake) | `keys.rs`: `SoftPrekeys` (`new`, `acquire`, `decapsulate`, `handle`, `uses`), `PrekeyPolicy` | `SoftPrekeys.tla` over `PrekeyRotation.tla` (`SoftAcquire`): `TypeOK`, `HeldOne`, `ServesAtMost`, `FreshEnough`, `RotatedOutRefused`, `GenerationsIncrease` (`SoftPrekeys.cfg`: lifetime 2, 2 uses, acquires at times 0 to 3, generations up to 4, about 300 states); witnesses `SoftPrekeys_wrap.cfg` (2 handles: `RotatedOutRefused` fails), `SoftPrekeys_rot.cfg` (a rotation happens: `NeverRotated` fails) | none | ML-KEM key generation, encapsulation and decapsulation are ideal; a refusal of the generator changes nothing and cannot happen with the real generator (it refuses only above 2^30 bytes), so it is not in the graph | `mbt_soft_prekeys`: the real store along every edge of `MbtSoftPrekeys.cfg` (lifetime 2 ns, 2 uses) and `MbtSoftPrekeys_zero.cfg` (lifetime 0, 0 uses), at time origins 0 and near `u64::MAX`, handle and use count compared after every call and every decapsulation answered with the encapsulated secret or refused as the model says; and 8 seeds of 25 policies (lifetimes 0 to `u64::MAX`, uses 0 to `u32::MAX`) of 300 calls against the documented rule |
| the TPM store's object slots: when the TPM has no room for an object a call needs, the store retries the flushes it was refused once, then unloads its least recently used loaded key the call does not use (the next one when the TPM refuses the flush) and sends the command again, and loads an unloaded key again from its wrapped blob on its next use, after retrying the refused flushes; `persist` records the key persistent once EvictControl ran and answers Ok when the flush of its transient copy is refused or lost (the handle is remembered for a retry); a key recorded as loaded is the TPM's object at its handle, a number on the stale list never names a live object (`adopt`), TpmFull only when no other loaded key is left, a refused unload is the error only when no other loaded key is left, `persist` answers Ok exactly when the key is persistent, a call sends its command at most the loaded keys plus two times, and after the store is dropped the TPM holds only other users' objects and those whose flush was refused (the slot fix, written before the code lands) | `TpmStore::{room, reclaim, discard, adopt, least_used, unload, handle_of, generate, load_persistent, persist, sign, seal, unseal}`, `Drop for TpmStore` | `KeystoreSlots.tla`: `Agree`, `StaleNotLive`, `LeakedOnStale`, `NoFalseFull`, `NoFalseRefusal`, `PersistTruthful`, `Usable`, `Bounded`, `NoLeakOnDrop` (`KeystoreSlots`: three slots, two keys, handles 1 to 4, flushes refused or their answers lost; `KeystoreSlots_persist`: one slot held by another user, persistent keys, flushes refused or their answers lost); witnesses `KeystoreSlots_w_unload` (`NeverUnloaded`: the unload is reached) and `KeystoreSlots_w_full` (`NeverFull`: two slots held by other users) | - | foreign objects that come and go are not modelled; the commands' bytes are `KeystoreTpm.tla`'s | none (the trace test of the TPM store records no call under pressure) |

### table

| Property | Rust item | TLA+ | Lean theorem | Assumptions | Trace validation |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| array by position: a place is present exactly after a `set` of it and before a `remove` of it, and `get` returns the record last set there; `set` is `Position` for a place past the end before `Length` for a record of the wrong length, and otherwise returns whether the place was present; `remove` returns whether it was; `len` is the number of present places; `set_order` is `Length` for a key slice not one key a place, and otherwise makes `order` the places in key order, equal keys in place order (a stable sort), always a permutation; `iter` is `order` filtered to the present places with their records; a refused call changes nothing | `crates/pinc-table/src/array.rs` (`Array::set`, `remove`, `get`, `set_order`, `order`, `iter`, `len`) | `TableArray.tla` with `TableArrayOps.tla`: `TypeOK`, `PresentOK`, `CountOK`, `OrderPerm`, `OrderOK`, `IterOK`, `ResultOK`, `RefusedUnchanged` (3 places, values 1..2, a record of the wrong length, the place past the end, every key vector over 0..1 and one of 4 keys; about 9,000 states); witness `TableArray_full` (every place present: `NotFull` fails) | none | records are values, not bytes (the code copies a record whole, so a record's bytes are one value); the refusals of `new` are `TableBytes.tla`'s; `get_mut` is not modelled; TLC runs 3 places | `TraceTableArray.tla`: 50 seeded runs of real arrays of 0, 1, 2, 3, 7 and 64 places (records of 2 bytes), each filled then driven by random sets, removals and key vectors (ties, distinct keys, wrong lengths), places past the end; the result and every place, the order and the walk after each call, recomputed by the model operators |
| block sizes: each table's `bytes` is exactly capacity times (record + the bytes each entry costs beside it: 3 for the array, 0 for the ring, 12 for the pool, the 2- or 4-byte index entry for the slab) when that fits a `usize`, and `usize::MAX` otherwise; `new` accepts exactly a capacity the table can number (array to 65,535 places, pool slots and slot size to `u32::MAX`, slab capacity to `u32::MAX` and key plus value below 2^bits) whose exact need is at most `isize::MAX`, over a block of at least that need, and otherwise is `Capacity`, or `Storage` with the exact need (never a saturated one); in an accepted table the regions tile the first `bytes` bytes of the block, the plain `+` and `*` the tables compute on their sizes do not overflow, and every slot or place number fits the entry that stores it; `Storage::zeroed` refuses exactly the lengths above `isize::MAX`; for a `usize` of 32 and of 64 bits | `crates/pinc-table/src/storage.rs` (`fit`, `no_block_holds`, `Storage::zeroed`); `Array::bytes`, `Array::new`; `Ring::bytes`, `Ring::new`; `Pool::bytes`, `Pool::new`; `SortedSlab::bytes`, `width`, `SortedSlab::new` | `TableBytes.tla` on `U64.tla`: `Inv` (`CaseOK`: bytes, `new` against the exact specification `SpecNew`, the layout; `Zeroed`); every table at 32 and 64 bits, capacities 2^j - 1, 2^j, 2^j + 1 for 13 values of j, record sizes at the powers of two and the sizes that put the need exactly on 2^(bits-1) and 2^bits, slab key and value lengths to `u64::MAX`, blocks of 0, need - 1, need, need + 1 and `isize::MAX` bytes; about 7,700 cases, one state each; witness `TableBytes_sat` (a capacity the table can number whose size does not fit: `NoSaturation` fails) | none | the block a table accepts may be longer than `bytes` (the tail is unused), as the code allows; numbers are 80-bit words of 16-bit limbs (a product is exact below 2^79); a `Storage::from_static` block is a length only; a refused `new` drops the block it was given | `TraceTableBytes.tla`: the real `bytes` and `new` of the four tables on the boundary sets at 64 bits and 1,000 seeded random cases, blocks of 0, need - 1, need and need + 1 bytes up to 1 MiB and 0 and 64 bytes above, and `Storage::zeroed` on refused and small lengths; about 15,000 calls, each against the code's operator and the exact specification; 64-bit hosts only |
| slot pool: `take` hands each slot out at most once until it is given back (a slot is out exactly when its generation is odd, and on the free stack, once, exactly when it is even), with nothing written to it, and is `None` exactly when every slot is out; free plus taken is the capacity (the count is conserved); `give`, `write` and `read` accept exactly a handle of the slot's current take (an odd generation, an index below the capacity, the slot's generation), so a second give-back, an old, a forged and an out-of-range handle are refused (`Stale`, `None`) until the slot has been taken 2^31 times since; `write` is `Length` for more bytes than a slot, writes a prefix and raises the written length to cover it; `read` is the written prefix; a refused call changes nothing | `crates/pinc-table/src/pool.rs` (`Pool::take`, `give`, `write`, `read`, `free`, `taken`; `Slot::from_bytes`) | `TablePool.tla` with `TablePoolOps.tla`: `TypeOK`, `StackOK`, `Conserved`, `ParityOK`, `AcceptOK`, `ReadOK`, `WrittenOK`, `ResultOK`, `TakeOK`, `RefusedUnchanged` (2 slots of 1 byte, generations modulo 4, each slot taken at most 3 times, every handle of index 0..2 and generation 0..3, data of 0..2 bytes; estimated 10,000 to 30,000 states); witnesses `TablePool_exhausted` (every slot out: `NotExhausted` fails) and `TablePool_wrap` (an old handle matches again after the generation wraps: `NoOldMatch` fails) | none | the generation wraps at 3 in TLC, at `u32::MAX` in the code (the parity argument needs only an even modulus); a give-back past the stack and the `Position` refusals of `write` cannot be reached and are checked as unreachable; the refusals of `new` are `TableBytes.tla`'s; the trace keeps generations below 2^31 - 1, so the wrap at `u32::MAX` is the code's unit test's | `TraceTablePool.tla`: 32 seeded runs of real pools of 0, 1, 2, 5 and 32 slots of 0 to 8 bytes: takes until exhausted and past it, give-backs of held, given-back, old, forged and out-of-range handles, writes of 0 bytes, a prefix, the whole slot and one byte too many, reads of every kind of handle; the result, the handle and the bytes read, and the free stack, every generation and written length (probes `trace_stack`, `trace_generation`, `trace_written`, feature `trace`) after each call, recomputed by the model operators |
| ring: records come out in the order they went in (FIFO), `get(i)` is the `i`-th oldest and `peek` the oldest; it never holds more than its capacity; a push to a full ring is `Full` and overwrites nothing (the place the next push writes holds no live record); a record of the wrong length is `Length` before `Full`; `pop` of an empty ring is `None`; `clear` empties it; the head and every live place stay inside the block; a refused call changes nothing | `crates/pinc-table/src/ring.rs` (`Ring::push`, `pop`, `peek`, `get`, `clear`, `is_full`, `len`) | `TableRing.tla` with `TableRingOps.tla`: `TypeOK`, `Bounded`, `HeadOK`, `FifoOK`, `GetOK`, `PeekOK`, `NextAt`, `NoOverwrite`, `AtInRange`, `FullOK`, `ResultOK`, `RefusedUnchanged` (capacities 0..3, values 1..2, a record of the wrong length; under 3,000 states); witness `TableRing_wrap` (a full ring whose oldest record is past place 0: `NotWrappedFull` fails) | none | records are values; the `Position` refusal of `push` (a place past the block) cannot be reached and is checked as unreachable; the refusals of `new` are `TableBytes.tla`'s; TLC runs capacities to 3 | `TraceTableRing.tla`: 48 seeded runs of real rings of capacity 0, 1, 2, 3, 7 and 64 (records of 2 bytes), pushes, pops, clears and bursts to full, records of the wrong length; the result, the head (probe `Ring::trace_head`, feature `trace`), the records, `peek` and `is_full` after each call, recomputed by the model operators |
| sorted slab: the index is a permutation of the slots holding records, in strictly ascending key order with no key twice, so `iter`, `entry`, `first`, `last`, `first_at_or_after` and `next_after` walk the records in key order; it holds at most its capacity; `insert` is `Length` for a key or value of the wrong length, replaces the value of a held key, is `Full` for a new key when full, and otherwise adds it; `remove` removes exactly the key named and keeps the records packed (the last record moves into the hole); `retain` calls the closure once per record in key order, keeps exactly the records it keeps with the values it set, returns the number removed and keeps the records packed; `clear` empties it; the slab is exactly the map of keys to values its calls describe; a refused call changes nothing | `crates/pinc-table/src/slab.rs` (`SortedSlab::insert`, `remove`, `retain`, `clear`, `search`, `entry`, `get`, `contains`, `first`, `last`, `first_at_or_after`, `next_after`, `iter`) | `TableSlab.tla` with `TableSlabOps.tla`: `TypeOK`, `Bounded`, `IndexPerm`, `Ascending`, `NoDupKeys`, `AbsOK`, `IterOK`, `QueriesOK`, `FullOK`, `ResultOK`, `RefusedUnchanged` (capacity 3, keys 1..4, values 1..2, a key or value of the wrong length, removals of 0..5, retain with each of the 16 key sets, changing the values or not; under 10,000 states); witnesses `TableSlab_full` (a new key refused `Full`: `NeverFull` fails) and `TableSlab_move` (retain moves a kept record into a hole: `NoRetainMove` fails) | none | keys are fixed-length big-endian byte strings, so their bytewise order is the integer order the model uses; the bytes past `len` are left out of the state and TLC stops on any read of them, so the code reads none; the `break` retain marks unreachable is checked unreachable; the index width (2 or 4 bytes) and the refusals of `new` are `TableBytes.tla`'s; `get_mut` is not modelled; TLC runs capacity 3 | `TraceTableSlab.tla`: 33 seeded runs of real slabs of capacity 0, 1, 3, 8, 64 (2-byte index entries) and 70,000 (4-byte entries) with 2-byte keys and values: inserts of new and held keys until full and past it, keys and values of the wrong length, removals of the first, last, middle and absent keys, `retain` keeping none, all or some, changing the values or not, clears; the result (and for `retain` the number removed and the keys the closure saw, in order), each position's slot (probe `trace_slot`, feature `trace`), key and value, and the queries of probe keys after each call, recomputed by the model operators |

### transport

| Property | Rust item | TLA+ | Lean theorem | Assumptions | Trace validation |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| control frames of a connection: a stream frame in flight is in the table under a fresh key until its packet is acknowledged or lost, and is then handed back once (acknowledged or to be queued again), a second report finding nothing; the table holds at most `TABLE` (24) frames and refuses `track` only when full; a held extension frame owns exactly one pool chunk, freed when its slot is (acknowledged or given up), and is sent again after a loss; a refused hold changes nothing; READY and the ticket batch are flags: a lost READY or tickets frame is sent again, and a batch issued is sent to the peer (the doc of `issue_tickets`), an acknowledgement clearing only the batch it carried | `conn/ctl.rs` (`Ctl::track`, `take`, `hold_ext`, `free_ext`, `apply`, `acked`, `lost`); the glue that sets the flags: `conn/drive.rs` (`issue_tickets`, `send_ready`), `conn/send.rs` (READY, tickets and extension frames put in a packet), `conn/recv.rs` (`tickets_acked`), `conn/api.rs` (an extension given up) | `CtlTable.tla` with `CtlTableOps.tla`: `TypeOK`, `TableKeys`, `Accounted`, `NoTwice`, `ExtChunks`, `ExtResent`, `TicketsOwed`, `ReadyOwed`, `RefusedKeeps`; configs `CtlTable_sc` (2 places, 3 frames, up to 3 sends), `CtlTable_ext` (2 slots, a pool of 2 shared with another user, up to 3 frames, 4 sends), `CtlTable_flags` (2 ticket generations, READY, 4 sends); every reference acknowledged, lost or acknowledged late in any order (states not measured yet, estimated under 20000 each); witnesses `CtlTable_full` (the table fills: `TableNotFull` fails), `CtlTable_extfull` (every slot held: `ExtNotFull` fails); the tickets rule is the code's (fix T1: `CtlTable_flags` checks it) | none | the REKEY flags of the outcome and the stream layer's own handling of a frame handed back are other units'; a loss with `requeue` false (repair packets and probes carry no control frame) is not modelled; the frame bodies and the size checks of putting a frame in a packet are left out (a frame either goes in a packet or waits) | `TraceCtlTable.tla`: about 15300 calls on a real `Ctl`, stream table and pool at the real sizes (24 places, 8 slots): the table filled and refused, every slot held with the pool full, bodies at and over the limit, ticket batches issued, sent, lost and acknowledged (an older one too), READY lost and rebuilt, and 100 seeded runs of 150 calls with every reference reported in seeded orders; the table, the keys, the slots, the flags and the chunks charged after every call; a reported tickets frame's id is the batch it carried (fix T1) |
| frame descriptors of a sent packet: the list holds the frames pushed, in push order (`as_slice`, `iter` and `into_iter` read exactly them; `len` and `is_empty` agree), never more than `MAX_FRAME_REFS` (8); a push to a full list is refused with `FrameRefsFull` and changes nothing, and only a full list refuses. There is no encoding of the list (it lives in memory beside the packet's record), so there is no round trip to check | `recovery/frame_ref.rs` (`FrameRefs::new`, `push`, `as_slice`, `len`, `iter`, `is_empty`, `IntoIterator`) | `FrameRefs.tla` with `FrameRefsOps.tla`: `Ordered`, `Bounded`, `RefusedKeeps` (the real 8 places, two descriptors one of which is the initial fill; states not measured yet, estimated about 1000); witness `FrameRefs_full` (the list fills: `NotFull` fails) | none | descriptors are opaque values to the list | `TraceFrameRefs.tla`: 4 boundary lists (the initial fill pushed, extreme ids, offsets and lengths, every control kind, repeats), each pushed past full, and 200 seeded lists of 1 to 12 pushes; against a naive vector; the slice, both iterators, `len` and `is_empty` after every push |
| lazy table (with `alloc`): the table always reads as `N` entries (`get` below `N`, nothing from `N` on; `iter` gives all `N`), an entry never written reads exactly as the blank, every entry past the written prefix is blank; `get_mut(i)` writes entry `i` and every one before it, `written_mut` and `prefix_mut` only reach entries already written; a call that returns nothing changes nothing; the written prefix never goes past `N`, so the room reserved at the start never grows and no write allocates | `stream/lazy.rs` (`Lazy::new`, `get`, `get_mut`, `written_mut`, `prefix_mut`, `clear`, `iter`, `written`) | `Lazy.tla` with `LazyOps.tla`: `TypeOK`, `Room`, `Reads`, `View`, `Beyond`, `RefusedKeeps` (N = 3, values {1, 2} and the blank 0, indices 0..3, prefix lengths 0..4; states not measured yet, estimated under 200); witness `Lazy_full` (the whole room written: `NotFull` fails) | none | the table without `alloc` (a plain array) is not modelled; `written_iter_mut` is not modelled (it writes only written entries, as `prefix_mut`) | `TraceLazy.tla`: tables of 1, 5 and 64 entries, a boundary sequence each (every refusal, the sweep of `get`, `clear`) and 20 x 60 seeded calls each, against a naive array; the written prefix, the `N` entries `iter` reads and the room (capacity and address) after every call |
| the endpoint's amplification ledger: per address `[rx, tx, validated]`; an address that wrote first is held to `tx <= 3 * rx` until it answers something sent to it, an address dialed (or written to first) is validated from the start; each datagram is charged its size plus 28; a held send changes nothing; the table holds at most 1024 addresses and evicts the one inserted first; counters saturate and never decrease, validation is sticky; `on_receive`, `open` and `allow_send` are exactly the documented steps | `pinc_core::Ledger::{on_receive, open, allow_send}` (`ledger.rs`) | `Ledger.tla` over `LedgerOps.tla` (the `AmpOps.tla` operators instanced with no overhead): `TypeOK`, `Bounded`, `Distinct`, `AmpBound`, `SendExact`, `OpenExact`, `ReceiveExact`, `TableStep`, `Monotone`; `Ledger.cfg` 3 addresses, 2 entries, counters to 3 (at most 6241 states), `Ledger_sat.cfg` 2 addresses, 1 entry, counters saturating at 30 with an overhead of 2 (at most 3845 states); witnesses `Ledger_w_evict` (`NoEvictReady`: a full table evicts), `Ledger_w_held` (`NotHeldAfterSending`: an address is held after a send was charged to it), `Ledger_w_sat` (`NotSaturated`: a counter saturates) | none | the bound is on the stored counters: once `tx` saturates at `u64::MAX` the bytes sent after it are not counted (unreachable: `2^64` bytes); an evicted address written to again counts as one spoken to first (the documented trade) | `TraceLedger.tla` (`u64` words, 1024 entries): about 4000 calls of the real `Ledger`: a table filled past its capacity, held and validated addresses, zero sizes, counters at `u64::MAX`, 16 seeded runs (4 that evict), each entry checked after every call |
| loss estimate: a batch is `acked` decay steps (`v -= v >> 5`) then `lost` growth steps (`v += (65536 - v) >> 5`), one per packet; the value is always in 0..65536; the decay is fixed below 32 and the growth above 65504, so a batch of any size equals a walk of at most a few hundred steps and every count from 65536 up gives the same value; a lost packet never lowers it and an acknowledged one never raises it; tail repair is sent exactly when the connection is at least 2 s old, its queue drained, the path direct and the estimate at least 328 | `recovery/loss_estimate.rs` (`step`, `LossEstimate::new`, `reset`, `get`, `record`, `should_send_repair`) | `LossEstimate.tla` with `LossEstimateOps.tla`: `StepOK`, `RepairOK` over 17 start values (0, the edges of both fixed points, the threshold and its neighbours, 65536) and batch counts {0, 1, 2, 31, 32, 299, 300, 400} squared, every flag and age case of the decision (states not measured yet, estimated about 1360); as ASSUMEs over every value 0..65536: both steps stay in range, the fixed points, both steps monotone, each walk to a fixed point shorter than 400 | none | the compare-and-swap loop of the atomic word under concurrent callers is not modelled (each `record` is taken as one atomic step); the plain-cell word of targets without 32-bit compare-and-swap is the same step | `TraceLossEstimate.tla`: about 19000 calls on real estimates: every pair of 16 boundary counts up to `u32::MAX`, walks to both fixed points, the repair threshold crossed both ways, ages around 2 s and up to `u64::MAX` with every flag, 200 seeded estimates of 50 calls; each value and each repair answer against the model, the test also against a per-packet replay of the doc |
| pool of handshake machine boxes (with `alloc`): a box is in exactly one place (one handshake, one idle cell, or freed); at most `MACHINE_BOXES` (64) idle boxes; a handshake takes an idle box (the first full cell) and allocates only when the pool is empty; a box retired (handshake confirmed or connection gone) goes to the first empty cell and is freed only when the pool is full; a box returned holds no secrets; a handshake that retired holds no machine | `conn/handshake.rs` (`Machines::new`, `take`, `put`, `MACHINE_BOXES`, `boxed`, `Handshake::retire_machine`, `Drop for Handshake`) | `Machines.tla`: `TypeOK`, `OneHolder`, `NoSecretIdle`, `IdleBound`, `DoneHoldsNone`, `AllocOnlyEmpty`, `FreeOnlyFull` (2 cells for the 64, 3 handshakes at once, up to 6 boxes allocated; states not measured yet, estimated under 5000); witness `Machines_full` (a box finds the pool full and is freed: `NoneFreed` fails) | none | without `alloc` the machine stays in the connection and there is no pool; the pool is one context's (`Rc` and `Cell`), so no concurrent use is modelled; the trace drives retirement by drop only (`confirm` needs a full handshake), the model drives both | `TraceMachines.tla`: about 2450 calls: up to 72 real client connections at once over one pool of 64 cells, opened and dropped in order, in seeded orders and in churn near the bound; every cell and the box every connection holds, after every call |
| handshake message codec: for the two Initial forms, both rejects, the Reply, the Finish, the resumption Hello and Reply (with its ticket batch) and the sealed identity plaintext, parsing the encoding gives the message back and the encoding fits a buffer of exactly its size and fails one byte short (`BufferTooSmall` of 1); every message fits `MAX_MESSAGE_LEN` (the outbox buffer) and every plaintext `MAX_ID_SIG_LEN`; every proper prefix is `Truncated` and every trailing byte is refused as trailing; a single changed byte that still parses re-encodes to itself (one accepted encoding per message); each type is refused by every other parser as the wrong type; each malformed class is refused with its own reason (unknown type, connection id over 20 bytes, ALPN length outside 1 to 32, AEAD offer outside 1 to 3, unknown AEAD, sealed length outside 16 to 5384, more than 4 tickets, unknown identity algorithm, key or signature length not the algorithm's); a field outside the documented ranges is written (an ALPN of 0 or 33 to 255 bytes, a sealed field of 15 or 5385 bytes, a key of the wrong length) and refused by the parser, and an ALPN of 256 bytes or a sealed field of 65536 is `VarintOverflow`; `Initial::write` does not refuse an ALPN of 0 or 33 to 255 bytes although its doc says it returns a length error for a field out of range (witness; the machines cannot build one, their ALPN is an `Alpn`) | `message.rs`: `MessageType::of`, `Initial`, `Reject`, `Reply`, `Finish`, `ResumeHello`, `ResumeReply`, `IdSig` (`parse`, `write`); `tickets.rs`: `TicketBatch::read`, `write` | `MessageCodec.tla`: `RoundTrip`, `Fits`, `Strict`, `Unique`, `Cross`, `Classes`, `OutOfRange` (`MessageCodec.cfg`: 190 messages, one state each, at the real sizes: connection ids of 0, 1, 4 and 20 bytes, ALPNs of 1 and 32, every offer and AEAD, sealed fields of 16, 17, 123 and 5384 bytes, 0 to 4 tickets, the four algorithms with and without credit; cuts, trailing bytes and changed bytes at the first 4, the last 3 and around every field boundary); witness `MessageCodec_alpn.cfg` (`WriteRefusesBadAlpn` fails) | none | the sizes are `Padding.tla`'s; the prekey hash and the credit record's fields are not modelled (`CreditRecord` is a bijection on its 6 bytes, `Credit.tla`); a byte inside a fixed field is copied through and is not changed by the TLC check (the trace changes random ones too) | `trace_message` (about 30000 calls of the real codec on the messages of the model, 70 seeded random ones, their cuts, trailing and changed bytes, cross parses, 300 garbage strings through every parser and `MessageType::of` of every byte), `TraceMessageCodec.tla` on all of them |
| which frames go in which kind of packet: a 0-RTT packet is refused whole (and counted) exactly when it carries ACK, CLOSE, REKEY, NEW_TICKETS or PATH_RESPONSE, whatever else it carries and in whatever order; a 1-RTT packet is never refused for its frames; a packet makes the receiver follow the peer's path exactly when it carries STREAM, DATAGRAM or WAKE, and counts as a WAKE exactly when it carries one; the builder seals a 0-RTT packet only as a dialing side in its handshake with early keys, and in every reachable state where it does, nothing its fill may write is a frame the receiver refuses there | `conn/recv.rs` (`scan`, `Props`, `process_payload`, `follow_peer`), `conn/send.rs` (`build_app`, `can_build_app`, `early_client`, `fill_frames`) | `PacketKind.tla`: `EarlyRule`, `ShortTakesAll`, `MoversRule`, `OrderFree`, `SenderAgrees` (`PacketKind.cfg`: every packet of at most 2 frames over the 19 kinds, as 0-RTT and as 1-RTT, and the 512 states of the builder under the facts of reachable states; one state, the invariants quantify over the cases); witness `PacketKind_facts` (without the facts, `SenderAgrees` fails) | none | the facts of reachable states are read off the code (a dialing side has an acknowledgement or a response pending only after a 1-RTT packet opened; the 1-RTT send and receive keys are installed together; only a responder sends tickets; a key roll is sent only with 1-RTT keys; only a dialing side holds early send keys) and checked on real sessions by the trace, not proven | `TracePacketKind.tla`: tests/trace_packet_kind.rs, 29 real 0-RTT packets of resumed dialing sides with crafted frames in front, 54 real 1-RTT packets, 72 packets on a direct path while the active one is relayed (moved or not), and every packet 12 real resumed sessions sealed (kind and frames) |
| held-address flag of a connection's paths: `Paths::held` always equals the number of tracked paths whose flag is set (after a flag changes, and after a slot is emptied or taken by a new path, which starts with the flag clear), so `has_held_paths` is one read; `set_path_held` on a tracked path sets that path's flag and changes nothing else; on a path the connection does not track it changes nothing | `conn/path_held.rs` (`Paths::recount`, `Connection::set_path_held`, `has_held_paths`); the recounts of `conn/path.rs` (`new_path_slot`, `remove_path`, `forget_gone`) | `PathHeld.tla`: `TypeOK`, `OneSlotPerId`, `HeldCount`, `HasHeld`, `SetExact` (2 slots, ids {1, 2, 3}, every slot may be emptied or taken, which includes the path.rs rules; states not measured yet, estimated under 500); witness `PathHeld_drop` (a held path leaves its slot: `NoHeldGone` fails) | none | which slot `conn/path.rs` may take or empty (never the active path) is that unit's; the model lets every slot be taken or emptied; that the flag stops only the size probe is read in `conn/send.rs` and not modelled | `TracePathHeld.tla`: 30 runs of a real client (4 path slots), half of them connected: a boundary sequence (repeats, untracked ids, a full table, a held path removed and replaced, the active path refused, an id of 2^40) and 60 seeded calls each; the count, `has_held_paths` and every slot after every call |
| path validation: a path is validated only by an answer, on that path, carrying one of the challenges it keeps (the outstanding one and up to 3 it replaced, the oldest overwritten), by a relayed path's tracking, by `path_returned` or by the host's info; an answer carrying anything else (a forged one, one to another path's challenge, an overwritten one) changes nothing but the time the path was heard; the table of a path keeps exactly its last 4 challenges since the last answer and survives a rebind; an answer is checked against the table of the slot it arrived on | `conn/path.rs` (`Path::push_challenge`, `is_challenge`, `set_challenge`, `queue_challenge`, `on_path_response`, `rebind_path`, `add_path`, `path_returned`), `conn/recv.rs` (`process_payload`, `on_frame`) | `PathMove.tla` with `PathMoveOps.tla` and `PathOps.tla`: `OutstandingKept`, `ReplacedKept`, `AnswerOnPath`, `ValidCause`, `TypeOK`, `Unique`, `ActiveLive` (`PathMove_valid.cfg`: 2 places, ids 1 to 3, direct paths, 2 challenges drawn, answers carrying any challenge or a forged one on any path, before and after a rebind, a challenge lost or never answered; `PathMove_ring.cfg`: 1 place, 5 challenges drawn, across the wrap of the table); witnesses `PathMove_replaced` (an answer to a replaced challenge validates: `NoReplacedAnswer` fails) and `PathMove_wrap` (more than 4 challenges on one path: `NoWrap` fails) | none | the random draw never repeats a challenge (ideal primitive); time is abstract: what the code reads of the clock is an input of each step | `TracePathMove.tla`: tests/trace_path_move.rs, 19 fixed runs and 40 seeded random runs of a real dialing side, every host call, injected packet, built datagram and timer checked against the model's step, the path table before and after |
| migration: the active path moves only by these rules: the host's `set_active` (a validated path), the peer's highest-numbered data packet on another path whose local address is not gone (not from a direct path onto a relayed one without WAKE), `choose_path` (to the best usable path at once from a relayed, gone or missing active path, else for a round trip lower by more than 5 ms), the fall back to the relayed path of the highest place when the active direct path idles out (15 s) or after three probe timeouts in a row on a validated active direct path; a path left by a choice is not gone unless it is the active one; the path left takes the smoothed round trip | `conn/path.rs` (`choose_path`, `best_path`, `switch_active`, `follow_active`, `relay_fallback`, `fail_over`, `idle_out`, `on_path_timers`, `fire_probe`, `set_active`, `set_path_info`, `path_lost`, `punch_known`), `conn/recv.rs` (`follow_peer`), `conn/timer.rs` (`run_recovery_timer`) | `PathMove.tla`: `ChooseValid`, `FollowRule`, `FallbackRule`, `LostRule`, `ValidCause`, `AnswerOnPath`, `OutstandingKept`, `TypeOK`, `Unique`, `ActiveLive` (`PathMove.cfg`: 2 places, ids 1 and 2, direct and relayed, 1 challenge drawn, the host's add, path_lost, set_active and set_path_info, data on any path, silence, the timers and the third probe timeout and later ones, the draw failing; `PathMove_answer.cfg`: the same places with probes, punches, challenges sent or lost, answers and forged answers on any path, challenges from the peer, path_returned and the fail over); witness `PathMove_fallback` (the fall back after three probe timeouts happens: `NoFallback` fails); findings, witnesses of the documented rule failing: `PathMove_follow` (`FollowsPeer`), `PathMove_relay` (`RelayValid`), `PathMove_stuck` (`DocFallback`), `PathMove_lost` (`GoneLeft`), `PathMove_gone` (`GoneNeverBack`), `PathMove_returned` (`DocValid`), `PathMove_gain` (`DocGain`: 2 places, IPv6 or not, round trips 0 and 6 ms, 3 challenges); `PathMove_follow_gone` (`FollowsPeer`, `GoneNeverBack` with a gone path held beside the active one: 2 places, ids 1 and 2, direct paths, add, path_lost and data, 1 challenge drawn), witness `PathMove_w_gonedata` (data with the highest number arrives on a held gone path: `NoGoneData` fails) | none | as above; the cadence of probes (gaps and counts), the amplification budget (`Amp.tla`) and the datagram size (`Mtu.tla`) are not modelled: a probe is only "one is left"; the clock near `u64::MAX` is not modelled | `TracePathMove.tla` (as above): idle boundary at 15 s exactly and 1 ns past it, thin and non-thin sessions, probe timeouts 3 to 9, re-joins held by their floor, a full table of 4 places with ids 1 to 7 |
| recovery integer helpers: `icbrt(n)` is the largest r with r^3 <= n and never overflows (each candidate's cube is computed with `checked_pow`, and the top candidates' cubes do overflow); `sat(v)` is v when it fits u64 and `u64::MAX` otherwise | `recovery/math.rs` (`icbrt`, `sat`) | `RecoveryMath.tla` with `RecoveryMathOps.tla`: `IcbrtOK`, `SatOK`, scaled (a 28-bit word for the u128, roots of 10 bits for the 43, which keeps the code's relation 3 x 43 > 128 > 3 x 42), about 4000 inputs (every n below 2000, k^3 and its neighbours, 2^j and its neighbours, the largest word); states not measured yet; witness `RecoveryMath_ovf` (a candidate's cube overflows the word: `NoOverflow` fails) | none | TLC integers are 32 bits, so the model runs scaled widths and the trace replays `icbrt` on n below 2^30; above that the Rust test checks the definition with u128 arithmetic; `SatOK` on the scaled widths restates the operator, the real `sat` is checked by the trace | `TraceRecoveryMath.tla`: about 7200 `icbrt` calls on n below 2^30 (every n below 2000, k^3 and its neighbours for k to 1023, 2^j and its neighbours, 2000 seeded) checked against the definition and the model's loop; about 1011 `sat` calls on u128 values as limbs (the u64 edges, 2^64, 2^127, `u128::MAX`, 1000 seeded); the Rust test also checks `icbrt` on values up to `u128::MAX` |
| stream send half, send credit: credit is spent once per byte and within the peer's window (at the write for a buffered stream, at the first transmission for a source-backed one), no frame ends past the limit, a retransmission spends no credit, a reset carries a final size the peer accepts, and the limit only grows | `Send::write`, `next_frame`, `raise_limit`, `reset`, `open_source` | `StreamSend.tla`: `CreditSpent`, `SentInWindow`, `FramesInWindow`, `RetxFree`, `ResetInWindow`, `OffsetsInRange`; `StreamSend.cfg` (buffered: chunks of 2 bytes, a ring of 1 over a pool of 1, a window of 0 raised to 2 or 4, writes of 1 or 3, frames of 0 to 2, offsets to 4) and `StreamSend_src.cfg` (source-backed: a window of 0 or 2 raised to 4, sources of 3 or 4 bytes whole or one byte short, and 5 refused past MAXOFF = 4); witness `StreamSend_block_w.cfg` (`NeverAtWindow`: a source-backed stream with bytes waiting and no credit) | none (the TLA+ model and the trace carry it; the `u64` edges are asserted by the test) | the limits the peer gives are at most `MAX_OFFSET` (a varint): `write` does not test `MAX_OFFSET` itself | `TraceStreamSend.tla` over `tests/trace_stream_send.rs` (`stream_send.ndjson`): every call of real send halves (ring of 3, pool of 4, 16 and 64 ranges) replayed as the model's step with its result and the whole state after it |
| stream send half, no released chunk is copied: a retransmitted frame starts at the acknowledged prefix and comes from the lowest lost range; a copy of any frame below `sent_max` (fresh, stale from before an acknowledgement, or cut by `clip`) reads only slots that hold the chunk its offsets name and never a released chunk, so a stale frame whose slot now holds a later chunk is refused (`DataUnavailable`) and the rest of it queued again; a write never writes into another chunk's slot and never fails on the pool once bounded by it | `Send::next_frame`, `copy_out`, `requeue`, `on_acked`, `write`, `Ring` | `StreamSend.tla`: `RetxAfterPrefix`, `LowestFirst`, `CopyNeverReleased`, `WriteSafe`, `HeldFromPrefix`, `BytesHeld`, `Pool`; `StreamSend.cfg`, `StreamSend_src.cfg` (as above; one lost and one acknowledged range, so every second disjoint range overflows); witnesses `StreamSend_wrap_w.cfg` (`NoStaleReuse`: the ring wraps onto a released chunk's slot) and `StreamSend_pool_w.cfg` (`PoolNeverBinds`: other users hold the pool and a write takes nothing) | none | none beyond the row above | `TraceStreamSend.tla`: `ReadsOwnOf` on every recorded copy, no aliasing on every recorded write, every retransmitted frame at or after the prefix; the test also compares every copied byte with the byte written at its offset |
| stream send half, one final size: once set it never changes, nothing is sent past it, and every frame with the end marker and every reset frame carry the first final size the peer was told, through a reset or a stop request from the peer in every phase | `Send::finish`, `open_source`, `reset`, `next_frame`, `take_control`, `control_lost` | `StreamSend.tla`: `FinalFixed` (a transition property), `EndWithinFinal`, `OneFinalSize`; `StreamSend.cfg`, `StreamSend_src.cfg` (as above); witness `StreamSend_final_w.cfg` (`NeverResetAfterFin`: a reset after the end marker was sent) | none | a stop request from the peer resets the half with its code (`Streams::on_stop_sending`, the row of STOP_SENDING) | `TraceStreamSend.tla`: every frame with the end marker and every taken reset frame checked against the first final size told; the test checks one final size per run |
| stream send half, the module's invariants: lost ranges lie below `sent_max`, `acked_prefix` is the end of the acknowledged prefix, chunks are held only from the one containing it, the bytes written and not acknowledged stay held, and the half's chunks plus other users' stay within the pool | `Send` (module invariants of `stream/send.rs`) | `StreamSend.tla`: `TypeOK`, `LostBelowSent`, `ApIsPrefix`, `HeldFromPrefix`, `BytesHeld`, `Pool`; `StreamSend.cfg`, `StreamSend_src.cfg` | none | none | `TraceStreamSend.tla`: `Inv` on every replayed state |

### wire

| Property | Rust item | TLA+ | Lean theorem | Assumptions | Trace validation |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| map keys: `check_key_order` accepts exactly length-then-bytewise increasing keys, refuses an equal key as a duplicate and a smaller one as out of order | `cbor::check_key_order` (through `MapReader::next_key`, `skip_value`, `MapKeys::key`) | `WireCbor.tla`: `Inv` (`KeysOK`), every pair of keys of 0 to 3 bytes over {0, 97, 255} | none | none | `TraceWireCbor.tla` (`tla/wire_cbor.ndjson`): map runs of the token sequences and of the random trees |
| the decoder accepts only the canonical subset: `next_item` decides as the head rules stated apart from the code (minimal argument, no reserved or indefinite form, valid UTF-8, finite 64-bit floats, only tag 42 over a byte string starting with 0x00), with the exact `Truncated` count, never past the input | `cbor::Decoder::next_item` | `WireCbor.tla` (`WireCbor.cfg`): `Inv` (`DecOK`), every first byte 0..255 followed by each of 37 tails, and the item after it; about 10,500 states with the families below | none | 64-bit `usize`; `str::from_utf8` is the Unicode well-formed UTF-8 (Table 3-7); `f64::is_finite` is "exponent not all ones" | `TraceWireCbor.tla`: the 9472 boundary inputs, 400 random encodings and their mutations |
| one accepted encoding per value: an accepted item re-encodes with the encoder to exactly the bytes it was read from (decode then encode is the identity) | `Decoder::next_item`, `Encoder` | `WireCbor.tla`: `Inv` (`DecOK`, `Canon`); same domain | none | as above | `TraceWireCbor.tla` |
| encode then decode: every encoder call writes a canonical item that decodes back to the same item followed by any rest; `tag_cid` decodes to tag 42 and the 0x00-prefixed bytes | `cbor::Encoder` (`unsigned`, `negative`, `bytes`, `text`, `array`, `map`, `tag_cid`, `bool`, `null`, `float`) | `WireCbor.tla`: `Inv` (`EncOK`: `InSubset`, `RoundTrip`), 80 calls (12 boundary arguments per header kind, strings of 0 to 256 bytes, CIDs of 0 to 255 bytes, 9 floats), 4 rests | none | `f64::from_bits` and `to_bits` round trip a finite value | `TraceWireCbor.tla`: each boundary call at 5 buffer sizes, 300 seeded random runs |
| bounded and atomic encoder: a call succeeds exactly when the room holds its encoding, else `BufferTooSmall` with the shortfall and nothing written; a NaN or infinity is refused whatever the room | `cbor::Encoder` | `WireCbor.tla`: `Inv` (`EncOK`), each call into buffers of 0, 1, L - 1, L, L + 1 bytes | none | as above | `TraceWireCbor.tla`: the whole buffer after each run, unwritten cells still the fill byte |
| ordered maps: `MapKeys::key` accepts a key exactly when one is still declared, it follows the previous key and it fits, writes nothing otherwise, and a completed map is accepted whole by `skip_value` and by `MapReader` with the same keys | `cbor::Encoder::ordered_map`, `cbor::MapKeys::key` | `WireCbor.tla`: `Inv` (`MkOK`), maps of 0 to 2 entries into 7 bytes, keys from {"", "a", "b", "aa"} | none | none | `TraceWireCbor.tla`: 1364 ordered-map runs |
| `skip_value` consumes exactly one well-formed value (keys text and in order, tag contents checked), with the exact `Truncated` count, and refuses nesting past the bound; a `MapReader` walk decides as `skip_value` of the whole map | `cbor::Decoder::skip_value`, `cbor::MapReader` | `WireCbor.tla` (`WireCbor_skip.cfg`, MaxDepth 2): `Inv` (`SkipAgrees`, `MapAgrees`), every sequence of up to 5 tokens grown while `skip_value` answers `Truncated`; about 3,000 states; witness `WireCbor_w_depth.cfg` (`NoDepth`: the depth bound is reached). Depth counted as documented (arrays and maps): violated today on a tag at the bound, see the finding | none | 64-bit `usize` | `TraceWireCbor.tla` with `MAX_DEPTH = 64`: token sequences, nesting of 62 to 66, random trees |
| a read never goes past the input: `pos + remaining` is the input length and the rest is the input after `pos` | `Reader` (`peek_u8`, `read_bytes`, `read_u8`, `read_u16`, `read_u32`, `read_u64`, `read_varint`) | `WireCursor.tla`: `Inv` (`RCursor`), one call from every position of every input of 0 to 3 bytes over {0, 1, 128, 255} and eight inputs of 8 to 10 bytes, 15 calls (`read_bytes` of 0..4, 8, 9, 10, `usize::MAX`); 5880 cases | none | 64-bit `usize`; the varint decoder is `Varint.tla`'s | `TraceWireCursor.tla` (`tla/wire_cursor.ndjson`): every boundary case and 300 seeded random runs of up to 12 calls |
| short input is an error: a read succeeds exactly when the input left holds what it asks, else `Truncated` with the exact shortfall (1 for `peek_u8` and a cut varint) or the varint's error, and consumes nothing | `Reader` | `WireCursor.tla`: `Inv` (`RShort`, `RRefused`); same domain; witness `WireCursor_w_rshort.cfg` (`NoShortWithBytesLeft`: a read refused with bytes left is reached) | none | as above | `TraceWireCursor.tla` |
| the position advances by exactly the bytes taken, and the result is those bytes, their big-endian value or the varint they encode | `Reader` | `WireCursor.tla`: `Inv` (`RAdvance`); same domain | none | as above | `TraceWireCursor.tla` |
| a write never goes past the buffer: its length is kept and no cell after `pos` is written | `Writer` (`put_slice`, `put_u8`, `put_u16`, `put_u32`, `put_u64`, `put_varint`, `ensure`) | `WireCursor.tla`: `Inv` (`WBound`), one call from every position of every buffer of 0 to 10 bytes, 23 calls; 1518 cases | none | 64-bit `usize` | `TraceWireCursor.tla`: the whole buffer after each run, unwritten cells still the fill byte; 300 seeded random runs |
| a write that does not fit fails with `BufferTooSmall` (the exact shortfall) and leaves the content and position unchanged; `put_varint` from 2^63 is `VarintOverflow`; `ensure` answers the same question and changes nothing | `Writer` | `WireCursor.tla`: `Inv` (`WFits`, `WRefused`); same domain; witness `WireCursor_w_wpartial.cfg` (`NoPartialRefusal`: a write refused with room left is reached) | none | as above | `TraceWireCursor.tla` |
| on success the position advances by exactly the call's bytes: the bytes given, the big-endian form or the canonical varint, and the content before is kept | `Writer` | `WireCursor.tla`: `Inv` (`WAdvance`); same domain | none | as above | `TraceWireCursor.tla` |

Every theorem named above has an `#audit_axioms` line, and `#audit_package` covers the rest. The handshake theorems are
over a free term algebra for the primitives and a Dolev-Yao attacker that owns the node `A`, drops, reorders,
duplicates, replays and injects every message it can build, and never learns an honest node's long-term or ephemeral
secrets, except that with a broken half of the hybrid (`BX`, `BK` in TLA+, `KnownB` in Lean) it also learns every
X25519 or every ML-KEM shared secret. The Lean attacker is a superset of the TLA+ one, with any number of sessions,
nodes and tickets and any ticket store capacity. The TLA+ model is the bounded cross-check and the bridge to the code: `TraceHandshake.tla` and the Lean
replay run the recorded steps of the real state machines through the same step functions (`HsOps.tla`,
`HandshakeModel.lean`). The handshake models were mutated and TLC or the trace check fails on each: a Resumed amplification factor of 0, a changed
resend budget, the full prekey every second send instead of the third, the fallback on the second send, a client store
without per-peer replacement, a server victim that skips expired slots, a skipped binder check, a late context that is
not cleared by `shed`, a client `take` that ignores the ALPN, an export that returns the oldest tickets; and a changed
field of a recorded line (a message length, an event, a ticket id, a connection id secret, a flag) fails both TLC and
`lake exe replay`. The factor 3 of the resend rule becoming 4, and dropping the removal of an expired slot on a rejected
Hello, fail trace validation too: the traces record the bound the code computed for a repeated Reply and the ticket
slot table of the responder after every step with its clock. Swapping two absorbs of any transcript fails
`TraceTranscript.tla` and the replay. A TLC config of the key roll with the answer not naming its request, one with the
duplicate dropped by the gate and one with the orphaned staged key are kept as witnesses that the three findings are real.

TLC bounds of the handshake (`Handshake.cfg` and ten more; each is a separate model; the script runs up to three models at a time):
- `Handshake`: one initiator at node 1, one responder at node 2, the attacker node, the short Initial allowed, every
  forged Initial, Hello and Reply the attacker can build, and prekey rejects delivered within or after the reject
  gap: 26664 distinct states (a Reply that fails a check before it authenticated is dropped, so no forged Reply
  ends the initiator in a state of its own).
- `Handshake_resume`: a seeded ticket, one initiator, two responder sessions of one node (the replay): 68180 states.
- `Handshake_late`: the loss timers fire (the third-send fallback, late resume replies, repeated Hellos and Initials
  answered by a resumed responder, `shed`): 457624 states (7 min 51 s on the build host).
- `Handshake_stores`: two initiator and two responder sessions, ticket ids drawn from a pool of two so they can collide,
  the client store accepting tickets and exporting, restoring and restarting: 44901 states (9 min).
- `Handshake_xbroken`, `Handshake_kbroken`: `Handshake` with X25519 or ML-KEM broken, every property: 38696 and 43720
  states. The extra power is used: an initiator takes in Replies that pair an honest responder's public value or
  ciphertext with the attacker's own half, and a responder that answered a man in the middle has a handshake key the
  attacker knows (it can seal a Finish, never one an honest initiator signed). `Handshake_bothbroken` (both) must
  violate `Secrecy`; with the chain key made to need only one of its two inputs, both one-broken configs fail.
- `Handshake_stores_xbroken`: `Handshake_stores` with X25519 broken, every property and `ResumedAsPsk`, with
  resumed sessions on tickets of a modelled full handshake: 44901 states (10 min). (The ML-KEM-broken sibling is not in the
  gate for its run time; it adds the least, since resumption runs no ML-KEM.)
- `Handshake_pskleak`: `Handshake_resume` with the seeded tickets' secrets leaked, `ResumedSecret` and `ResumedAsPsk`:
  83168 states; `Handshake_pskleak_xbroken` (X25519 broken as well) must violate `ResumedSecret`.
The forged messages of `Handshake_resume`, `_late`, `_stores` and the configs built on them are only those the property
needs (a bad binder, copied connection ids, a forged late reply); the full attacker is in `Handshake` and the two
configs built on it, and the Lean attacker, which sends any message it can build, is a superset of all of them.

The concerns the layer answers, each by a theorem in both languages:

| Concern | TLA+ | Lean |
|---|---|---|
| reflection and amplification by spoofed sources | `Amp.tla` `AmplificationBound`, `CanSendExact`; `Padding.tla` `BudgetCoversReply`, `Short32`, `ReplySendable` | `Amp.amplification_bound`, `canSend_iff`; `Padding.budget_covers_reply`, `short_ed25519`, `reply_sendable` |
| spoofed Initials cannot change a responder's state: a reject changes nothing, the cache and stores take only authenticated sessions, the short Initial is 464 bytes | `Handshake.tla` `RejectsOnlyFallback`, `CacheAuthenticated`, `TicketsIssued`; `Padding.tla` `Short32` | `HandshakeRej.rejectRespPrej`, `rejectRespRrej`, `HandshakeStore.cacheAuthenticated`, `ticketsIssued`; `Padding.short_ed25519` |
| peers with different limits: the record is authenticated, never over-granted, and a resumption works only against the remembered record | `Credit.tla` `NewSpec`, `EncodeInjective`; `Transcript.tla` `InitialRecovered`; `Handshake.tla` `Agreement` | `Credit.never_exceeds`, `encode_injective`; `Schedule.ticket_psk_binds_credit`; `HandshakeResume.agreement_credit` |
| long-lived keys and AEAD limits: the roll mixes a fresh Diffie-Hellman value, the limits fire, no key is reused | `KeyRoll.tla` `KeysAgree`, `NoKeyReuse`; `Record.tla` `LimitFires`, `NoNonceReuse` | `KeyRoll.post_compromise`; `KeyRollSysThm` `keysAgree`, `noKeyReuse`; `Record.seal_aes_limit`, `openFail_chacha_limit`, `no_nonce_reuse` |
| packet-number privacy: header protection changes only the low bits and is its own inverse | `HeaderProt.tla` `Involution`, `OnlyLowBits` | `Record.apply_involutive`, `unprotect_protect` |
| version and capability negotiation: the version is bound in the transcript, a wrong version or ALPN is refused, the AEAD choice is bound | `Transcript.tla` `InitialRecovered`, `HelloRecovered`; `Handshake.tla` `Agreement` (ALPN, AEAD) | `Transcript.initialFields_injective`, `helloFields_injective`; `HandshakeResume.agreement_credit` |
| replay of 0-RTT data: a ticket is single use, a wrong binder does not burn it, a restored used ticket is rejected | `Handshake.tla` `ZeroRttOnce`, `NoEarly` | `HandshakeEarly.zeroRttOnce`, `acceptedGone`, `earlyTied`; `HandshakeResume.restoredTicketSingleUse` |
| spoofed Initials evicting cached prekeys: a prekey enters the cache only after its initiator authenticated, into a true LRU | `Handshake.tla` `CacheAuthenticated`; `PrekeyCache.tla` `EvictsLRU`, `Refines` | `HandshakeStore.cacheAuthenticated`; `PrekeyCache.insert_evicts_lru` |
| a stolen ticket staying usable: tickets expire, an expired ticket is neither taken, exported nor accepted, and eviction takes expired tickets first | `Tickets.tla` `IssueOrder`, `TakeOnce`, `RestoreFilters`; `Handshake.tla` `Expire` | `Tickets.victim_expired`, `take_expired_dropped`, `export_mem`; `HandshakeStore.acceptedGone` |

Not proved here: the transport's cap on half-open sessions and their lifetime, and the real packet-level behaviour of
the connection's key flip (its phase bit, selected key and hold deadline are not visible through the public API); the
flip and hold layer is covered by the model of it, not by a trace of the code.

The specifications of the machine-integer code were mutated the same way: a deliberate wrong change to the
operator that mirrors the code (a continuation bit on the wrong byte, a wrong length formula, a non-minimal test
shifted by one, a limit of `2^64`, a pair size of 63 bytes, a saturating multiply made plain, the depth without the
`- 1`, the group count without the remainder test, a wrong preorder step, a wrong group range, a wrong right-child
index, a push condition, the output bound, an unchecked fold, a narrowing to 8 bits, the merge of the builder stack,
the hasher offset, `div_ceil` made a floor, the `finish` size, the bytes taken per iteration, the verifier's copy
length, the fast-path test, the order of the pushed children, a wrong `usize` width, the binary search probe) makes
TLC fail on the invariant that states it, and a changed field in a recorded trace (a byte, a count, an offset, a slot,
an answer, a `consumed`, a `fast`) makes the trace check fail. One change that looked like a mutation did not fail:
the hasher offset `consumed / group * group` equals `consumed` whenever `in_group` is 0, which is the only time it is
computed, so replacing it by `consumed` changes nothing; replacing it by 0 fails.
